## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. "Nose"—i.e., police informer. ↩︎
2. Nine months. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. "Nose"——即警方线人。 ↩︎
2. 九个月。 ↩︎

## I / 第一章

===Original===
Lady Raytham drew aside the long velvet curtains and looked down into Berkeley Square. It was half-past four o’clock on a cheerless February evening. Rain and sleet were falling, and a thin yellow mist added to the gloom of the dying day. An interminable string of cars and taxicabs was turning towards Berkeley Street, their shining black roofs reflecting the glare of the overhead lights that had just then hissed and spluttered to life.

She looked blankly towards the desolation of the gardens, a place of bare-limbed trees and shaking shrubs—stared, as though she expected to see some fog wraith take a definite and menacing shape, and give tangible form to the shadows that menaced reason and life.

She was a woman of twenty-eight, straight and slim. Hers was the type of classical beauty which would defy the markings of age for the greater part of a lifetime. A fascinating face—calm, austere, her eyes a cold English grey. You might imagine her the patrician abbess of some great conventual establishment, or a lady of broad manors defending inexorably the stark castle of her lord against the enemy who came in his absence. Analyze her face, feature by feature, put one with the other, and judge her by the standards which profess to measure such things, and brow and chin said “purpose” with unmistakable emphasis.

===Chinese===
雷瑟姆夫人（Lady Raytham）撩开长长的天鹅绒窗帘，俯瞰伯克利广场。那是二月一个阴冷傍晚的四点半。雨夹着雪纷纷落下，一层淡黄色的薄雾给这垂死的白昼更添几分阴郁。一长串汽车和出租车正转向伯克利街，锃亮的黑色车顶映着头顶路灯的光芒——那些灯刚嘶嘶作响地亮起来。

她茫然地望着花园里一片荒芜的景象，光秃秃的树枝和瑟瑟发抖的灌木丛——她凝视着，仿佛期待看到某个雾气凝成的幽灵显出确定而险恶的形体，为那些威胁着理性和生命的阴影赋予有形的轮廓。

她是个二十八岁的女子，身材挺拔苗条。她属于那种古典美的类型，大半生都不会显出衰老的痕迹。一张迷人的脸——沉静、端庄，一双冷淡的灰眼睛，是典型的英国灰色。你可以想象她是某座大型修道院里贵族出身的女修院长，或者是一位坐拥广袤庄园的贵妇，在丈夫不在时坚定不移地守卫着他那冷峻的城堡，抵御来犯之敌。逐一分析她的五官，再综合起来评判，用那些自诩能衡量此类事物的标准来看，她的额头和下巴都清楚地传达着"意志"二字。

===Original===
She was not in her purposeful mood now; rather was she uncertain and irritable, the nearest emotions to fear she knew.

She let the curtains fall back until they overlapped, and walked across to the fireplace, glancing at the tiny clock. The saloon was half lit; the wall sconces were dark, but the big lamp on the table near the settee glowed redly. This room bore evidence of money well but lavishly spent. The greater part of its furnishings would one day reach the museums of millionaire collectors; three pictures that hung upon the apple-green walls were earmarked for the National Gallery.

As she stood looking down into the fire there was a gentle tap at the door, and the butler came in. He was a tall man, rather portly in his way—a man of double chins and an unlined face. He carried a small salver in his hand, a buff oblong in the centre.

===Chinese===
而此刻的她并无那种果决的心绪；相反，她显得迟疑而烦躁——这是她所知的最接近恐惧的情绪。

她让窗帘合拢重叠，然后走到壁炉前，瞥了一眼那只小钟。客厅只亮了一半的灯；墙上的壁灯熄了，但沙发旁桌上那盏大灯发出红彤彤的光芒。这间屋子处处透着金钱被妥善却奢靡地挥霍的痕迹。它的大部分家具日后终将进入百万富翁收藏家的博物馆；挂在苹果绿墙上的三幅画已被指定捐给国家画廊。

她正站在那里低头凝视炉火，门上传来轻柔的叩击声，管家走了进来。他是个高个子男人，体态颇有些发福——双下巴，脸上没有皱纹。他手里端着一个小银盘，盘中央放着一个淡黄色的信封。

===Original===
Lady Raytham tore open the envelope. It was dated from Constantinople and was from Raytham. She had been expecting the telegram all that afternoon. Raytham, of course, had changed his plans. In that sentence was epitomized his life and career. He was going on to Basra and thence to Bushire, to see the Interstate Oil Wells or the sites of them. He was expensively apologetic for two closely written sheets. If he could not return before April, would she go on to Cannes as she had arranged? He was “awfully sorry”; he must have said that at least four times.

She read it again, folded the pink sheets, and laid them on the table.

The butler was waiting, head slightly bent forward as though to catch her slightest whisper. She did not look at him.

“Thank you.”

“Thank you, m’lady.”

He was opening the door when she spoke.

“Druze, I am expecting the Princess Bellini and possibly Mrs. Gurden. I will have tea when they come.”

“Very good, m’lady.”

===Chinese===
雷瑟姆夫人撕开信封。信是从君士坦丁堡发来的，发信人是雷瑟姆。她整个下午都在等这封电报。当然，雷瑟姆（Raytham）又改了计划。他的人生和事业就浓缩在这一句话里了。他要继续前往巴士拉（Basra），然后去布什尔（Bushire），查看州际油田或其遗址。这两页写得密密麻麻的信纸充满了昂贵的歉意。如果他在四月前回不来，她能否按原计划去戛纳？他"非常抱歉"——这句话他至少说了四遍。

她又读了一遍，把粉色的信纸折好，放在桌上。

管家在等候，头微微前倾，仿佛要捕捉她最轻微的低语。她没有看他。

"谢谢你。"

"谢谢您，夫人。"

他正要开门，她开口了。

"德鲁兹（Druze），我在等贝里尼公主（Princess Bellini），可能还有格登夫人（Mrs. Gurden）。她们来了就上茶。"

"好的，夫人。"

===Original===
The door closed softly behind; she raised her sombre eyes and looked at the polished wood of it with a curious listening lift of her head, as though she expected to hear something. But the butler was going slowly down the stairs, a quizzical smile in his eyes, his white, plump hands sliding in and over one another. He stopped on the landing to admire the little marble statue of Circe that his lordship had brought from Sicily. It was a habit of his to admire that Circe with the sly eyes and the beckoning finger. And as he looked, his mouth was puckered as though he were whistling.

A sharp rat-tat on the door made him withdraw from his contemplation. He reached the hall as the second footman opened the door.

Two women came in out of the murk; through the open door he had a glimpse of a limousine drawing away.

“Her ladyship is in the drawing-room, your Highness—shall I take your Highness’s coat?”

“You can’t,” said the first and the bigger woman brusquely. “Help Mrs. Gurden out of hers. Why you wear such horrible contraptions I can’t understand.”

Mrs. Gurden smiled largely.

“Darling, I must wear something. Thank you, Druze.”

Druze took the transparent silk coat and handed it to the second footman; the Princess was already stamping up the stairs. She pushed open the door and walked in unannounced, and Lady Raytham, standing by the fire, her head pillowed on her arm, looked up, startled.

===Chinese===
门在身后轻轻关上；她抬起忧郁的眼睛，带着一种奇特的、倾听般的神情抬头望着那扇抛光的木门，仿佛期待听到什么声响。但管家正慢慢走下楼梯，眼中带着揶揄的笑意，白胖的双手互相摩挲着。他在楼梯平台上停下来，欣赏主人从西西里岛带回来的那尊小小的大理石塞壬雕像。他有欣赏那尊长着狡黠眼睛、伸出召唤手指的塞壬的习惯。他看着看着，嘴巴嘬了起来，像是在吹口哨。

门上传来一阵急促的敲击声，将他从沉思中拉回。他走到大厅时，第二个男仆正打开门。

两个女人从暮色中走进来；透过敞开的门，他瞥见一辆豪华轿车正驶离。

"夫人在客厅里，公主殿下——要我帮您拿外套吗？"

"你拿不了，"第一个、也是身材较高的那个女人唐突地说，"帮格登夫人脱外套。你们穿这种可怕的东西，我真是搞不懂。"

格登夫人（Mrs. Gurden）绽开一个大大的笑容。

"亲爱的，我总得穿点什么。谢谢你，德鲁兹。"

德鲁兹接过那件透明的丝绸外套，递给第二个男仆；公主已经跺着脚上了楼。她推开门，不等通报就走了进来。雷瑟姆夫人正站在炉火旁，头枕在手臂上，闻声惊愕地抬起头来。

===Original===
“I’m so awfully sorry. Push the light, Anita; the button is by your hand. Well?”

The Princess Anita Bellini struggled unaided out of her tweed coat and threw it over the back of a chair, jerked off her hat with another movement, and tossed it after the coat.

People who saw Anita Bellini for the first time gazed at her in a little awe; there was a certain ruthless strength in every line, every feature. She was something more than fifty and was just under six feet in height.

The masculinity of the powerful face was emphasized by the grey hair cut close in an Eton crop and the rimless monocle which never left her eye. Between her white teeth she gripped a long amber holder, in which a cigarette was burning.

Her speech was direct, abrupt, almost shocking in its frankness.

“Greta?”

She jerked the end of the cigarette-holder towards the door.

===Chinese===
"我真是抱歉。开灯，安妮塔（Anita）；按钮就在你手边。怎么样？"

安妮塔·贝里尼公主（Princess Anita Bellini）不用人帮忙，自己挣扎着脱下粗花呢外套，往椅背上一扔，又猛地摘下帽子，随手抛在外套上。

第一次见到安妮塔·贝里尼的人，总会带着几分敬畏凝视她；她的每一道轮廓、每一处五官都透着某种无情的刚毅。她年过五十，身高接近六英尺。

那张刚毅面孔上的男性气质，被剪成伊顿式短发的灰白头发和从不离眼的无框单片眼镜衬托得更加突出。她白皙的牙齿咬着一支长长的琥珀烟嘴，烟嘴里的香烟正燃着。

她说话直截了当，唐突生硬，坦率到几乎令人咋舌。

"格蕾塔（Greta）呢？"

她用烟嘴朝门口方向一甩。

===Original===
“Being fussed over by Druze. That woman would ogle a dustman! She’s that age. It is a horrible thing to have been pretty once and to have produced certain reactions. You can never believe that the spirit has evaporated.”

Jane Raytham smiled.

“They say you were an awfully pretty girl, Nita—” she began.

“They lie,” said Princess Anita calmly. “Russells used to retouch my photographs till there was nothing left but the background.”

Greta floated in, hands outstretched, her big, red mouth opened ecstatically.

“Darling!” she breathed, and caught both Jane’s hands in hers. Anita Bellini’s fleshy nose wrinkled in a sneer.

And yet she should have grown accustomed to Mrs. Gurden, for ecstasy was Greta’s normal condition. She had that habit of touching people, holding them by the arms, stooping to look up into their faces with her big, black eyes that sometimes squinted a little.

She had been pretty, but now her face was long and a little haggard, the face of a woman who was so afraid of missing something that she could not spare the time to sleep. Her lips were heavily carmined, her eyes carefully made up as though she were still expecting a call to return to the chorus from which Anita had rescued her.

===Chinese===
"正被德鲁兹围着献殷勤呢。那个女人连个收垃圾的都能看直眼！她就是那个年纪。曾经漂亮过是一件可怕的事，它会产生某些挥之不去的反应。你永远无法相信那份精神头已经消散了。"

简·雷瑟姆（Jane Raytham）微微一笑。

"他们说你以前漂亮得不得了，妮塔（Nita）——"她开口道。

"他们撒谎，"安妮塔公主平静地说，"拉塞尔照相馆以前修我的照片，修到最后只剩背景了。"

格蕾塔飘然走了进来，双臂张开，红红的大嘴惊喜地咧着。

"亲爱的！"她柔声唤道，双手抓住简的双手。安妮塔·贝里尼肉乎乎的鼻子不屑地皱了皱。

然而她本该对格登夫人习以为常了，因为狂喜本就是格蕾塔的常态。她有触碰别人的习惯，抓着别人的胳膊，俯身抬起那双有时会微微斜视的大黑眼睛看对方的脸。

她曾经漂亮过，但现在她的脸变得瘦长，略显憔悴，是一张因害怕错过什么而舍不得花时间睡觉的女人的脸。她的嘴唇涂着厚厚的口红，眼睛精心描画过，仿佛还在等着安妮塔把她从合唱队里救出来后重返舞台的召唤。

===Original===
“My lovely Jane! Exquisite as usual. That dress—don’t tell me! Chenel, isn’t it?”

“Is it?” Jane Raytham scarcely looked down. “No, I think it is a dress I bought in New York last year.”

Greta shook her head speechlessly.

Anita Bellini blew out a smoke ring and tapped off the ash in the fireplace.

“Greta lays it on thick when she lays it at all,” she said, and cast a critical eye over her hostess. “You’re peaky, Jane. Missing your husband?”

“Terribly.”

The irony of tone was not lost on Anita.

“Raytham—what is he doing? The man is ill of money and yet won’t take a day off making it. Where the—oh, here he is.”

Druze wheeled in the tea-wagon.

“Give me a whisky and soda, Druze, or I’ll perish.”

She drank the contents of the goblet at a gulp and handed back the glass.

Anita fixed her monocle more firmly and lit another cigarette. The door closed behind the butler.

“Druze wears well, Jane. Where did you get him?”

===Chinese===
"我可爱的简（Jane）！一如既往地精致。那件裙子——别告诉我！是谢内尔（Chenel）家的吧？"

"是吗？"简·雷瑟姆几乎没低头看，"不，我想是我去年在纽约买的一件裙子。"

格蕾塔无言地摇了摇头。

安妮塔·贝里尼吐出一个烟圈，在壁炉边弹了弹烟灰。

"格蕾塔要么不拍马屁，一拍就拍得震天响，"她说着，用审视的目光打量着女主人，"你脸色不好，简。想丈夫了？"

"想得厉害。"

话里的讽刺意味没有逃过安妮塔的耳朵。

"雷瑟姆——他在干什么？那个男人对钱着了魔，却一天也不肯歇着。他到底——哦，他来了。"

德鲁兹推着茶车进来了。

"给我来杯威士忌加苏打水，德鲁兹，不然我要渴死了。"

她一口喝干杯中的液体，把空杯递回去。

安妮塔把单片眼镜扶正了些，又点了一支烟。管家关上门退了出去。

"德鲁兹保养得真好，简。你从哪儿找来的？"

===Original===
Lady Raytham looked up quickly.

“Does he? I scarcely notice him. He has always been the same so long as I can remember. He was with Lord Everreed before.”

“That goes back a few years; I remember him when he was a young man.”

The Princess had an unhappy habit of smiling with her mouth closed. It was not very pretty.

“It is funny how age comes—thirty to fifty goes like a flash of lightning.”

She changed the subject abruptly and talked about her call of the afternoon.

“I went for bridge and got a string quartet playing every kind of music except one with a tune in it.”

“It was lovely!” breathed Greta, her eyes screwed tight in an agony of admiration.

“It was rotten,” retorted the grey-haired Anita. “And more rotten because my sister-in-law was there. The woman’s narrowness depresses me.”

Lady Raytham’s eyes had returned to the fire.

“Oh!” she said.

“I asked her what she was going to do about Peter—thank heavens she has a little sense there! Peter has been wiped off the slate. Margaret would not even discuss him. The only person who believes in him is Everreed—but Everreed was always a simpleton. He would never have prosecuted, but the bank forced his hand.”

===Chinese===
雷瑟姆夫人迅速抬起头。

"是吗？我几乎没注意到他。在我记忆所及的范围内，他一直就是那个样子。他以前是为埃弗里德勋爵（Lord Everreed）做事的。"

"那可有好些年了；我记得他年轻时候的样子。"

公主有个不讨喜的习惯——抿着嘴笑。那样子实在不太好看。

"人老起来真奇怪——从三十到五十，快得就像一道闪电。"

她突然转换了话题，谈起下午的拜访。

"我是去打桥牌的，结果碰上一个弦乐四重奏，演奏各种各样的音乐，就是没一首带调子的。"

"太好听了！"格蕾塔屏息赞叹，眼睛因极度的仰慕而紧紧眯成一条缝。

"糟透了，"灰头发的安妮塔反驳道，"更糟的是我嫂子也在场。那个女人的狭隘真让我受不了。"

雷瑟姆夫人的目光又回到了炉火上。

"哦！"她说。

"我问她打算怎么处理彼得（Peter）的事——谢天谢地她还有一点理智！彼得已经被从名册上抹去了。玛格丽特（Margaret）连提都不愿提他。唯一还相信他的人是埃弗里德——但埃弗里德一直是个傻瓜。他本来不会起诉的，是银行逼他不得不这样做。"

===Original===
She said this with some satisfaction. She had never liked her nephew, and Peter hated her—hated her gibes at him when he, the son of a wealthy man, had preferred a private secretaryship with that great Parliamentarian, Viscount Everreed, to entering his late father’s bank. She had sat in court with a contemptuous smile on her lips when the haggard boy had been sentenced for forging his employer’s name to a cheque for five thousand pounds.

The woman by the fire stirred her tea absently.

“When does—”

“He come out? About now, I think. Let me see, he had seven years, and they tell me that these people get a remission of sentence for good conduct—three months in every year. Why, the Lord knows. We pay enormous sums to catch ’em, and as soon as they are safe under lock and key we go tinkering with the lock to get them out.”

“Disgraceful!” murmured Greta.

But Jane Raytham did not hear her.

“I wonder what he will do?” she mused. “Life will go pretty hardly for a man like Peter—”

“Rubbish!” Anita snapped the word. “For goodness’ sake don’t get melancholy about Peter! He has been five years in prison, and at Dartmoor—or wherever he is—they teach men to use their hands to do something besides forge cheques. He will probably make an excellent farm hand.”

===Chinese===
她说这话时带着几分得意。她向来不喜欢这个侄子，而彼得也恨她——恨她在他选择给那位伟大的政治家、子爵埃弗里德当私人秘书，而不愿进入他已故父亲的银行时对他冷嘲热讽。当那个憔悴的男孩因在雇主签名的五千英镑支票上伪造签名而被判刑时，她坐在法庭上，嘴角挂着轻蔑的微笑。

炉火旁的女人心不在焉地搅着茶。

"他什么时候——"

"出狱？我想大概就快了。让我想想，他被判了七年，我听说这些人表现好可以获得减刑——每年减三个月。天知道是怎么回事。我们花大把的钱把他们抓起来，等他们被安全地关进牢里，我们又开始捣鼓那把锁，想办法把他们放出来。"

"真不像话！"格蕾塔喃喃道。

但简·雷瑟姆没有听见。

"不知道他会做什么？"她若有所思，"像彼得这样的人，日子会过得很艰难——"

"胡扯！"安妮塔厉声打断，"看在上帝的份上，别为彼得伤感了！他在监狱里待了五年，在达特穆尔（Dartmoor）——或者他待的地方——他们教那些人动手干活，干的可不只是伪造支票。他很可能会成为一个出色的农场帮手。"

===Original===
Lady Raytham shivered.

“Ugh! How awful!”

The Princess smiled.

“Peter Dawlish is just fool. He belongs to the kind of human that is made for other people’s service. If you start worrying about Peter, you’ll shed tears over the partridge that comes to your table. I wonder what he thinks about Druze?”

Lady Raytham looked up.

“Do you think he still hates him?”

Anita pursed her large lips.

“Druze was Everreed’s butler and cashed the cheque; the next day Peter disappears on his holiday—in reality, on his great adventure. He returns and is arrested, swears he knows nothing about the cheque, and accuses poor Druze of forgery—which doesn’t save him from imprisonment.”

Lady Raytham said nothing.

“Naturally Peter feels sore—if he still believes Druze was the villain of the piece. There may be trouble—we needn’t deceive ourselves.”

Her cigarette had gone out. She opened her bag with an impatient tug and searched.

“Matches? Never mind.”

There was a letter in the bag; she tore a strip from the top and, bending, lit the paper at the fire.

“Who is Leslie Maughan?”

She was glancing at the signature which footed the letter.

“Leslie Maughan? I don’t know him. Why?”

Anita crumpled the paper into a ball.

“Leslie Maughan would like to see me on a personal matter”—Anita invented the stilted and supercilious accent which she supposed the writer of the letter might assume. “And Leslie Maughan will be glad to know what hour will be convenient for me to see him. He is an inventor or a borrower of money or he has an expedition to the Cocos Islands that he would like me to finance. To the devil with Leslie Maughan!”

===Chinese===
雷瑟姆夫人打了个寒噤。

"呃！太可怕了！"

公主微微一笑。

"彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish）就是个傻瓜。他天生就是给别人使唤的料。你要是开始为彼得操心，那你看见盘子里的鹧鸪也要掉眼泪了。我想知道他对德鲁兹怎么看？"

雷瑟姆夫人抬起头。

"你觉得他还恨德鲁兹吗？"

安妮塔噘起她丰厚的嘴唇。

"德鲁兹是埃弗里德的管家，兑现了那张支票；第二天彼得就消失了，说是去度假——实际上是去历险。他回来后被逮捕，发誓说对那张支票一无所知，并指控可怜的德鲁兹伪造支票——这也没能让他免于牢狱之灾。"

雷瑟姆夫人没有说话。

"彼得自然心怀怨恨——如果他仍然相信德鲁兹是罪魁祸首的话。可能会有麻烦——我们不必自欺欺人。"

她的香烟灭了。她不耐烦地拽开手提包翻找。

"火柴？算了。"

包里有一封信；她从信封顶端撕下一条，弯下腰，用那张纸在炉火上点燃。

"莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan）是谁？"

她瞥了一眼信尾的签名。

"莱斯利·莫恩？我不认识他。怎么了？"

安妮塔把那张纸揉成一团。

"莱斯利·莫恩希望就一件私事与我会面"——安妮塔模仿她想象中写信人会使用的那种拿腔拿调、傲慢自大的口音说道，"莱斯利·莫恩很想知道什么时间方便与他见面。他是个发明家，或者是个借钱的人，要么就是有个去科科斯群岛（Cocos Islands）的探险计划想让我资助。让莱斯利·莫恩见鬼去吧！"

## II / 第二章

===Original===
Druze had come in noiselessly at the door and stood, hand clasping hand. His face was strangely pale; as he spoke his right cheek twitched spasmodically.
"Yes?"
"Will your ladyship see Miss Leslie Maughan﻿—?"
"Miss!" snorted Anita, as Jane Raytham rose.
"Miss Leslie Maughan, of the Criminal Investigation Department, Scotland Yard?"
Lady Raytham put out her hand and gripped the back of the chair; her face was bloodless, she opened her mouth to speak, but no word came. Greta was staring at the big woman, but Princess Anita Bellini had no eyes but for the pale butler.
"I will see her﻿—in the small drawing-room, Druze. Excuse me."
She swept out of the room and pulled the door behind her until Druze had disappeared round the lower landing. By her right hand was the door of her own room, and she entered swiftly and noiselessly, switching on the lights as she closed the door. She stared into the mirror. Ghastly! That white, drawn face of hers carried confession. Had she been betrayed? Had they fulfilled their threat?
Pulling out a drawer of her dressing-table, she fumbled for and found a little pot of rouge, and with a quick, deft hand brought an unaccustomed bloom to her cheeks.
Another glance at her face in the glass and she went out and sailed down the stairs, a smile on her lips and in her heart despair.

===Chinese===
德鲁兹（Druze）悄无声息地从门口进来，双手交叉紧扣在身前。他的脸色异常苍白；说话时右脸颊不自主地抽搐着。
"什么事？"
"夫人愿意见莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan）小姐吗——？"
"小姐！"安妮塔（Anita）嗤了一声，简·雷瑟姆（Jane Raytham）已站起身来。
"莱斯利·莫恩小姐，刑事调查局，苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的？"
雷瑟姆夫人（Lady Raytham）伸手攥住椅背；她面无血色，张嘴想说话，却发不出一个字。格蕾塔（Greta）瞪着这个高大的女人，但安妮塔·贝里尼（Anita Bellini）公主的目光只盯着苍白的管家。
"我要见她——在小客厅，德鲁兹。失陪了。"
她翩然走出房间，随手带上门，直到德鲁兹消失在楼下楼梯拐角处。她右手边就是自己的房门，她迅速而无声地闪身进去，关灯关门。她盯着镜中的自己。可怖！那张苍白憔悴的脸写满了招供的意味。她被出卖了吗？他们兑现了威胁吗？
她拉开梳妆台的抽屉，摸索着找到一小盒胭脂，用灵巧的手指飞快地给双颊抹上不寻常的红晕。
她又在镜中瞥了一眼自己的脸，然后走出去，昂首步下楼梯，唇边挂着微笑，心中却满是绝望。

---

===Original===
All the lights were lit in the little drawing-room, and her first emotion was one of surprise and relief. She had not known there were women detectives at Scotland Yard, but she could imagine them as hard-faced, sour creatures in ready-made clothes.
The girl who stood by the table looking down at the illustrated newspaper that Druze had supplied looked to be about twenty-two. She wore a straight nutria coat, a big bunch of violets pinned to one revere. As tall as Jane Raytham and as straight; trim, silken ankles, neatly shod; dark. The face under the upturned brim of a little felt hat was more surprising yet. A pair of dark eyes rose to meet Jane Raytham's. The lips, red as Greta's, yet owing nothing to artifice, were finely moulded, a firm, round chin, and the hint of a white throat somewhere behind the protective fur﻿—in some confusion Lady Raytham catalogued the visible qualities of her unexpected caller.
"You are not Miss Maughan?" she asked.

===Chinese===
小客厅灯火通明，她第一个反应是惊讶中带着宽慰。她不知道苏格兰场还有女警探，但她想象中的女警探都是面目冷峻、脾气刻板、穿着成衣的邋遢货色。
站在桌旁低头看画报的女孩看上去大约二十二岁。她穿着一件挺括的水獭皮大衣，翻领上别着一大束紫罗兰。和简·雷瑟姆一样高，身板一样挺直；脚踝纤细匀称，穿鞋利落；肤色深。小毡帽翻起帽檐下的那张脸更令人意外。一双深色眼睛抬起来与简·雷瑟姆四目相对。双唇如格蕾塔一般鲜红，却不施脂粉，轮廓精致，下巴圆润结实，护身皮草背后隐约露出一截白皙的脖颈——雷瑟姆夫人有些慌乱地盘点着这位不速之客的外在品质。
"你不是莫恩小姐？"她问道。

---

===Original===
When Leslie Maughan smiled, she smiled with eyes and lips, and the dimpled hollows that came to her cheek made her seem absurdly young.
"Yes, that is my name, Lady Raytham; I am awfully sorry to bother you, but my chief is rather a martinet."
"You are a detective? I didn't know﻿—"
"That there were women detectives?" laughed the girl. "And you're right! My position is unique. I am assistant to Chief Inspector Coldwell. The Commissioners, who are rather conservative people, do not object to that. But I suppose I really am a detective. I make inquiries."
She stood by the table, one hand on her hip, one playing with the leaves of the picture paper, her unwavering gaze fixed on Jane Raytham.
"I'm making inquiries now, Lady Raytham," she said quietly. "I want to know why you drew twenty thousand pounds from your bank last Monday."

===Chinese===
莱斯利·莫恩微笑时，眼睛和嘴唇都带着笑意，脸颊上泛起的酒窝让她看起来出奇地年轻。
"是的，那就是我的名字，雷瑟姆夫人；实在抱歉打扰您，不过我的上司是个相当严厉的人。"
"你是警探？我不知道——"
"竟然有女警探？"女孩笑了。"您说得对！我的职位很特殊。我是科尔德威尔（Coldwell）总警探的助手。委员会那帮比较保守的先生们不反对这样做。不过我想我确实是个警探。我负责调查。"
她站在桌旁，一只手叉着腰，另一只手翻弄着画报，目光坚定地盯着简·雷瑟姆。
"我正在进行调查，雷瑟姆夫人，"她平静地说。"我想知道您上周一为什么从银行取出了两万英镑。"

---

===Original===
For a second the woman was panic-stricken; so far lost charge that she all but stammered the truth. The will that held her silent, apparently unmoved, was the supreme effort of her life. Then her training came to her rescue.
The control of her voice was perfect.
"Since when have the police had authority to supervise the banking accounts of private citizens?" she asked in cold, measured tones. "That is an extraordinary request. Is it, then, an offence for me to withdraw twenty thousand pounds from my own account? How did you know?"
"One gets to know things, Lady Raytham."
She was cool, unruffled by the indignation, real or simulated.
"Lady Raytham, you think we are being very impertinent and abominable. And it is certain that, if you report this matter to Scotland Yard, I shall be reprimanded. But we expect that."
Jane Raytham had so far recovered towards the normal that she could open her grey eyes in astonishment.
"Then why on earth have you come?" she asked.
She saw Leslie Maughan draw a deep breath, the ghost of a smile trembled at the corner of her mouth and vanished.

===Chinese===
那一秒钟里，这个女人惊恐万分；她几乎失控，险些把真相脱口而出。她竭力保持沉默、装作无动于衷，这已是她此生最大的努力。然后她受过的训练救了场。
她的声音控制完美无缺。
"警察什么时候有了监管私人银行账户的权力？"她冷冷地质问道，声调平稳。"这要求真是离奇。那么，我从自己的账户里取出两万英镑是犯法吗？你怎么知道的？"
"人总能打听到事情，雷瑟姆夫人。"
她沉着冷静，面对对方的愤怒——不论真假——毫不动摇。
"雷瑟姆夫人，您一定觉得我们非常无礼，非常可恶。而且可以肯定的是，如果您把这件事报告给苏格兰场，我会受到训斥。但我们早有预料。"
简·雷瑟姆已恢复到近乎常态，她可以惊讶地睁大灰色的眼睛了。
"那你到底为什么来？"她问。
她看到莱斯利·莫恩深吸了一口气，嘴角掠过一丝若有若无的笑意，转瞬即逝。

---

===Original===
"Twenty thousand pounds is a lot of money," she said softly. There was a note of pleading in her voice, and, suddenly, with a cry she could not suppress, the significance of the visit flashed upon Lady Raytham. They knew. The police knew the destination or purpose of that money.
Her breath came faster; she could only look into those dark eyes in fear and try as best she could to order her thoughts. Dark eyes, violet﻿—not the burnt brown of Greta's, but a violet that was almost black. A detective﻿—this slip of a girl! She was well dressed, too; the femininity in Jane Raytham took stock unconsciously. The gloves were from Renauds﻿—only Renaud cut that quaint, half-gauntlet wrist.
"Won't you tell me? It might save you so much unhappiness. We try to do that at the Yard﻿—save people unhappiness. You'd never dream that, would you? But the police are more like big brothers than ogres﻿—won't you?"
Jane Raytham shook her head; it was a mistake, the only one she made, to attempt speech.
"No, I won't!" she said breathlessly. "There is nothing to tell﻿—your interference is unwarrantable. I shall write﻿—I shall write﻿—"
She swayed, and instantly Leslie Maughan was by her side, and the strength of her grip was the second surprise that Jane Raytham had.
With an effort she wrenched her arm free.
"Now you can go, please. And if I do not report you, it is because I think you have acted in ignorance, over zeal."
She nodded towards the door, and Leslie slowly gathered up her bag and her umbrella.
"If you ever want me, you will find my telephone number on my card."
Lady Raytham still held the crumpled card in her hand. Now she looked at it, and very deliberately walked to the fire and dropped it into the flames.
"Or the telephone book," said Leslie as she went out.
Druze was in the hall, dry-washing his hands with nervous rapidity. He hastened to the street door and opened it.
"Good night, miss," he said huskily, and she looked at him and shivered. And why Leslie Maughan shivered she did not know, but she had at that moment a vivid and terrifying illusion.
It was as though she were looking into the blank eyes of one who was already dead.

===Chinese===
"两万英镑是一大笔钱，"她柔声说道。声音里带着恳求的意味。突然，一声无法抑制的惊叫涌上喉头，此行的含义闪电般击中了雷瑟姆夫人。他们知道。警察知道了那笔钱的去向或用途。
她的呼吸变得急促；她只能恐惧地凝视那双深色的眼睛，竭力理清思绪。深色的眼睛，紫罗兰色——不是格蕾塔那种焦褐色，而是一种近乎黑色的紫罗兰色。警探——这么个瘦弱的姑娘！而且衣着考究；简·雷瑟姆内心深处的女性直觉不自觉地做了评判。那副手套是伦诺（Renaud）家的——只有伦诺裁得出那种别致的半指手套腕口。
"您就不能告诉我吗？这或许能为您省去很多不幸。我们苏格兰场就是做这个的——帮人们免于不幸。您一定想不到吧？但警察更像老大哥，不像食人魔——您说呢？"
简·雷瑟姆摇了摇头；开口说话是个错误，她唯一的错误。
"不，我不会说！"她气喘吁吁地说。"没什么可说的——你的干涉毫无道理。我要写信——我要写信——"
她身子晃了晃，莱斯利·莫恩立刻出现在她身旁，握力之大是简·雷瑟姆的第二个意外。
她费力地挣脱了手臂。
"现在请你离开。我不举报你，是考虑到你出于无知，过度热心。"
她朝门口点了点头，莱斯利慢慢收拾起手袋和雨伞。
"如果您需要我，我的名片上有电话号码。"
雷瑟姆夫人手里还攥着那张皱巴巴的名片。此刻她看了一眼，然后不慌不忙地走到壁炉前，把名片丢进了火焰里。
"或者查电话簿，"莱斯利边说边走了出去。
德鲁兹在门厅里，紧张地反复搓着手。他急忙跑到前门打开。
"晚安，小姐，"他声音嘶哑地说。莱斯利看着他，打了个寒颤。她不知道自己为何打寒颤，但那一刻她眼前浮现出一个生动而可怕的幻象。
仿佛在凝视一个已死之人空洞的眼眶。

## III / 第三章

===Original===
Leslie Maughan came striding briskly along the Thames Embankment. It was a bitterly cold night, and the nutria coat was not proof against the icy northerner which was blowing. The man who walked by her side was head and shoulders taller than she. He had the gait of a soldier, and his umbrella twirled rhythmically to his pace.

“Suicide on the left,” he said pleasantly, as though he were a guide pointing out the sights.

The girl checked her pace and looked back.

“Really? You don’t mean that, Mr. Coldwell?”

Her eyes were fixed upon the dark figure sprawling across the parapment, his arms resting on the granite crown, his chin on his hands. He was a gaunt figure of a man, differing in no respect from the waifs who would gather here from midnight onward and strive to snatch a little sleep between the policeman’s visits.

“It is any odds,” said Mr. Coldwell carefully, “when you see one of these birds watching the river in that way, he is thinking up a new way of settling old accounts. Are you interested—sentimentally?”

She hesitated.

“Yes, a little. I don’t know whether it’s sentiment or just feminine curiosity.”

===Chinese===
莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan）沿着泰晤士河堤快步走着。夜晚寒气逼人，那件海狸鼠皮大衣抵挡不住呼啸而来的刺骨北风。走在他身边的那个男人比她高出整整一个头。他走路带着军人的步伐，雨伞随着他的步调有节奏地转动着。

"左边有个寻短见的，"他愉快地说，就像导游在指点景点。

女孩放慢脚步，回头望去。

"真的？你不是在开玩笑吧，科尔德威尔先生（Mr. Coldwell）？"

她的目光落在那个趴在护墙上的黑影身上，那人双臂搭在花岗岩顶沿上，下巴搁在手上。他是个瘦骨嶙峋的男人，和那些午夜以后聚集到这里、趁警察巡逻间隙勉强打个盹的流浪汉没什么两样。

"十有八九，"科尔德威尔先生谨慎地说，"当你看到这些家伙这样看着河水的时候，他是在想一个新的了结旧账的办法。你感兴趣吗——出于感伤？"

她迟疑了一下。

"是的，有一点。我不知道这是感伤还是纯粹的女性好奇心。"

===Original===
She left his side abruptly and walked back to the man, who may have been watching her out of the corner of his eyes, for he straightened himself up quickly.

“Down and out?” she asked, and heard his soft laugh.

“Down, but not out,” he replied, and it was the voice of an educated man, with just a trace of that drawl, the pleasant stigmata which the Universities give to their children. “Did I arouse your compassion? I’m sorry. If you offer me money I shall be rather embarrassed. You will find plenty of poor beggars on this sidewalk who are more worthy objects of—charity. I use the word in its purest sense.”

She looked at his face. A slight moustache and a ragged fringe of beard did not disguise his youth. Chief Inspector Coldwell, who had come closer, was watching him with professional interest.

“Would you like to know what I was really thinking about?” There was an odd quality of banter in his voice. “I was thinking about murder. There is a gentleman in this town who has made life rather difficult for me, and I had just decided to walk up to him at the earliest opportunity and pop three automatic bullets through his heart when you disturbed the homicidal current of my thoughts.”

Coldwell chuckled.

“I thought I recognized you; you’re Peter Dawlish,” he said, and the shabby figure lifted his hat with mock politeness.

===Chinese===
她突然离开他身边，朝那个男人走去。那人可能用眼角余光注意到了她，因为他迅速直起身来。

"落魄潦倒？"她问道，听到他轻声笑了。

"落魄，但没有潦倒，"他回答，那声音属于一个受过教育的人，带着一丝若有若无的拖腔——那是大学留给子弟们的愉快印记。"我让你产生同情心了吗？抱歉。如果你要给我钱，我会相当尴尬。你会发现人行道上有很多比我更值得施舍的可怜乞丐。我用的是这个词最纯粹的含义。"

她看着他的脸。淡淡的胡须和参差不齐的络腮胡掩饰不住他的年轻。首席督察科尔德威尔（Chief Inspector Coldwell）走近了些，以职业性的兴趣注视着他。

"你想知道我刚才真正在想什么吗？"他的声音里带着一种奇特的戏谑意味，"我在想谋杀。这个城里有位先生让我的日子过得很不好过，我刚决定一有机会就走过去，用自动手枪在他心脏里打三个窟窿，你就打断了我杀气腾腾的思绪。"

科尔德威尔轻声笑了。

"我就说认出你了；你是彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish），"他说。那个衣衫褴褛的身影故作礼貌地抬了抬帽子。

===Original===
“Such is fame!” he said sardonically. “And you are Coldwell; the recognition is mutual. And now that I have hopelessly committed myself, I presume you will call the nearest City policeman and put me out of the way of all temptation.”

“When did you come out?” asked Coldwell.

The girl listened, staggered. They had been discussing this man not a quarter of an hour before; she had spent the afternoon thinking of him, and now to meet him on that windswept pavement, he of all the millions of people in London, was something more than a coincidence. It was fatalistic.

“Mr. Dawlish, I wonder if you will believe me when I say that you’re the one man in London I was anxious to meet. I only knew today that you were—out. Could you call and see me tonight?”

The man smiled.

“Invitations follow thick and fast,” he murmured. “Only ten minutes ago I was asked into a Salvation Army shelter. Believe me, madam—”

“Mr. Dawlish”—her voice was very quiet, but very clear—“you are being awfully sorry for yourself, aren’t you?”

She did not see the flush that came to his face.

“I suppose I am,” he said, a little gruffly. “But a man is entitled—”

“A man is never entitled to be sorry for himself in any circumstances,” she said. “Here is my card.”

===Chinese===
"这就是名声啊！"他讽刺地说，"你是科尔德威尔；彼此彼此。既然我已经无可挽回地暴露了自己，我想你会叫来最近的警察，让我远离一切诱惑吧。"

"你什么时候出来的？"科尔德威尔问道。

女孩听着，惊愕不已。不到一刻钟前他们还在谈论这个人；她整个下午都在想他，而现在在那个人来人往的伦敦，在那条风吹过的堤道上遇见他——在数百万伦敦人中偏偏遇见他，这已经不仅仅是巧合了。这是宿命。

"道利什先生，如果我说你是我在伦敦最想见的人，你愿意相信吗？我今天才知道你——出狱了。你今晚能来看我吗？"

男人微微一笑。

"邀约纷至沓来啊，"他喃喃道，"十分钟前我刚被邀请进救世军的收容所。相信我，夫人——"

"道利什先生，"她的声音很轻，却很清晰，"你是在自怜自艾，不是吗？"

她没有看见他脸上泛起的红晕。

"我想是的，"他略带生硬地说，"但一个人有权——"

"一个人在任何情况下都无权自怜，"她说，"这是我的名片。"

===Original===
She had slipped back the cover of her bag, and he took the little pasteboard from her hand, and, bringing it close to his eyes, read, in the dim light that a distant standard afforded.

“Will you come and see me at half-past ten? I shan’t offer you money; I won’t even offer to find a job for you cutting wood or sorting wastepaper—it is a very much bigger matter than that.”

He read the name and superscription again, and his brows met.

“Oh yes—really—yes, if you wish.”

He was, of a sudden, awkward and uncomfortable. The girl was quick to recognize the change in his manner and tone.

“I’m afraid I’m rather a scarecrow, but you won’t mind that?”

“No,” she said, and held out her hand.

He hesitated a second, then took it in his. She felt the hardness of the palm, and winced at the thought of all that these callosities signified. In another second she had joined the waiting Coldwell. Peter Dawlish watched them until they were out of sight, and then, with a little grimace, turned and walked slowly towards Blackfriars.

===Chinese===
她推开手包的搭扣，他从她手中接过那张小纸片，凑到眼前，在远处路灯投来的微弱光线下辨认着。

"你十点半能来看我吗？我不会给你钱；甚至不会给你找份劈柴或分拣废纸的工作——这远比那重要得多。"

他又看了一遍名字和地址，眉头皱了起来。

"哦，好的——当然——如果你愿意的话。"

他突然变得局促不安起来。女孩敏锐地注意到了他态度和语气的变化。

"恐怕我这副样子像個稻草人，但你不会介意吧？"

"不介意，"她说着，伸出了手。

他犹豫了一秒，然后握住她的手。她感受到他掌心的硬茧，想到这些老茧所代表的一切，不禁缩了一下。片刻之后，她已经回到了等候着的科尔德威尔身边。彼得·道利什目送他们直到消失不见，然后做了个鬼脸，转身朝黑衣修士区（Blackfriars）慢慢走去。

===Original===
“I knew about the smallness of the world,” said Coldwell, swinging his furled umbrella, “but I had no idea that applied to London. Peter! It’s years since I saw him last. He was rather a weed five years ago.”

“Do you think he really is a forger?”

“A jury of his fellow-countrymen convicted him,” said Mr. Coldwell cautiously, “and juries are generally right. After all, he needed the money; his father was an old skinflint, and you cannot run a hectic establishment and escort pretty ladies to New York on two hundred and fifty pounds per annum. He was a fool; if he hadn’t taken that three months’ holiday the forgery would never have been discovered.”

“Who was she?” Leslie asked; she felt that this question was called for.

“I don’t know; the police *cherchez la femme*—forgive my mongrel French—but they never ran her to earth. Peter said it was a chorus-girl from the Paris Opera House. He wasn’t particularly proud of it.”

The girl sighed.

“Women are hell,” she said profanely.

“Both places,” suggested Mr. Coldwell, and twirled his grey moustache. “Both places!”

Near the dark entrance of Scotland Yard he stopped.

“Now,” he said, standing squarely before her, “perhaps you will cease being mysterious, and tell me why you are so frantically interested in Peter Dawlish that you have talked Peter Dawlish for the past three days?”

She looked up at him steadily from under the lowered brim of her hat.

“Because I know just why Peter Dawlish is going to kill and whom he is going to kill,” she said.

“Druze—a child would guess that!” scoffed the detective. “And he is going to kill him because he thinks Druze’s evidence sent him to gaol.”

She was smiling—a broad smile of conscious triumph.

“Wrong!” she said. “If Druze dies, it will be because he doesn’t love children!”

Mr. Coldwell could only gape at her.

===Chinese===
"我知道世界很小，"科尔德威尔挥动着收起的雨伞说，"但没想到伦敦也这么小。彼得！我上次见他是好几年前了。五年前他还是个弱不禁风的家伙。"

"你认为他真的是个伪造犯吗？"

"陪审团由他的同胞组成，认定他有罪，"科尔德威尔先生谨慎地说，"陪审团通常是对的。毕竟，他需要钱；他父亲是个吝啬鬼，靠一年两百五十英镑的收入是养不起一个挥金如土的排场、也请不起漂亮女士去纽约的。他是个傻瓜；如果他不请那三个月的假，伪造的事永远不会被发现。"

"那个女人是谁？"莱斯利问道；她觉得这个问题是必须问的。

"我不知道；警察们'cherchez la femme'——原谅我这半吊子法语——但他们始终没能找到她。彼得说她是巴黎歌剧院的一个合唱队女郎。他并不以此为荣。"

女孩叹了口气。

"女人是地狱，"她不敬地说。

"两个地狱，"科尔德威尔先生捻着灰白的胡子说，"两个都是！"

在苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）幽暗的入口附近，他停下了脚步。

"现在，"他正对着她站定，"也许你可以不再故弄玄虚，告诉我你为什么对彼得·道利什如此着迷，以至于过去三天一直在谈论他？"

她从压低的帽檐下稳稳地抬眼望着他。

"因为我知道彼得·道利什为什么要杀人，以及他要杀谁，"她说。

"德鲁兹（Druze）——连小孩都猜得到！"侦探嗤之以鼻，"他要杀他，因为他认为是德鲁兹的证词把他送进了监狱。"

她笑了——那是一种充满胜利自觉的开怀笑容。

"错了！"她说，"如果德鲁兹死了，那是因为他不爱孩子！"

科尔德威尔先生只能目瞪口呆地看着她。

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
Mr. Coldwell peered down into the girl’s face.
“Let me get this right,” he said slowly. “Druze will be killed—if he is killed—because he does not like children?”
Leslie Maughan nodded.
“I know you hate mysteries—everybody in Scotland Yard does,” she said; “and one day I will tell you just what I mean. Do you remember last August you gave me a month’s vacation?”
Chief Inspector Coldwell remembered that very well.
“I went to Cumberland just to loaf around,” she said. “I was most anxious to pretend that there wasn’t such a place in the world as Scotland Yard. But I’ve got that prowling, inquisitive spirit that would have made me the first woman inspector of the C.I.D. if the Commissioners were not such stuffy, old-fashioned gentlemen. One day I was loafing through a little village, when I found something which brought me eventually to this conclusion, that Druze doesn’t like children. And one day, when he discovers the fact, Peter Dawlish will kill him for it!”

===Chinese===
科尔德威尔（Coldwell）低头凝视着女孩的脸。
“让我把事情弄清楚，”他缓缓说道。“德鲁兹（Druze）会被杀——如果他被杀的话——仅仅因为他不喜欢孩子？”
莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan）点了点头。
“我知道你讨厌谜团——苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）里的每个人都讨厌，”她说；“总有一天我会告诉你我的意思。你还记得去年八月你给了我一个月的假期吗？”
首席警探科尔德威尔记得很清楚。
“我去了坎布里亚郡（Cumberland）只是想闲逛，”她说。“我极力想假装世界上根本不存在苏格兰场这个地方。但我有一种喜欢四处打探、追根究底的天性，如果那些委员们不是些古板守旧的老派绅士，这天性本该让我成为刑事调查局（C.I.D.）的第一位女警探。有一天，我正漫无目的地穿过一个小村庄，无意中发现了一些线索，最终让我得出这个结论：德鲁兹不喜欢孩子。而有一天，当他发现这个事实时，彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish）会为此杀了他！”

===Original===
“Mysteriouser and mysteriouser!” groaned Coldwell. “You’re probably chasing a boojum. It is the fate of all enthusiastic young officers—not that you’re an officer.”
Leslie Maughan had started her police career as a very junior stenographer at Scotland Yard. Her father had been that famous Assistant-Commissioner Maughan whose exploits have formed the basis for so many stories of police work, and he had left his daughter with an income which put her above the necessity of working for her living. But police investigation was in her blood, and she had graduated through successive stages, until the authorities, reluctant to admit that any woman had an executive position at Police Headquarters, admitted her to the designation of “assistant” to the Chief of the Big Four.
“She’s brilliant—there’s no other word for her,” he had told the Chief Commissioner. “And although I don’t think it’s much of a woman’s job, there never was a woman who was better fitted to hold down a high position at the Yard.”
“What are her chief qualifications?” asked the Commissioner, slightly amused.
“She thinks quickly and she’s lucky,” was the comprehensive reply.

===Chinese===
“越来越神秘了！”科尔德威尔呻吟道。“你可能是在追逐虚无缥缈的东西。所有热情的年轻警官都是这种命运——虽然你算不上警官。”
莱斯利·莫恩的警察生涯是从苏格兰场一名非常初级的速记员开始的。她的父亲是那位著名的助理总监莫恩，他的英勇事迹构成了许多警察故事的基础，他给女儿留下了一笔收入，使她无需为生计工作。但警察调查的基因流淌在她的血液里，她一路晋升，直到当局——极不情愿承认任何女性能在警察总部担任要职——勉强同意给她一个“助理”的头衔，协助四大部门主管工作。
“她才华横溢——没有别的词可以形容她，”他曾告诉首席总监。“虽然我不认为这是适合女性的工作，但从未有过哪个女人比她更适合在苏格兰场担任要职。”
“她最主要的资质是什么？”总监略带兴趣地问。
“她思维敏捷，而且运气好，”他给出了一个全面的回答。

===Original===
This question of luck exercised the mind of Leslie as she walked home to her flat in the Charing Cross Road. The very fact that that apartment was hers was strong support for the theory of luck. She had taken a long lease of a floor above a cinematograph renter’s at a time when flats were going begging. She might have drawn double the rent from a sub-tenant; but the place was central, comparatively cheap, and she withstood all temptations to change her abode at a profit.
A side door led to the apartments, and she had hardly closed the door behind her when a voice hailed her from the top of the stairs.
“That you, Miss Maughan?”
“That’s me,” said Leslie.
She hung her coat in the narrow hall and went upstairs to the girl who was waiting on the landing. Lucretia Brown, her one servant, was a very tall, broad-shouldered girl, with a round and not unpleasant face. She stood now with her hands on her hips, surveying her mistress.
“I thought you were—” she began.
“You thought I’d been murdered and thrown into the river,” said Leslie good-humouredly. “As you always think if I am not back on the tick.”
“I don’t trust London,” said Lucretia.
It was her real name, chosen by a misguided farm labourer, who, having heard a lecture on the Borgias, delivered at the parish hall, came away with a vague idea that the historical character who bore that name was a worthy creature.
“I never did trust London, and I never will. Have you had dinner, miss?”

===Chinese===
当莱斯利走回查令十字路（Charing Cross Road）的公寓时，运气这个问题困扰着她。这间公寓是她的，这个事实本身就是运气理论的有力佐证。她在公寓供过于求的时候，长期租下了电影院楼上的一层。她本可以以双倍的租金转租出去；但这地方位于市中心，租金相对便宜，而且她经受住了所有转租获利的诱惑。
一扇侧门通往公寓，她刚把身后的门关上，就听到楼梯顶上传来一个声音。
“是你吗，莫恩小姐？”
“是我，”莱斯利说。
她把外套挂在狭窄的门厅里，然后上楼去找在楼梯平台等候的女孩。卢克雷蒂娅·布朗（Lucretia Brown），她唯一的仆人，是一个身材高大、肩膀宽阔的女孩，圆脸，长相并不难看。她双手叉腰站在那里，审视着女主人。
“我以为你——”她开口说。
“你以为我被谋杀扔进河里了，”莱斯利和颜悦色地说。“你总是这样想，如果我没有准时回来的话。”
“我不信任伦敦，”卢克雷蒂娅说。
这是她的真名，由一位误入歧途的农场工人所取，此人在教区礼堂听了一场关于博尔吉亚家族（Borgias）的讲座后，模模糊糊地觉得历史上那个同名的人物是个值得尊敬的人。
“我从来不信任伦敦，也永远不会信任。你吃过晚饭了吗，小姐？”

===Original===
“Yes, I’ve had my dinner,” said Leslie, and looked at the clock. “I am expecting a man to call here at half-past ten, so when you open the door to him please don’t tell him that I’m out and not expected back for three weeks.”
Lucretia made a little face.
“Half-past ten’s a bit late for a gentleman visitor, miss. A friend of yours?”
Leslie could never train her out of a personal interest in her affairs. In a way, Lucretia was privileged. Her first memory was of the broad-faced Lucretia pushing a perambulator in which Leslie took the air.
“Is it anybody we know, miss? Mr. Coldwell?”
Leslie shook her head.
“No,” she said; “he is a man who has just come out of prison.”
Lucretia closed her eyes and swayed.
“Good Gawd!” she said in a hushed voice, “I never thought I’d live to see the day when you’d be having a convick up to see you at half-past ten at night. What about asking a policeman to stand by the door, miss?”
“You’re much too partial to policemen,” said Leslie severely, and the big maid grew incoherent in her indignant protests.

===Chinese===
“是的，我吃过晚饭了，”莱斯利说着，看了看钟。“我约了一个人十点半来这里，所以你给他开门的时候，请别告诉他我不在家，要三周后才回来。”
卢克雷蒂娅做了个鬼脸。
“十点半对男士访客来说有点晚，小姐。是你的朋友吗？”
莱斯利始终无法让她不关心自己的私事。在某种程度上，卢克雷蒂娅享有特权。莱斯利最早的记忆就是圆脸的卢克雷蒂娅推着婴儿车，而她就在车里呼吸新鲜空气。
“是我们认识的人吗，小姐？科尔德威尔先生？”
莱斯利摇了摇头。
“不，”她说；“他是个刚从监狱里出来的人。”
卢克雷蒂娅闭上眼睛，身子摇晃起来。
“天哪！”她压低声音说，“我做梦也没想到会有看到你晚上十点半带个犯人来看你的那一天。要不要叫个警察在门口守着，小姐？”
“你对警察太偏爱了，”莱斯利严厉地说，这位高大的女仆愤怒地抗议着，话说得语无伦次。

===Original===
Half-past ten was striking from St. Martin’s-in-the-Fields when the door bell rang, and Lucretia came in to her, eyes big with excitement.
“That’s him!” she said melodramatically.
“Well, let him in.”
“Whatever happens,” began Lucretia, “I’m not responsible.”
Leslie pointed to the door. He came so lightly up the stairs that she did not hear his steps. The door opened and Lucretia backed in.
“The gentleman,” she said loudly, and cast an apprehensive glance at the stranger as she sidled out of the room and closed the door.
Peter Dawlish stood where Lucretia had left him, his soft hat in his hand, glancing from the girl to the cosy room, a half-smile on his thin face. She saw now how shabbily dressed he was; his shirt was collarless, his boots grey with mud, the old ill-fitting suit he wore stained and patched.
“I warned you I was a scarecrow,” he said, as though he read her thoughts. “They gave me a beautiful prison-made suit at Dartmoor, but it didn’t seem the right kind of equipment to face a censorious world, so I swapped it for this.”

===Chinese===
圣马丁教堂（St. Martin’s-in-the-Fields）的钟声敲响十点半时，门铃响了，卢克雷蒂娅眼睛睁得大大的，兴奋地走进来。
“是他！”她戏剧性地说。
“好吧，让他进来。”
“无论发生什么，”卢克雷蒂娅开口说，“我都不负责。”
莱斯利指了指门。他上楼的脚步如此轻盈，她都没听见。门开了，卢克雷蒂娅倒退着进来。
“先生，”她大声说，一边紧张地瞥了一眼陌生人，一边侧身溜出房间，关上了门。
彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish）站在卢克雷蒂娅离开的地方，手里拿着软帽，目光从女孩移到舒适的房间，瘦削的脸上带着一丝笑意。她现在看出他衣着多么寒酸；他的衬衫没有领子，靴子沾满了灰色的泥巴，身上那件不合身的旧衣服又脏又破。
“我警告过你我是个衣衫褴褛的人，”他说，仿佛读懂了她的心思。“他们在达特穆尔（Dartmoor）给了我一套漂亮的监狱制服装，但似乎不是面对这个挑剔世界的合适装备，所以我用它换了这身。”

===Original===
She pushed a chair up to the fire.
“Sit down, won’t you, Mr. Dawlish?”
“‘Mr. Dawlish,’” he repeated. “That sounds terribly respectable.”
“You may smoke if you wish,” she said, as he seated himself slowly, and again he smiled.
“I wish, but I have not the wherewithal,” and, as she hastily opened a drawer and took out a tin of cigarettes: “Thank you.”
He took the cigarette in his fingers and frowned.
“That is certainly queer,” he said.
“What is certainly queer?” she asked.
“These gaspers—I used to smoke them in the old days. Had ’em imported from Cairo. You can’t buy them here; at least, you couldn’t when I—retired. Heigho! Am I being very sorry for myself again? That stung. I loath these self-pitiers, and it was a revelation to discover that I had gone over to the majority.”
He lit the cigarette and drew luxuriously.
“This is rather wonderful,” he said.

===Chinese===
她把一张椅子推到炉火旁。
“请坐，道利什先生，好吗？”
“‘道利什先生，’”他重复道。“听起来非常体面。”
“如果你想抽烟的话，请便，”她说，他缓缓坐下，他又笑了。
“我想抽，但没有烟，”当她急忙打开抽屉，拿出一盒香烟时，他说道：“谢谢。”
他用手指夹起香烟，皱了皱眉。
“这确实很奇怪，”他说。
“什么确实很奇怪？”她问。
“这些烟卷——我过去常抽。从开罗（Cairo）进口的。在这里买不到；至少，我——退休的时候还买不到。哎呀！我又在自怨自艾了吗？这真伤人。我讨厌这些自怜的人，发现自己也沦落到这大多数人中间，真是个启示。”
他点燃香烟，惬意地吸了一口。
“这真是美妙，”他说。

===Original===
“Have you had any food?” she asked.
He nodded.
“I dined like a sybarite, at a small shop in the Blackfriars Road. The dinner cost sixpence; it was rather extravagant, but I felt I needed bracing for this ordeal.”
“You have no lodging?”
He shook his head.
“No, I have no lodging.”
He was twiddling his long, thin fingers. She noted with satisfaction that his hands were scrupulously clean, and again he seemed to divine her thoughts, for he looked down at them.
“I don’t exactly know what information I can give you, if it is information you require; and if you had been a male of the species policeman I should have declined your invitation rather loftily. But a woman policeman is unique; I’ve seen them, of course—rather fat little bodies with squat little helmets. I suppose they’re useful.”
He noticed that she herself was not smoking, and commented upon it.
“No, I very rarely smoke,” she said. And then, in a changed tone: “Do you mind if I speak very plainly?”
“The plainer the better,” he said, and he leaned back in his chair and sent a cloud of smoke to the ceiling.

===Chinese===
“你吃过东西了吗？”她问。
他点点头。
“我像享乐主义者一样吃了一顿，在黑衣修士路（Blackfriars Road）的一家小店里。晚餐花了六便士；相当奢侈，但我觉得为这次考验我需要提振一下精神。”
“你没有住处？”
他摇了摇头。
“没有，我没有住处。”
他摆弄着细长的手指。她满意地注意到他的手非常干净，而他似乎再次猜到了她的心思，因为他低头看了看自己的手。
“我不太清楚我能给你什么信息，如果你需要信息的话；如果你是个男性警察，我会相当傲慢地拒绝你的邀请。但女警察是独特的；我见过她们，当然——都是些矮胖的小个子，戴着圆圆的小头盔。我想她们是有用的。”
他注意到她自己没有抽烟，便评论了一句。
“不，我很少抽烟，”她说。然后，语气一变：“你介意我说话非常直白吗？”
“越直白越好，”他说，同时向后靠在椅子上，吐出一团烟雾飘向天花板。

===Original===
“You have no money, of course?”
He shook his head.
“Which means that you’ll walk London tonight?”
“It has become a habit,” said Peter Dawlish. “And, really, it would be rather amusing if one weren’t so horribly tired. They gave me a little money when I left prison. It lasted me the greater part of a week; I fear I was improvident. One gets quite a lot of sleep in the daytime, especially the sunny days, in odd corners of the parks. And on rainy nights I know a gardener’s tool-house, which is not perhaps to be compared with the bridal suite at the Ritz, but is cosy. I slept there last night with an ex-colonel of infantry and a lawyer who lived in the same ward at Dartmoor.”
She eyed him steadily.
“Tonight you will sleep decently,” she said, in her quiet, even tone; “and tomorrow you will buy a new suit of clothes and interview your father.”
He raised his eyebrows, amusement in his eyes.
“I didn’t realize that you had scraped down to the family skeleton,” he said. “And why am I to do this, Miss Maughan? The suit of clothes would be a waste of money; my parent would not be impressed by my appearance of affluence. Rather he would imagine that I had found another good-natured gentleman who trusted me with his chequebook. Furthermore, all this would cost money; and I think you should know, before we go any farther, that I am not taking any money from you on any pretext.”

===Chinese===
“你当然没有钱了？”
他摇了摇头。
“这意味着你今晚要在伦敦街头过夜？”
“这已经成了习惯，”彼得·道利什说。“而且，真的，要不是累得要命，这还挺有趣的。我出狱时他们给了我一点钱。我用了差不多一周；恐怕我太不会规划了。白天在公园的角落里可以睡很多觉，尤其是晴天。雨夜我知道一个园丁的工具房，也许比不上丽兹酒店（Ritz）的新婚套房，但很舒适。昨晚我和一位前陆军上校以及一位律师睡在那里，他们在达特穆尔同一个牢房。”
她坚定地看着他。
“今晚你会睡得像个样，”她用平静、平稳的语调说；“明天你会去买一套新衣服，然后去见你的父亲。”
他扬起了眉毛，眼里带着笑意。
“我没想到你已经挖到了家族的丑事，”他说。“为什么我要这么做，莫恩小姐？买衣服是浪费钱；我父亲不会因为我显得富有而印象深刻。相反，他会以为我又找到了一个信任我、把支票簿交给我的好心绅士。而且，这一切都要花钱；我想你应该知道，在我们继续之前，我不会以任何借口拿你的钱。”

===Original===
She had the extraordinary knack of making him feel foolish. He always remembered afterwards that in the first two meetings with this strange girl, he had gone hot and cold either at her words or the inflection of her voice.
“That kind of pride which refuses to take money from a woman is very admirable.” There was a note of cold sarcasm in her voice which made him writhe. “It is the attitude of mind behind man’s subconscious sense of superiority to the female of the species—not particularly flattering to a woman, but it must be immensely gratifying to a man! May I ask you another question, Mr. Peter Dawlish? Do you intend sinking down into the dregs? Is your vista of life lined on either side by common lodging Houses, with a pauper’s graveyard at the end of it?”
“I don’t exactly see what you’re driving at.”
She had made him angry, and, was secretly amused.
“I shall do my best, naturally, to find work. I had an idea of going abroad.”
“Exactly.” She nodded. “To one of the colonies. It is the most popular of all delusions that people without grit or ambition can magically acquire these qualities the moment they go ashore at Quebec or Sydney, or wherever their high spirits lead them.”
He was laughing now in spite of himself.
“You’ve certainly got a knack of riling a man.”
“Haven’t I?” she smiled. “I’ll tell you what I was driving at, Mr. Dawlish. For you to refuse a loan of money now suggests that you’re perfectly satisfied in your mind that you will never earn enough to repay the loan. The only way you can justify a refusal of money is to believe that you can never pay it back; that you’re going to belong to the bread lines and the park benches and the public charities.”

===Chinese===
她有一种非凡的本事，能让他觉得自己很傻。他后来总是记得，与这个奇特女孩的前两次见面，无论她说的话还是语调的变化，都让他时而发热时而发冷。
“那种拒绝接受女人钱的骄傲令人钦佩。”她声音里带着冰冷的讽刺，让他感到难堪。“这是男人潜意识中认为自己比女性优越的心理态度——对女人来说没什么值得夸耀的，但对男人来说一定非常满足！我能再问你一个问题吗，彼得·道利什先生？你打算沉沦到社会的最底层吗？你的人生愿景是不是两边都是廉价的寄宿公寓，尽头是一片穷人的墓地？”
“我不太明白你的意思。”
她惹他生气了，而她暗自觉得好笑。
“我自然会尽力找份工作。我曾想过出国。”
“正是如此。”她点点头。“去某个殖民地。有一种最流行的幻想，认为那些没有毅力或抱负的人一旦踏上魁北克（Quebec）或悉尼（Sydney）的土地，或者无论他们兴致所至的地方，就能奇迹般地获得这些品质。”
他现在忍不住笑了。
“你确实有惹人生气的本事。”
“不是吗？”她微笑道。“我来告诉你我的意思，道利什先生。你现在拒绝借钱，表明你内心完全相信自己永远赚不够钱来偿还这笔债。你拒绝接受钱的唯一正当理由，就是你相信自己永远还不起；你注定要加入排队领救济面包的队伍，坐公园长椅，接受公共慈善机构的施舍。”

===Original===
She saw that her shaft had got home, and went on quickly:
“Of course, you will do nothing of the sort! You’ve come out of prison with a grievance against the world, and you’re hardly to be blamed for that. I should imagine you are one of the few innocent men who ever went to Dartmoor.”
He looked at her shrewdly.
“You believe I was innocent?”
She nodded.
“I’m pretty sure,” she said, and then: “Do you carry a gun?”
He laughed aloud.
“The price of a Browning pistol would keep me in luxury for two months,” he said. “No, I carry nothing more dangerous than a toothbrush.”
The drawer from which she had taken the cigarettes was still open, and she put in her hand and took out a small black cashbox, and jerked back the lid.
“We will do this thing in a businesslike way,” she said. “You will find a paper and pencil on the desk; sign an I.O.U. for twenty pounds. If you believe in your heart of hearts that you’ll be unable to pay me back, that a man of twenty-nine or twenty-eight, or whatever age you are, will never earn a sufficient margin above his cost of living to send back that money in a year, or two years, then you need not take a cent. And this little bit of charity, as you call it—”
“I’ve called it nothing of the sort.”
“In your mind you have,” she said calmly. “It is very rude to contradict a lady! Now, Mr. Dawlish, I challenge you. If you think you are permanently down and out, the incident is finished—and I think you’re finished, too.”
She looked at him through her half-closed eyes, nodding slowly.
“You mean I’m not worth salving?” he said, and got up. “I’ll accept your challenge.”

===Chinese===
她看到自己的话击中了要害，迅速继续说：
“当然，你不会那么做的！你出狱时对世界心怀怨恨，这很难怪你。我想你是少数几个无辜却进了达特穆尔监狱的人之一。”
他精明地看着她。
“你相信我是无辜的？”
她点点头。
“我相当肯定，”她说，然后问道：“你带枪了吗？”
他大声笑起来。
“一把勃朗宁（Browning）手枪的价格够我奢侈地生活两个月，”他说。“不，我带的东西没比牙刷更危险的了。”
她拿香烟的那个抽屉还开着，她把手伸进去，取出一个小黑钱盒，猛地掀开盒盖。
“我们要公事公办，”她说。“你会发现桌子上有纸和笔；签一张二十英镑的借据。如果你内心深处相信自己无法偿还，相信一个二十九或二十八岁（或不管你现在多大）的男人永远赚不够足够的钱来偿还这笔债，一年或两年内寄回，那么你一分钱都不必拿。而这点你所谓的慈善——”
“我可没这么叫过。”
“你心里是这么想的，”她平静地说。“反驳一位女士非常无礼！现在，道利什先生，我向你挑战。如果你认为自己永远完蛋了，这事就到此为止——而且我认为你也完了。”
她透过半闭的眼睛看着他，慢慢地点了点头。
“你是说我不值得拯救？”他说着，站了起来。“我接受你的挑战。”

===Original===
He took the pencil, scribbled a few words on the writing-pad, and, tearing it off, handed it to the girl.
“Produce your twenty pounds.”
He was amused in a sour way, but his anger was mostly directed inward to himself, that he should be angry at all. If anybody had told him, when he had walked into that room, that he would accept a loan of money from the girl who had not been absent from his thoughts since he had met her, he would have laughed at such a suggestion. Yet here he was, counting solemnly the Treasury notes as they were handed to him, and pocketing them without one single qualm of conscience.
“I think I’m beginning to know myself,” he said. “I started a weakling, and prison hasn’t improved me. No, no, I do not mean that it is a weakness to accept this money, but it would have been a weakness to have refused. I’m awfully obliged to you.”
She held out her hand.
“Where will you be staying?” she asked.
“I don’t know. But I will keep in touch with you. Please don’t bother about me any more. If I can’t get a job of some kind I’m really not worth helping. Why are you doing this? It isn’t part of the usual police procedure.”
She shook her head.
“The police help where they can; you ought to know that,” she said quietly. “But I admit that this is a purely personal action on my part. You are part of a big experiment. It isn’t my womanly heart, but my scientific brain that is dictating just now.” And then, going off at a tangent: “I wish you would shave yourself, Mr. Dawlish; you look too much like a musical genius to be thoroughly wholesome.”
He was still chuckling to himself when Lucretia closed the outer door upon him with unnecessary violence.

===Chinese===
他拿起铅笔，在便签本上草草写了几行字，撕下来递给女孩。
“拿出你的二十英镑。”
他带着一种苦涩的自嘲，但他的愤怒主要指向自己，他竟然会生气。如果在他走进那个房间时有人告诉他，他会接受那个从遇见起就一直在他脑海中的女孩借钱给他，他会对这种想法嗤之以鼻。然而他现在就在这里，庄严地数着递给他的钞票，然后揣进口袋，没有丝毫良心不安。
“我想我开始了解自己了，”他说。“我一开始就是个懦夫，监狱并没有让我变好。不，不，我并不是说接受这笔钱是懦弱，但拒绝它才是懦弱。我非常感激你。”
她伸出手。
“你打算住哪里？”她问。
“我不知道。但我会和你保持联系。请不要再为我操心了。如果我找不到一份工作，我真的不值得帮助。你为什么要这么做？这不是通常的警察程序。”
她摇了摇头。
“警察会在能力范围内提供帮助；你应该知道这一点，”她平静地说。“但我承认这纯粹是我个人的行为。你是一个大实验的一部分。此刻指引我的不是我那颗女性的心，而是我的科学头脑。”然后，话题一转：“我希望你刮刮胡子，道利什先生；你看上去太像个音乐天才了，不太健康。”
他还在暗自发笑时，卢克雷蒂娅不必要地用力关上了外面的门。

===Original===
He knew a small temperance hotel where he could sleep that night, a place in Lambeth, near Waterloo Station. “Temperance Hotel” was rather a grand name for an establishment which was only a little superior to a common lodging-house, but he guessed it was too late to get a bed at any of the Rowton Houses.
He walked briskly down Charing Cross Road and into the Strand, crowded with cars and taxis, for the theatres were closing, and the northern sidewalk was almost impassable. And then he thought he saw his mother preceding another lady into a car, and stopped. Yes, it was Margaret Dawlish, and the lady with the dirty grey hair was Aunt Anita. He could afford to grin now, and the discovery was very pleasurable. He could well imagine that if he had seen that party earlier in the evening, the sight would have evoked a sneer and just that twinge of self-pity against which he was trying hard to guard himself.
He turned back, lest in passing they recognized him, and went down Villiers Street, mounting the stairs to Hungerford Bridge. It was not the twenty notes in his pocket, a compact, cosy little roll, that made his heart and his step lighter; he had caught something of the girl’s spirit, had been imbued with a little of her courage and sanity.
Leslie Maughan puzzled him. She was more than pretty; there was in her face a spirituality which he had not detected in the face of any woman of his acquaintance or knowledge. He realized with a start that he had always disliked clever women. He liked them soft and feminine, and, if the truth be told, a little silly. But he liked this capable and pretty young woman.

===Chinese===
他知道一家小型禁酒旅馆，今晚可以在那里过夜，在滑铁卢车站（Waterloo Station）附近的兰贝斯区（Lambeth）。“禁酒旅馆”这个名字对于一家只比普通寄宿公寓好一点的旅馆来说相当堂皇，但他猜现在去罗顿公寓（Rowton Houses）找床位太晚了。
他沿着查令十字路快步走进斯特兰德大街（Strand），那里挤满了汽车和出租车，剧院正在散场，北边的人行道几乎无法通行。然后他似乎看见他的母亲正要和另一位女士上车，于是停下了脚步。没错，那是玛格丽特·道利什（Margaret Dawlish），而那位头发脏兮兮灰白的女士是安妮塔姨妈（Aunt Anita）。他现在可以笑了，这个发现令人愉快。他完全可以想象，如果他今晚早些时候看到那群人，那景象会激起一阵冷笑和一阵自怜——他正努力克制这种自怜。
他转身往回走，以免路过时被他们认出，然后沿着维利尔斯街（Villiers Street）走向亨格福德桥（Hungerford Bridge）。让他心情和步伐都变得轻松起来的，并不是口袋里那卷整齐、舒适的小纸币；他捕捉到了那个女孩的一些精神，被灌输了一点她的勇气和理智。
莱斯利·莫恩让他困惑。她不只是漂亮；她的脸上有一种他从未在任何相识或了解的女性脸上发现过的灵性。他猛然意识到，他一直不喜欢聪明的女人。他喜欢她们温柔、有女人味，而且，说实话，有点傻。但他喜欢这个能干又漂亮的年轻女人。

===Original===
Leslie Maughan had just enough of the official quality to keep him at a distance, and yet she was genuinely friendly, as friendly as a sensible elder sister might be, though in truth she must be years younger than he. Sometimes he felt a very old man—Leslie Maughan had made him feel like a child.
He was over the middle of the river now, and there was revealed to him the pageantry of the Embankment, with its lights reflected in the dark waters of the Thames; a great Scotsman in lights ornamented a tower on the south side. He felt himself responding to the glow and colour of it. And then, for no reason at all, he had an uncomfortable feeling that he could not trace, but instinctively he looked back. There were several people crossing with him, but immediately behind him, not half a dozen yards away, were three little men who moved shoulder to shoulder. They had the curious high-stepping walk which he had seen in Orientals; a sort of modified prance. They were not speaking to one another, as friends might who were walking home together, and, curiously enough, it was their silence which made him uneasy. Five years in a penal establishment had not been a good nerve cure for a man of Peter Dawlish’s temperament.
He ran down the steps and found himself in a dim and gloomy street. From here was a shortcut to the York Road, near where his temperance hotel was situated. His way led him through a deserted street of tiny houses that was not quite a slum but was barely respectable. As he turned into the thoroughfare he glanced back and saw that the three little men were following. They moved noiselessly, as though they were wearing rubber shoes. Peter crossed the road and they followed, a little nearer to him.
He was wondering whether it would not be better to turn and face them till they had passed, and he had decided upon this action when something fell over his head. He raised his hand quickly to catch at the thin rope, but too late; the slipknot tightened about his throat, two muscular little figures leapt at him, and in another second he was lying on the ground, fighting for life, strangled, his head bursting, his hands clawing at the rope. And then consciousness left him. After an eternity he felt somebody lifting him up and propping him against a wall; a brilliant light shone on his face.

===Chinese===
莱斯利·莫恩恰好有足够的官方气质让他保持距离，但她又是真心友好的，就像一个理智的姐姐可能会那样友好，尽管实际上她肯定比他年轻好几岁。有时他觉得自己很老——莱斯利·莫恩让他感觉自己像个孩子。
他现在走到了河中央，堤岸的盛景展现在他面前，灯光倒映在泰晤士河（Thames）漆黑的水面上；南岸一座塔楼上装饰着一个巨大的苏格兰人（Scotsman）灯牌。他感到自己被这光芒和色彩所吸引。然后，不知为何，他产生了一种无法名状的不安感，本能地回头看去。有几个人和他一起过桥，但就在他身后不到几码的地方，有三个小个子男人并肩而行。他们步伐奇特，高抬腿走路，像他在东方人身上见过的那样；一种改良的昂首阔步。他们不像一起回家的朋友那样交谈，而且奇怪的是，正是他们的沉默让他感到不安。在监狱里度过的五年对彼得·道利什这样性情的人来说，并不是治疗神经的好方法。
他跑下台阶，发现自己置身于一条昏暗阴郁的街道。从这里有一条捷径通往约克路（York Road），他的禁酒旅馆就在附近。他的路穿过一条荒凉的小房子街道，算不上贫民窟，但勉强还算体面。当他拐上大街时，回头瞥了一眼，发现那三个小个子男人在跟踪。他们悄无声息地移动，仿佛穿着橡胶鞋。彼得穿过马路，他们也跟了过来，离他更近了。
他正思忖着是否不如转身面对他们，等他们走过去，就在他决定采取这个行动时，有东西套住了他的头。他迅速抬手去抓那根细绳，但太迟了；活结勒紧了他的喉咙，两个肌肉结实的小个子扑向他，下一秒他就倒在地上，为生命挣扎着，被勒得喘不过气，头要炸开，双手抓挠着绳子。然后他失去了意识。过了仿佛永恒那么久，他感到有人把他扶起来，靠在墙上；一道刺眼的光照在他脸上。

===Original===
Peter put his hand to his throat; the rope had gone, but he could still feel the deep depression it had made upon his skin.
“What was the game?” said a gruff voice.
He blinked up, could distinguish a helmeted head—a policeman.
“How do you feel? Would you like me to get an ambulance? I can put you into the hospital in a minute.”
Shaking in every limb, Peter struggled to his feet.
“I’m all right,” he said unsteadily. “Who were they?”
The policeman shook his head.
“I don’t know. They passed me at the end of the street and I thought they looked queer. Little fellows with flat noses—more like monkeys than men. And then I saw them go for you and came after them. I think I just about saved your life, young fellow.”
“I think you did,” said Peter ruefully, as he felt at his scarred throat.
“Run! I never saw anybody run as fast as they did,” said the constable. “Did you have a row with them?”
“No; I never saw them before in my life,” said Peter.
“Humph!” The officer was looking at him dubiously. “Wonder who they was? They talked in some lingo I didn’t understand. I only caught one word, or maybe it’s two—orange pander or bander.”
“*Orang blanga?*” asked Peter quickly, and whistled.
“Know ’em?”
Peter shook his head.
“No, I don’t know them. I guess their nationality. Javanese.”
The officer was loth to leave him.
“Where are you going now?”
“I’m trying to find a lodging.”
He was still far from recovered, for when he took a step the street and the officer went round in a mad whirl, and but for the policeman’s arm he would have fallen.
“You’ll get yourself pinched for being drunk,” said the policeman humorously. “Lodgings? Now, where did I see a lodging?”
He switched on his light, walked slowly down the street, flashing the lamp upon the windows. Presently he stopped.
“Here you are,” he said.
Peter made a slow and cautious way to where the policeman was standing. The lantern was focused upon a little card in the window:

===Chinese===
彼得把手伸向喉咙；绳子已经不见了，但他仍能感觉到它在皮肤上留下的深深凹痕。
“搞什么鬼？”一个粗哑的声音说。
他眨了眨眼，抬起头，能辨认出一个戴着头盔的脑袋——一个警察。
“你感觉怎么样？需要我叫救护车吗？我马上就能把你送进医院。”
彼得浑身发抖，挣扎着站起来。
“我没事，”他声音不稳地说。“他们是谁？”
警察摇了摇头。
“我不知道。他们在街尾从我身边经过，我觉得他们看起来很古怪。小个子，塌鼻子——更像猴子而不是人。然后我看到他们袭击你，就追了过来。我想我刚好救了你的命，年轻人。”
“我想是的，”彼得懊恼地说，一边摸着伤痕累累的喉咙。
“跑得真快！我从没见过谁跑得像他们那么快，”警官说。“你和他们吵过架吗？”
“没有；我这辈子从没见过他们，”彼得说。
“哼！”警官怀疑地看着他。“想知道他们是谁？他们说一种我听不懂的土话。我只听懂了一个词，也许是两个——橙子潘德尔或班德尔（orange pander or bander）。”
“*Orang blanga?*”彼得迅速问道，然后吹了声口哨。
“认识他们？”
彼得摇摇头。
“不，我不认识他们。我猜他们的国籍。爪哇人（Javanese）。”
警官不愿离开他。
“你现在要去哪里？”
“我想找个住处。”
他远未恢复，因为当他迈步时，街道和警官都在疯狂地旋转，要不是警察扶着他，他早就摔倒了。
“你会因为醉酒被拘捕的，”警察幽默地说。“住处？我在哪里见过一个住处来着？”
他打开手电筒，慢慢沿着街道走去，灯光扫过窗户。不久他停了下来。
“就在这里，”他说。
彼得缓慢而谨慎地走到警察站着的地方。手电筒的光照在窗户上的一张小卡片上：

===Original===
> Lodgings for a Respectable Young Man.
“Will this do for you?”
Peter nodded, and the constable rapped gently on the door. He had to wait some time, but presently there was a heavy foot in the passage, and a woman’s voice asked hoarsely:
“Who is there?”
“It’s all right, missis,” said the custodian of the law. “I’m a policeman; there’s a gentleman here who wants a lodging.”
The door was unlocked and opened a few inches.
“I’ve got a room, yes; but it’s a bit late, ain’t it?”
The constable uttered an exclamation of surprise.
“Why, bless me, it’s Mrs. Inglethorne!”
“Yes, it’s Mrs. Inglethorne,” repeated the woman bitterly. “And well you ought to know, considering the trouble you police have brought on me. My old man being as innocent as a babe unborn—and our lodger as nice a young man as ever drew the breath of life.”
She peered at Peter in the reflection from the policeman’s light; he saw a bloated red face, a loose mouth, and eyes of singular smallness. She was short and stout, and wore a red flannel dressing-gown, though apparently she had not disrobed for the night.
“I can’t take you unless you’ve got money,” she said. “I’ve been done before.”
Peter skinned a pound from the roll and showed it to her.
“All right, come in,” she said ungraciously.
Stopping only to thank the policeman for his offices, Peter followed her into the narrow, evil-smelling passage, and the door closed behind him.
Fate had played its supreme joke on Peter Dawlish when it had led him to the unsavoury home of Mrs. Inglethorne.

===Chinese===
> 为体面的年轻人提供住宿。
“这个适合你吗？”
彼得点点头，警官轻轻敲了敲门。他不得不等了一会儿，但不久走廊里传来沉重的脚步声，一个女人嘶哑的声音问道：
“谁啊？”
“没事的，太太，”执法者说。“我是警察；这里有位先生想找个住处。”
门锁打开了，开了几英寸。
“我有间房，是的；但时间有点晚了，不是吗？”
警官惊讶地叫了起来。
“哎呀，天哪，是英格尔索恩太太（Mrs. Inglethorne）！”
“是的，是英格尔索恩太太，”女人苦涩地重复道。“你早该知道，考虑到你们警察给我惹的麻烦。我男人像刚出生的婴儿一样无辜——而我们的房客是个再好不过的年轻人，从没伤害过任何人。”
她借助警察手电筒的反光打量着彼得；他看到一张浮肿的红脸，一张松垮的嘴，还有异常小的眼睛。她身材矮胖，穿着一件红色法兰绒晨衣，显然还没有脱衣服睡觉。
“你没钱我不能收你，”她说。“我以前被骗过。”
彼得从那卷钞票中剥出一张一英镑的给她看。
“好吧，进来吧，”她不客气地说。
彼得谢过警察的帮助后，跟着她走进狭窄、散发恶臭的走廊，门在身后关上了。
命运对彼得·道利什开了一个最大的玩笑，把他引到了英格尔索恩太太这个令人不快的家。

===Original===
She struck a match, lit a smelly little oil-lamp, and preceded him up a steep, short flight of stairs to the floor above.
“Here’s the room,” she said, and he followed her into the front and the best bedroom in the house.
To his surprise it was fairly well furnished; the bed was a new one, the walls had been lately papered, the two cheap engravings which constituted the pictorial embellishment of the apartment were in good taste.
“This was my lodger’s room; he furnished it himself,” said Mrs. Inglethorne rapidly. “As nice a man as ever drew the breath of life.” She pronounced the last sentence so quickly that it almost seemed to be one word.
“Has he left you?”
She glanced at him suspiciously, as though she thought that he was already informed as to the lodger’s fate.
“He’s got five for busting a house up at Blackheath. My old man got seven, and an honester man there never was.”
A grim jest this, thought Peter Dawlish, that he, newly from that drab and drear establishment on Dartmoor, should be offered the vacant bedroom of one who had taken his place, was probably in the very cell in B Ward he had occupied.
“Pay in advance—eight shillings. I’ll give you the change tomorrow.” Mrs. Inglethorne held out her hand. In the light of the lamp she was even more unprepossessing than Peter had thought.
He gathered from certain evidence that prohibition would find no vigorous supporter in her; she took the money he gave her, and, setting down the lamp, opened a chest, and extracted two new sheets. Evidently, thought Peter, as he watched the process of bed-making, the burglar lodger was fastidious in the matter of comfort; the sheets were linen. He discovered later that the pillows were of down, and that the bed itself was a luxurious article purchased at great cost in Tottenham Court Road.
“He liked everything of the best,” said Mrs. Inglethorne, pausing in her labors to extol the absent tenant.
She went out soon after, leaving behind her a faint odour of spirituous liquor, and he undressed slowly by the light of the lamp, preparing for the first good night’s sleep he had had in a week.
The bed was soft—too soft. Although he was desperately tired, he tossed from side to side in a vain endeavour to sleep. It must have been two hours before he dozed, and then he woke.
It was a shrill, thin cry that woke him, and he sat up in bed listening. It came again, from somewhere downstairs. It was a cat, he thought, no human voice was capable of such an attenuation of sound.
Again the cry. He got out of bed, walked to the door, opened it, and bent his head, listening. And then the hair of his head rose. It was a child’s sobs he heard, and then a voice.
“I want my daddy! I want my daddy!”
He heard Mrs. Inglethorne’s growling voice, as if she had been wakened from her sleep.
“Shut up, blast you! If I get up to you I’ll break your neck!”
And then the voices ceased, and Peter went back to bed. But it was not until the sound of closing doors in the street told him that the early workers were abroad that he fell into a troubled sleep, disturbed by dreams of a child who cried and moaned all the time: “I want my daddy! I want my daddy!”

===Chinese===
她划了根火柴，点燃一盏发臭的小油灯，领着他走上一段陡峭的短楼梯，到了楼上。
“房间在这儿，”她说，他跟着她走进了这间位于房子前部、也是最好的卧室。
令他惊讶的是，房间布置得相当不错；床是新的，墙壁最近刚贴过壁纸，装饰房间的两幅廉价版画品味不错。
“这是我房客的房间；他自己布置的，”英格尔索恩太太语速很快地说。“是个再好不过的年轻人。”她说最后一句话时快得几乎像是一个词。
“他离开你了？”
她怀疑地看了他一眼，仿佛他已知道房客的下场。
“他在布莱克希思（Blackheath）因入室盗窃被判五年。我男人被判七年，他是我见过的最老实的人。”
这是个冷酷的玩笑，彼得·道利什想，他刚从达特穆尔那个沉闷阴郁的地方出来，却被安排住进那个顶替他位置、很可能就在B区他住过的牢房里的罪犯腾出的空房间。
“先付钱——八先令。明天找你零钱。”英格尔索恩太太伸出手。在灯光下，她看起来比彼得想象的还要令人不快。
他从某些迹象推断，禁酒令不会得到她的积极支持；她接过他给的钱，放下灯，打开一个箱子，取出两条新床单。显然，彼得一边看着她铺床一边想，这位盗贼房客在舒适方面很讲究；床单是亚麻的。他后来发现枕头是羽绒的，而床本身是在托特纳姆法院路（Tottenham Court Road）高价买来的豪华货。
“他喜欢用最好的东西，”英格尔索恩太太停下手中的活，夸赞着缺席的房客。
她很快就出去了，留下一股淡淡的烈酒气味。他在灯光下慢慢脱衣服，准备迎接一周来的第一个好觉。
床很软——太软了。尽管他累得要命，却徒劳地辗转反侧，试图入睡。大约两个小时后他才打了个盹，然后又醒了。
是一声尖利细弱的哭声惊醒了他，他坐在床上倾听。哭声再次传来，从楼下的某个地方。是只猫，他想，人类的声音不可能发出这么细的声音。
哭声又响了。他下床走到门口，打开门，弯下腰倾听。然后他的头发竖了起来。他听到的是孩子的呜咽声，接着是一个声音。
“我要爸爸！我要爸爸！”
他听到英格尔索恩太太低吼的声音，好像被从睡梦中吵醒了。
“闭嘴，该死的！要是我上来，就打断你的脖子！”
然后声音消失了，彼得回到床上。但直到街上关门的声音告诉他早起的工人已经出门时，他才陷入不安的睡眠，梦中一直有一个孩子在哭喊：“我要爸爸！我要爸爸！”

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
Druze﻿—a woman! It was incredible﻿—almost impossible! Yet that shrewd old man would not have jested with her. She dragged herself up the stairs, her limp body aching for rest, her mind very wide awake and alert.
Druze a woman! She shook her head helplessly. And then she remembered Lady Raytham's hysterical laughter. "What was Druze to you?" Jane Raytham knew!
Leslie was too sane, too big, to feel foolish. She stopped on the landing and, leaning heavily on the balustrade, she recalled the hairless face and figure of the portly butler. All her theories must go by the board. A scaffolding must be erected on a new foundation.
She found Lucretia Brown huddled up in a chair before a dead fire fast asleep. Lucretia had never been trained out of her habit of "waiting up." It was her firm conviction that only this practice of hers saved her mistress from a terrible fate. She woke with a start and came reeling to her feet.
"Oh, miss!" she gasped. "What time is it?"
Leslie glanced at the mantelpiece.
"Three o'clock," she said, "and a fine morning! Why aren't you in bed, you poor, knock-kneed girl?"
"I'm not knock-kneed, and never was," protested Lucretia. "Three o'clock, miss? What a time!" She shivered. And then, morbidly curious: "Has anything been up, miss?"

===Chinese===
德鲁兹——是个女人！这太不可思议了——几乎不可能！但那个精明的老头儿不会跟她开玩笑。她拖着疲惫的身躯走上楼梯，酸痛的身体渴望着休息，头脑却格外清醒警觉。
德鲁兹是个女人！她无奈地摇摇头。这时她想起了雷瑟姆夫人歇斯底里的笑声。"德鲁兹对你算什么？"简·雷瑟姆知道！
莱斯利心智健全、气量宽宏，不会觉得自己犯蠢。她在楼梯平台上停下脚步，重重倚着栏杆，回忆起那个胖管家光滑无须的面庞和体态。她所有的理论都必须推倒重来。一切都要在新的地基上重建。
她发现卢克丽霞·布朗（Lucretia Brown）蜷缩在一把椅子上，面前的炉火已灭，她睡得很沉。卢克丽霞从未被训练改掉"熬夜等候"的习惯。她坚信只有这个习惯才能将女主人从可怕的厄运中拯救出来。她猛然惊醒，摇摇晃晃地站起来。
"哦，小姐！"她喘着粗气。"几点了？"
莱斯利瞥了一眼壁炉架。
"三点钟，"她说，"还是个大晴天！你怎么不上床睡觉，可怜的罗圈腿丫头？"
"我没有罗圈腿，从来没有过，"卢克丽霞反驳道。"三点钟，小姐？好晚了！"她打了个寒颤。接着又病态地好奇起来："出什么事了吗，小姐？"

---

===Original===
"More things are 'up' than will ever come down, I think," replied Leslie, as she dropped into a chair. "There's been a murder."
"Good Gawd!" said the shocked Lucretia. And then, with pardonable curiosity: "Who done it?"
"If I knew 'who'd done it,' I'd be a very contented female."
Leslie stifled a yawn.
"Run the bath, Lucretia, make me some hot milk, and don't wake me till ten o'clock."
"If I'm awake then," said Lucretia ominously. "I never see such a place as this. You turn night into day, as the Good Book says﻿—London's a modern Babbyling! Did he have his throat cut?" She returned to the tragedy.
"No, I'm sorry to disappoint you; but it was quite ghastly enough."
She dragged herself to her feet and went to her desk, turning over the letters that had arrived by the night mail. There was one which looked promising. She tore off the end of the envelope, read its contents, and locked the document away in a drawer. Ten minutes later, before Lucretia had run the water from the bath, Leslie Maughan, snug between sheets, was sleeping dreamlessly.

===Chinese===
"我觉得'出事'的东西太多了，多到收拾不完，"莱斯利说着跌进椅子。"出了命案。"
"天哪！"卢克丽霞惊呼。接着又带着情有可原的好奇心问："谁干的？"
"我要是知道'谁干的'，我就是个心满意足的女人了。"
莱斯利忍住一个哈欠。
"去放洗澡水，卢克丽霞，给我热杯牛奶，十点之前别叫我。"
"到时候我能不能醒还不一定呢，"卢克丽霞不祥地预言。"我从没见过这种地方。就像《圣经》说的，你把黑夜变成了白昼——伦敦就是现代的巴比伦（Babylon）！他是被割喉的吗？"她又回到了悲剧上。
"不是，我很抱歉让你失望了；不过已经够惨的了。"
她拖着身子站起来走到书桌前，翻看夜间送达的信件。有一封看起来有戏。她撕开信封的一角，读完内容，把文件锁进抽屉。十分钟后，卢克丽霞还没放好洗澡水，莱斯利·莫恩已经舒舒服服钻进被窝，沉沉地睡着了。

---

===Original===
She woke with a dim remembrance of the rattle of teacups and of Lucretia's calling her. Half opening her eyes, she saw the cup by her bedside. She was horribly tired; bed was a warm and luxurious place. She must have dozed, for the sound of voices wakened her.
Her bedroom led from her sitting-room, and the door was half-open. Two people were speaking﻿—Lucretia and somebody else whose voice was familiar.
"I will wait. Please don't wake Miss Maughan specially for me."
Leslie sat up in bed. Through the closing door which the maid was jealously guarding, she saw the big, straight figure of a woman. Lady Raytham! In an instant she was out of bed, thrust her feet into slippers, and pulled her dressing-gown about her. She stopped only at the mirror to brush back her hair.

===Chinese===
她醒来时隐约记得茶杯碰撞的声响和卢克丽霞的呼唤。半睁开眼睛，她看见床头柜上放着茶杯。她累极了；床铺温暖而舒适。她一定是打了个盹，因为说话声把她吵醒了。
她的卧室与起居室相连，房门半开着。两个人在说话——卢克丽霞和另一个声音很熟悉的人。
"我等着就好。请不要特意叫醒莫恩小姐。"
莱斯利在床上坐起身。透过被女仆小心守护着正在关上的门缝，她看到一个高大挺拔的女人身影。雷瑟姆夫人！她瞬间翻身下床，把脚塞进拖鞋，披上晨袍。只在镜子前停下脚步梳了梳头发。

---

===Original===
Lady Raytham was standing in the middle of the study, a bright coal fire was burning, and the room, at that early hour of the morning, had a special attraction for its young owner. But Jane Raytham's presence seemed, for some unaccountable reason, to lend it a new distinction, as a great bunch of Easter lilies or a bowl of narcissus might have done.
"Good morning. I'm sorry to be so early. I hope I did not disturb you."
She was polite, almost frigidly so, and Leslie could only look at her in wonder. All the evidence of distress and terror that had marked her face on the night before had vanished﻿—all except that dark tint under the eyes.
"Won't you sit down? Have you had breakfast?" asked Leslie practically.
Lady Raytham shook her head.
"Please don't bother about me; I have plenty of time and can wait," she said.
There was a certain resentful admiration in her gaze; she was thinking how few women of her acquaintance were presentable at such an hour and in such circumstances. She had never seen Leslie Maughan in the daytime before, and not only did she stand the test of the cruel morning light, but she looked even prettier. She liked the poise of the girl and the readiness with which she accepted this sensible suggestion and disappeared into the bathroom, the gawky maid, her arms laden with garments, following. By the time she came out, Lucretia Brown had laid a little table; huge blue coffee cups and china racks bristling with crisp brown toast.
"No, I couldn't eat, thank you." Lady Raytham shook her head. "I will have some coffee."
Leslie looked significantly at the door and Lucretia regretfully disappeared.

===Chinese===
雷瑟姆夫人站在书房中央，炉火明亮，这个清晨时分的房间对年轻的女主人有着特殊的吸引力。但简·雷瑟姆的出现不知为何为房间增添了一种新的格调，就像一大束复活节百合或一盆水仙花可能带来的效果。
"早上好。抱歉来得这么早。希望没有打扰到您。"
她彬彬有礼，几乎到了冷淡的地步，莱斯利只能惊讶地看着她。前一晚写在她脸上的所有痛苦和恐惧的痕迹都消失了——只剩下眼底那一抹暗青色。
"不坐坐吗？吃过早饭了吗？"莱斯利务实地问道。
雷瑟姆夫人摇了摇头。
"请不必为我费心；我有的是时间，可以等，"她说。
她目光中带着某种不情愿的钦佩；她在想，她认识的女人里，有几个能在这种时辰、这种状态下还拿得出手。她以前从未在白天见过莱斯利·莫恩，而莱斯利不仅经受住了刺眼晨光的考验，甚至比晚上更漂亮。她欣赏这女孩的沉稳，也欣赏她爽快地接受这个合理建议，消失在浴室里，身后跟着那个笨手笨脚、怀里抱着一堆衣服的女仆。等莱斯利出来时，卢克丽霞·布朗已经摆好了一张小桌子；巨大的蓝色咖啡杯，瓷杯架上插满了酥脆的棕色烤面包片。
"不了，我吃不下，谢谢。"雷瑟姆夫人摇摇头。"来杯咖啡就好。"
莱斯利意味深长地看了一眼门口，卢克丽霞遗憾地退了出去。

---

===Original===
"Yes, I slept," said Jane Raytham listlessly. "I don't know how or why, but I did. I suppose I just couldn't sleep any more. There is nothing about the murder in the newspapers."
Leslie made a mental calculation.
"There wouldn't be; it will be in the evening press. I know all about Druze."
"You know﻿—about her?" Jane Raytham looked at her steadily.
"What was her name?" asked Leslie, but the other woman shook her head.
"I don't know; she was always Druze to me."
"Did your husband know﻿—"
"That she was a woman?" She shook her head. "No. Poor Raytham! He'd have had a fit! But then, he never notices anything."

===Chinese===
"是的，我睡着了，"简·雷瑟姆无精打采地说。"我不知道怎么睡着的，也不知道为什么，但我确实睡着了。我大概是实在撑不住了。报纸上没有关于谋杀案的报道。"
莱斯利在心里算了算。
"不会有；晚报才会登。我全都知道了——关于德鲁兹。"
"你知道——关于她？"简·雷瑟姆定定地看着她。
"她叫什么名字？"莱斯利问，但对方摇了摇头。
"我不知道；我一直叫她德鲁兹。"
"你丈夫知道吗——"
"她是个女人？"她摇摇头。"不知道。可怜的雷瑟姆！他要是知道了非得犯心脏病不可！不过话说回来，他什么都没注意到。"

---

===Original===
She had married the first Baron Raytham when he was a little over fifty, bachelor-minded, a man of set habits, who had found himself most unexpectedly a Benedict and was a little aghast at the discovery. For the greater part of a year he had striven to be the model husband, and had been something of a bore. The domestic habit was foreign to him. Society and all its dainty etceteras he loathed. Before the end of the first year of their married life, he had given up all attempt to interest himself in the new complexities which marriage had brought. Thereafter he devoted his energies and thoughts to his concession; his boards of directors, balance-sheets, and all the precious things which were life to him, and Jane Raytham was left very much to her own devices.
"My husband is very seldom in London﻿—probably not two months a year. He has"﻿—she hesitated﻿—"other interests."
Very wisely, Leslie did not pursue the subject. She, too, had heard that Lord Raytham had carried into married life a loose string or two that were substantially attached and which he was unwilling or unable to drop. Leslie was too versed in the ways of the world to be shocked at this; too sophisticated to be anything but mildly amused at the inefficiency of man, who finds it so easy to get rid of a wife and so difficult to discharge a mistress.

===Chinese===
她在第一位雷瑟姆男爵（Baron Raytham）五十出头时嫁给了他。他是个心思不定的单身汉，习惯固定的生活方式，却意外地发现自己竟然结了婚，对此有点惊惶。整整大半年，他努力做个模范丈夫，结果颇为令人厌烦。家庭生活对他来说是陌生的。社交场及所有精致的繁文缛节他都厌恶。结婚不到一年，他就放弃了对婚姻带来的一切新复杂事物的关注。此后他把精力和心思都投入到他的特许经营权上；他的董事会、资产负债表，以及所有对他而言就是生命的东西，简·雷瑟姆就这样被丢在一边，自生自灭。
"我丈夫很少在伦敦——大概一年待不上两个月。他有"——她犹豫了一下——"别的兴趣。"
莱斯利非常明智地没有追问。她也听说雷瑟姆勋爵带进婚姻的那几根松散的线实际上还牢牢系着，他既不愿也不能斩断。莱斯利深谙世故，对此不会大惊小怪；她太老练了，对男人这种无能只能淡淡一笑——他们发现甩掉妻子如此容易，遣散情妇却如此困难。

---

===Original===
"Your name is Leslie, isn't it?" And, when she nodded: "I wonder if you would mind my 'Leslie-ing' you? You're not so formidable as I thought. I﻿—I rather like you. My name is Jane﻿—if you ever feel friendly enough to 'Jane' me﻿—I've been abominably rude to you, but now I've come to ask you for favours."
Leslie laughed.
"I owe you an apology," she said and the other woman was quick to see her meaning.
"About Druze? It would be a beastly idea, only﻿—women are such queer fools, aren't they? You can hardly pick up a Sunday newspaper that specializes in such matters without having proof of these strange﻿—mésalliances. No, I knew Druze was a woman; that made everything so hideous. It besmirched me, made me cringe when I thought of it﻿—I wonder if you will believe me when I say that that was almost my heaviest cross﻿—almost."
"What was really the heaviest?" asked Leslie quietly.
Lady Raytham fetched a long sigh and looked out of the window.
"I don't know﻿—it is rather difficult to compare these things." And then, quickly: "Of course, I know now which is the heaviest, but that is so new and so crushing that I dare not let myself think about it. Something Druze said to me before she went out; something she told me that froze my blood." She closed her eyes and shuddered, but recovered instantly. "That is why I got my car and went in search of her. She told me a little but not everything, and I had to know! My first thought was﻿—you'll think I'm a hypocrite﻿—that Peter had killed her. If I thought at all! I don't think I cared. I had only one idea in my mind, to find something she had boasted about."
"Not the necklace?"
Jane Raytham smiled contemptuously.
"The necklace! As if I cared for that. I'm making a clean breast of everything﻿—up to a point. The necklace you saw at the house last night﻿—"
"Was a copy; I know that," said Leslie quietly. "An exact replica of the real emerald chain, and valueless! When you didn't bother to put it back in the safe, I guessed."

===Chinese===
"你的名字是莱斯利，对吗？"看到她点头，又说："你介意我直呼你莱斯利吗？你没有我想象的那么可怕。我——我还挺喜欢你的。我叫简——如果你觉得够亲近了，也可以叫我简——我之前对你非常失礼，但现在我是来求你帮忙的。"
莱斯利笑了。
"我欠你一个道歉，"她说。对方立刻明白了她的意思。
"关于德鲁兹？那想法真是糟透了，不过——女人就是这么奇怪的傻瓜，不是吗？你随便拿起一份专登这类事情的周日报纸，都能看到这些奇怪的——门不当户不对的关系。不，我知道德鲁兹是个女人；这让一切变得如此丑恶。它玷污了我，一想到它我就浑身不自在——我不知道你信不信，但这几乎是我最沉重的包袱——几乎是。"
"最沉重的包袱究竟是什么？"莱斯利平静地问。
雷瑟姆夫人长长叹了口气，望向窗外。
"我不知道——这些东西很难比较。"接着又很快说："当然，我现在知道哪件事最沉重了，但那太新、太沉重了，我不敢让自己去想。德鲁兹出门前跟我说了一些话；她告诉我的一件事让我血液都凝固了。"她闭上眼睛，打了个寒颤，但随即恢复过来。"所以我才开车出去找她。她告诉了我一点，但不是全部，而我必须知道！我第一个念头是——你会觉得我虚伪——是彼得杀了她。我到底有没有想清楚？我觉得我根本不在乎。我脑子里只有一个念头，就是找到她吹嘘过的那样东西。"
"不是那条项链吧？"
简·雷瑟姆轻蔑地笑了。
"项链！好像我在乎似的。我把一切都坦白了——到目前为止。你昨晚在我家看到的那条项链——"
"是复制品；我知道，"莱斯利平静地说。"是真正的绿宝石项链的完美仿品，一文不值！当你懒得把它放回保险柜时，我就猜到了。"

---

===Original===
Eye met eye, each striving to read the other's thoughts.
"What else did you guess?" asked Jane Raytham, after a long silence, and then: "No, no, don't tell me. I want to feel that nobody knows that﻿—nobody! You will tell me that I am trying to create a fool's paradise myself, and I'm a moral coward﻿—I wonder if I am?" And then, obliquely: "Have you seen Peter?"
"I saw him last night, yes. He knew nothing about the murder﻿—not so much as you," said Leslie.
The woman ignored this challenge.
"I wonder how much you do know, Leslie?"
It was a strain to ask the question. Even as she had her reservations, so also had Leslie Maughan. The truth must come from Jane Raytham or not be truth at all.
"I know you were being blackmailed; that the necklace you gave was part of the price; the twenty thousand pounds, which I imagine was all you could raise in cash, was the other part. I guess also that Druze was a blackmailer. Am I right?"
Jane nodded; there was a perceptible brightening of her face as though, fearing to hear worse, she was experiencing relief at the limitation of the girl's knowledge.
"How long have you been paying?"
