Her face, pinched and thin and translucent, had a beauty which made the girl catch her breath. She raised two big eyes to survey the visitor, and then the long lashes fell on her cheeks.

"Your tea," she said.

===Chinese===
"你看上去像只打瞌睡的猫咪，"他笑了笑。就在这时，门口出现了一个小小的身影。

很难判断她多大，莱斯利猜她六岁，虽然在这个年纪算高个子。她瘦得让人心疼，两只小胳膊小心翼翼地端着一杯茶，细得几乎只剩骨头裹着皮。她的脸瘦削苍白，近乎半透明，却有一种令人心头一震的美。她抬起一双大眼睛打量了一下访客，然后长长的睫毛垂落在面颊上。

"你的茶，"她说。

===Original===
Leslie took the cup gently from the child's hand and set it down.

"What's your name?" she asked, and as she put her hand on the yellow head, the little creature shrank back, her face puckered with fear.

"That's Belinda," said Peter, with a smile.

The child wore a ragged old mackintosh over a nightgown that had once been of red flannelette, but which had washed to the palest of pinks. Her hands, lightly clasped before her, were almost transparent.

"I'm Mrs. Inglethorne's little girl," she said in a low voice. "My name is 'Lizabeth—not Belinda."

===Chinese===
莱斯利轻轻从孩子手中接过杯子，放在一旁。

"你叫什么名字？"她问道，伸手去摸那头黄头发时，小女孩却缩了回去，小脸因恐惧皱成一团。

"她叫贝琳达（Belinda），"彼得微笑着说。

孩子身上罩着一件破旧的旧式防水外套，里面是一件曾经是红色法兰绒的睡裙，洗得只剩下淡淡的粉白。她的双手轻轻交叠在身前，几乎透明。

"我是英格尔索恩太太的女儿，"她轻声说，"我叫丽兹（'Lizabeth）——不叫贝琳达。"

===Original===
She raised her eyes quickly to the man and dropped them again. The gravity of her tone, the low sweetness of her voice, amazed Leslie Maughan. For a second she forgot that she was too tired to be interested even in the bizarre.

"Won't you come and talk to me?"

The child glanced at the door.

"Mother wants me—"

"Talk to the lady!"

===Chinese===
她飞快地抬眼看了看那个男人，又垂下目光。她语气的郑重，嗓音低沉的甜美，令莱斯利·莫恩暗暗吃惊。有那么一瞬间，她忘了自己累得连对稀奇事都提不起兴趣来。

"你不愿意过来跟我说说话吗？"

小女孩瞟了一眼门口。

"妈妈要我——"

"跟这位女士说话！"

===Original===
Evidently Mrs. Inglethorne, at the bottom of the stairs, had good ears. The child started, looked apprehensively round, and came sidling towards Leslie.

"What do you do with yourself?" asked Leslie. "Do you go to school?"

Elizabeth nodded.

"I think about daddy most of the time."

Leslie remembered that daddy was at that moment serving his country in Dartmoor.

===Chinese===
楼下的英格尔索恩太太显然耳朵很好使。孩子吓了一跳，惶恐地四下看了看，然后怯怯地挪向莱斯利。

"你平时做什么？"莱斯利问，"上学吗？"

伊丽莎白点点头。

"我大部分时间都在想爸爸。"

莱斯利想起，"爸爸"此刻正在达特穆尔（Dartmoor）监狱服刑。

===Original===
"I keep him in a book; he's very nice—ever so nice—"

The child nodded soberly.

"In a book?" asked Leslie. "What kind of a book?"

A voice outside the door supplied an answer. Mrs. Inglethorne must have crept up the stairs the better to listen.

"Don't take no notice of her, miss; she's a bit cracked. Any good-looking feller she sees in a book she says is her father. Why, she used to take the King once, and then Lord What's-his-name, and when I think of her own poor dear father, that worked his fingers to the bone and got a 'stretch' for nothing, as innocent as a babe unborn, it's very hard."

===Chinese===
"我把他夹在书里；他很帅——特别帅——"

孩子一本正经地点点头。

"夹在书里？"莱斯利问，"什么书？"

门外的声音替她回答了。英格尔索恩太太想必是蹑手蹑脚上了楼来偷听的。

"小姐，您别理她，她脑子有点问题。书里但凡看到个长得好看的小伙子，她就说那是她爹。哼，她以前还把国王当她爹呢，后来又是什么勋爵大人，想想她自个儿那可怜的亲爹，拼死拼活干到手指头都快断了，结果蹲了号子——天大的冤枉，清清白白跟刚出生的娃儿似的——真叫人心寒啊。"

===Original===
Elizabeth was tense now; her big eyes narrowed, her ear turned to the door. It was an attitude of apprehension, and Leslie's heart ached for the child. She smoothed her hair, and this time the little girl did not shrink.

"I'll send you some wonderful pictures, and you'll be able to make up fathers and uncles and all sorts of nice things from them."

Stooping, she kissed the child, and with her arm about her painfully thin shoulders, led her to the door. On the landing the unhealthy-looking Mrs. Inglethorne smirked and squirmed, a picture of gratitude for the lady's condescement.

"I'm going to be very interested in Elizabeth," said Leslie, her steady eyes on the woman. "You won't mind if I come round sometimes to see how she is getting on?"

===Chinese===
伊丽莎白此刻绷紧了身子，大眼睛眯了起来，耳朵朝着门口的方向。这是一种警觉的、戒备的姿态，莱斯利看着心疼。她抚了抚孩子的头发，这一次小女孩没有退缩。

"我给你寄些漂亮的图画来，你就能照着它们画出爸爸、叔叔，还有各种好东西。"

她弯下腰亲了亲孩子，搂着她瘦得硌人的肩膀，把她送到门口。楼梯平台上，气色不佳的英格尔索恩太太满脸堆笑、浑身不自在地扭着身子，那副感激涕零的样子活像一幅画——感恩贵人屈尊降贵的画。

"我以后会常来看伊丽莎白，"莱斯利说，目光平静地看着那个女人，"你不介意我偶尔来转转，看看她过得怎么样吧？"

===Original===
Mrs. Inglethorne made a fearful grimace which was intended to express her pleasure.

"How many children have you?"

"Five, miss."

The woman was looking at her curiously, possibly fascinated by her first meeting with the female of the hated species.

"Five in this little house?" Leslie raised her eyebrows. "Where do you keep them all?"

===Chinese===
英格尔索恩太太挤出一个可怕的笑容，大概是想表示高兴。

"你有几个孩子？"

"五个，小姐。"

女人好奇地打量着她，大概是第一次跟这个讨厌物种的雌性打交道，看得入了迷。

"这么小的房子住了五个？"莱斯利扬起眉毛，"你把他们都塞在哪儿？"

===Original===
Again the woman wriggled, this time uncomfortably.

"In the kitchen, miss, except the two girls; they sleep in my room."

"I'd like to have a look at your kitchen."

"It's a bit late, and you'd wake 'em up," said Mrs. Inglethorne after hesitation.

But Leslie waited, and reluctantly Mrs. Inglethorne went down the stairs, the girl following. The kitchen was at the back of the house, approached by a narrow passage. It was a room barely ten feet square, cold and miserably furnished. In the unsatisfactory light of the oil-lamp the woman carried, she saw not three, but four, little bundles; one, a child which could not have been three years old, slept in a soap box on the floor. Its coverlet was a strip of dusty carpet, which had been roughly cut to fit the shape of the box. Two children were huddled together under the table, wrapped in an old army overcoat. The fourth lay in a corner under a flour sack, so still that she might have been dead; a girl of eleven, sandy-haired, sharp-featured, who shivered and groaned in her sleep as the light of the lamp came upon her face.

===Chinese===
那女人又扭了扭身子，这次带着几分不自在。

"在厨房里，小姐，除了两个闺女；她们跟我睡。"

"我想看看你的厨房。"

"时间有点晚了，您会把他们吵醒的，"英格尔索恩太太犹豫了一下才说。

但莱斯利执意要等，英格尔索恩太太只好不情不愿地走下楼去，莱斯利跟在后面。厨房在房子后面，要经过一条窄走廊才能到。这间屋子不到十英尺见方，冰冷而寒酸，家具破烂不堪。借着女人端着的油灯那微弱的光，莱斯利看到的不是一个，而是四个小包袱：一个不满三岁的孩子睡在地上的肥皂箱里，盖着一条灰扑扑的旧地毯，是粗粗裁成箱子的形状铺上去的。两个孩子挤在桌子底下，裹着一件旧军大衣。第四个蜷在角落的面粉袋下面，一动不动，像死了一样——是个十一岁的女孩，沙色头发，面容尖刻，灯光照到她脸上时，她在睡梦中发着抖，呻吟着。

===Original===
"It's very 'ard on a woman who's got five mouths to fill," whined Mrs. Inglethorne; "but I wouldn't part with 'em for the world. And it's warm in the kitchen when we've had a coke fire going all evening."

Leslie went out of that sad little room sick at heart. Poverty she had seen and understood. Possibly these unfortunate children were as well off as thousands of others in the great metropolis. The weaklings would die; the fittest survive and presently drag their stunted bodies to a free school, where they would be taught just enough to enable them to write their betting slips and read the football reports intelligently.

Peter was waiting for her at the foot of the stairs.

"I think I'll go home now. I'm rather tired," she said. "Most likely you will be interrogated tomorrow either by Mr. Coldwell or an officer from the Yard. I think the best thing you can do is to go up and interview him."

===Chinese===
"要养五张嘴的女人苦着呢，"英格尔索恩太太呜呜咽咽地说，"可让我扔下他们，说什么也不干。而且晚上生了焦炭炉子，厨房暖和着呢。"

莱斯利从那间悲惨的小屋里走出来，心如刀绞。贫穷，她见过，也理解。也许这些不幸的孩子在这个大都市里已经算好的了。体弱的会死掉；最强壮的能活下来，然后拖着发育不良的身子去上免费学校，学校教的东西刚好够他们填下注单和看懂足球赛果。

彼得在楼梯脚下等她。

"我想我该回家了。我实在累了，"她说，"明天科尔德威尔先生（Mr. Coldwell）或者苏格兰场的人大概会来找你问话。我觉得你现在最好的选择就是上去见他。"

===Original===
And then, abruptly:

"Have you seen your mother since you have been free?"

He shook his head.

"My parent has expressed her wishes on the matter in unmistakable terms. We were never en rapport, so to speak, and perhaps it is a little too late now to attempt to arrive at mutual understanding."

She looked down at the floor, her lips pursed.

"I wonder," she said, and held out her hand. "Good night, Peter Dawlish."

===Chinese===
然后她突然问道：

"你出来之后见过你母亲吗？"

他摇了摇头。

"我的母亲大人已经用毫不含糊的措辞表达了她的意思。打个比方，我们从未有过默契，现在也许已经过了挽回的时候了。"

她低头看着地面，嘴唇微微抿着。

"那可未必，"她说，伸出手来，"晚安，彼得·道利什。"

===Original===
He took her hand, held it for a second, and then:

"You're rather wonderful. I'm getting a new angle on life," he said.

She had one more call to make. Inspector Coldwell had promised to wait at Scotland Yard until she returned with her report, and she found him sipping coffee in the lobby, and told him briefly the result of her visit.

"I never thought Peter knew much about it. What does he know of Druze?"

He listened intently until she had finished.

"Rum! All the paths in this maze lead back to the Princess Bellini. Yes, I'll see Peter; I'll wire him in the morning," he yawned. "It is time all honest people were in bed. I'm going to take you home."

===Chinese===
他握住她的手，停了一瞬，然后说：

"你真的很了不起。我对生活有了新的认识，"他说。

她还有一站要去。科尔德威尔探长（Inspector Coldwell）答应在苏格兰场等她汇报回来，她在大厅里找到了他，正小口啜着咖啡。她简要向他报告了此行的结果。

"我从不觉得彼得知道多少内情。他对德鲁兹了解多少？"

他专注地听她讲完。

"古怪！这迷宫里的每一条路都通回贝里尼公主那里。好吧，我会见彼得；明天给他发封电报，"他打了个哈欠，"正经人都该睡觉了。我送你回家。"

===Original===
Her cab was waiting, and though she had no need of an escort, he pointed out that her way was largely his.

"What we're going to do about Lady Raytham, I don't know. I'm taking it for granted that you have discovered a whole lot that you haven't told me."

"Not a whole lot, a little," she admitted.

Mr. Coldwell scratched his head.

"That little is usually crucial. However, I am not going to discourage you. Keep your mystery—a little romance in police work has a wonderful tonic value."

===Chinese===
她的出租车在等着。虽然她不需要护送，他还是指出他们的路大半是同路。

"雷瑟姆夫人的事我们怎么处理，我还没想好。我猜你已经发现了许多还没告诉我的事情。"

"不算多，一点点，"她承认。

科尔德威尔先生挠了挠头。

"这'一点点'往往至关重要。不过我不会泼你冷水。保持你的神秘感——警察工作里掺点浪漫，提神醒脑。"

===Original===
The cab carried them across deserted Trafalgar Square, and a few seconds later stopped before the door of Leslie's flat.

"I suppose you know all that is to be known about the case?" he said, with a touch of the sardonic, as he handed her out of the cab. "Whilst I, a poor old muddleheaded copper, am groping round like a blindfolded man in a fog."

"I think I know a lot," she admitted, with a tired smile.

Coldwell was amused.

"The complacency of the woman! Here she is, keeping all her clues up her sleeve, ready to spring them out and reduce police headquarters to a bewildered pulp! Know all about Druze, do you?"

"I know a lot about him."

"Fine!" said Coldwell.

===Chinese===
出租车驶过空寂的特拉法加广场（Trafalgar Square），几秒钟后停在了莱斯利公寓的门口。

"我猜你对这个案子已经了如指掌了吧？"他把她在车外接出来时，语带几分讥诮，"而我呢，一个老糊涂的笨警察，像蒙着眼在雾里摸路一样。"

"我觉得我知道得不少，"她带着疲倦的微笑承认。

科尔德威尔被逗乐了。

"这女人多自信！藏着掖着所有线索，就等着一下子全摊出来，把警察总部搅成一团浆糊！你很了解德鲁兹？"

"我对他了解很多。"

"好极了！"科尔德威尔说。

===Original===
She had the door open now, and he waited until she was in the passage before he dropped his bombshell.

"Promise me you won't come out and ask questions, but will go straight up to bed, if I tell you something?"

"I promise," she said.

He put his hand on the knob of the door, ready to shut it.

"Arthur, or Anthony, Druze, as he was variously called, was a woman!"

The door slammed on her; before she recovered from her stupor she heard the rattle of the cab as it moved away.

===Chinese===
她已经开了门，他等着她走进走廊，然后才投下了炸弹。

"我告诉你一件事，你保证不追出来问，直接上楼睡觉？"

"我保证，"她说。

他把手放在门把上，准备关门。

"那个叫亚瑟（Arthur）或安东尼（Anthony）的德鲁兹——反正他用过各种名字——是个女人！"

门在她面前砰地关上；她还没从震惊中回过神来，就听到了出租车驶离时嘎嘎的声响。

## X / 第十章

===Original===
"Druze was illiterate, but, like all illiterate people, had acquired a certain form of culture and was very clever to conceal this misfortune. I think, in fact I know, she had the schooling of an average child, but she was just incapable of learning﻿—the Council Schools and even the Public Schools are full of people like that, of girls and boys familiar with the most obscure sciences who have never tasted these elementary arts."
Leslie thought quickly.
"Her signature was on the passport?"
"I wrote it," said the surprising woman. "She told me she wanted to go across to France for a weekend trip and asked me if I would sign the passport form. That was only a few weeks ago, so it is fresh in my mind. Now tell me what I am to do? The police will come to me, and I am prepared to tell them the truth, though I cannot see how I can help them."

===Chinese===
"德鲁兹不识字，但像所有不识字的人一样，她学到了某种形式的文化修养，并且非常巧妙地掩盖了这一不幸。我认为——事实上我确定——她受过普通孩子的教育，但她就是学不进去——公立学校甚至公学里到处都是这样的人，那些精通最深奥学科的男男女女，却从未触碰过这些基础的读写技艺。"
莱斯利飞快地思索着。
"她的签名在护照上？"
"是我代签的，"这个令人惊讶的女人说。"她告诉我她想去法国度个周末，问我能不能帮她在护照表格上签名。那是几周前的事了，所以我记忆犹新。现在告诉我该怎么办？警察会来找我，我准备告诉他们真相，虽然我不确定自己能帮上什么忙。"

---

===Original===
"The whole truth?" asked Leslie significantly.
Jane Raytham looked at the girl for a long time before she answered.
"As much as I've told you﻿—not as much as you guess," she said in her even voice.
Leslie carried her cup of coffee to the desk.
"Would you like me to write down the gist of what you have said, and sign the statement?" she asked. "That may save you an awful lot of trouble."
Jane hesitated.
"Is it necessary? I suppose it is," she said. "Yes, if you would be so kind."

===Chinese===
"全部真相？"莱斯利意味深长地问。
简·雷瑟姆（Jane Raytham）看了女孩好一会儿才回答。
"和我告诉你的那些一样多——不会比你猜的更多，"她用平稳的声调说。
莱斯利端着咖啡杯走到书桌前。
"你愿意让我把你所说的内容要点写下来，然后签字确认吗？"她问。"这也许能省去你很多麻烦。"
简犹豫了一下。
"有必要吗？我想是的，"她说。"好的，如果你愿意费心的话。"

---

===Original===
For ten minutes she watched the girl as her pen flew over the paper, and took the pages from her as they were written.
"You have put my case more cleverly than I could have put it myself," she said with a little smile. "I almost think you're sympathetic."
"You don't know how sympathetic I am," said Leslie, rising from her chair to make way for the other.
Lady Raytham sat down, read the last sheet again, and had dipped her pen in the ink, when the sound of voices came from outside the door. It was Lucretia's raised protest, and a deeper voice, which Leslie instantly recognized, and, running to the door, threw it open. The Princess Anita Bellini stood on the landing, glaring through her monocle at the defiant Lucretia.

===Chinese===
她看着女孩笔尖飞快地在纸上划过，一页页写完便递给她。
"你把我的事写得比我本人所能表达的还精妙，"她带着一丝微笑说。"我几乎觉得你是个心地善良的人。"
"你不知道我有多善良，"莱斯利说着，从椅子上起身给对方让座。
雷瑟姆夫人坐下来，重新读了最后一页，刚把笔蘸进墨水瓶，门外便传来了说话声。那是卢克丽霞（Lucretia）提高了嗓门的抗议，还有一个更低沉的声音，莱斯利立刻就认出来了。她跑到门口一把推开门。安妮塔·贝里尼公主（Princess Anita Bellini）站在楼梯平台上，透过单片眼镜怒视着不肯让步的卢克丽霞。

---

===Original===
"You can't come in; Miss Maughan's engaged," she was saying. "I don't care if you're a princess or if you're the Queen of Sheba. When Miss Maughan's engaged nobody can﻿—"
"That will do, Lucretia. Come in, Princess."
The big woman strode into the apartment without a word of thanks, not even deigning to look at the defiant maid.
"Where is﻿—?" she began, and then she saw Lady Raytham at the desk. "What are you writing, Jane?" she demanded loudly. "You're not being such a fool as to make a statement to the police, are you?"

===Chinese===
"你不能进来；莫恩小姐有客人，"她正在说。"我不管你是什么公主还是什么示巴女王。莫恩小姐有客人的时候谁也不能——"
"够了，卢克丽霞。请进，公主。"
那个高大的女人连一声谢谢也没说就大步走进了公寓，甚至不屑看一眼那个不肯让步的女佣。
"人在——"她开口，随即看见了书桌前的雷瑟姆夫人。"你在写什么，简？"她大声质问道。"你不会蠢到要给警察写什么供述吧？"

---

===Original===
"Lady Raytham is merely telling me as much as I already know," said Leslie.
"Jane, you must not sign it. I forbid it!"
There was a tremor of anger in the hard voice, and, looking at the woman, Leslie saw how deeply the tragedy must have affected her. She seemed ten years older. The big slit of a mouth was downturned at the ends, the eyes red and inflamed.
Very calmly Lady Raytham affixed her signature.
"Don't be foolish, Anita," she said quietly. "The police are entitled to know certain things about Druze."

===Chinese===
"雷瑟姆夫人只是在告诉我一些我已经知道的事情，"莱斯利说。
"简，你绝不能签字。我不准！"
那严厉的声音中带着一丝颤抖的愤怒。莱斯利看着这个女人，看得出这场悲剧对她的打击有多深。她仿佛老了十岁。那张大嘴的嘴角向下耷拉着，眼睛红肿充血。
雷瑟姆夫人非常平静地签上了自己的名字。
"别犯傻了，安妮塔，"她轻声说。"警察有权知道关于德鲁兹的一些事情。"

---

===Original===
"What have you told them? Can I see this precious document?"
She reached out her hand, but Leslie was before her.
"Let me read it to you, Princess," she said, and placed the desk between herself and her furious visitor. That Princess Bellini was in a cold tremble of rage was patent.
She read without interruption to the end.
"Jane Raytham, you're a fool to sign a thing like that!" stormed the woman. "Let them find things out without committing yourself to paper. This girl has tricked you into a confession﻿—"

===Chinese===
"你告诉他们什么了？我能看看这份宝贝文件吗？"
她伸出手，但莱斯利抢先一步。
"让我读给你听吧，公主，"她说着，把书桌横在了自己和暴怒的访客之间。贝里尼公主气得浑身发冷地颤抖，这是一目了然的。
莱斯利一口气读到了最后。
"简·雷瑟姆，你签这种东西简直是犯蠢！"那女人怒吼道。"让他们自己去查好了，何必白纸黑字地给自己留把柄。这个女孩骗你写了认罪书——"

---

===Original===
"Confession?" said Leslie, with a smile. "How absurd! Lady Raytham knew that Druze was a woman; it was impossible that she should not. And, as she says, she has only told us what we already knew. And what you already knew."
"I knew nothing," said Anita Bellini harshly, her baleful eyes fixed on the girl. "Except that you have tricked Lady Raytham into making a statement which will involve her in considerable trouble."
Leslie faced her squarely, and for the first time Anita Bellini became dimly and uncomfortably conscious of the strength of this inconsiderable person. They had met before, and the honours of that meeting had not rested with the Princess. But she had thought of Leslie as a girl with a certain glibness of tongue, a gift of smart repartee, but without any of the especial qualities that she might expect in a foe worthy of her heaviest metal. But now it had dawned upon her that, whether she was "Coldwell's pretty typist," as she had contemptuously referred to her, or whether she was "a Scotland Yard underling," she was certainly a factor to be considered and forestalled. And if she had any doubt on the subject, Leslie Maughan's first words would have dispelled it.

===Chinese===
"认罪书？"莱斯利微微一笑。"多荒谬啊！雷瑟姆夫人知道德鲁兹是个女人；她不可能不知道。而且，正如她所说，她告诉我们的只是我们已经知道的——也是你早就知道的。"
"我什么都不知道，"安妮塔·贝里尼冷酷地说，恶毒的目光死死盯着那个女孩。"只知道你骗雷瑟姆夫人写了一份供述，会让她惹上大麻烦。"
莱斯利正面迎上她的目光。安妮塔·贝里尼第一次隐约而不安地意识到这个不起眼的人物的力量。她们以前见过面，那次交锋中占上风的并不是公主。但她此前一直认为莱斯利不过是个嘴皮子利索、善于机智应答的女孩，并不具备一个值得她全力以赴的对手所应有的特殊品质。但现在她渐渐明白，不管莱斯利是她曾轻蔑地称之为"科尔德威尔（Coldwell）的漂亮打字员"也好，还是"苏格兰场的下级探员"也好，她绝对是一个需要认真对待并抢先防范的角色。如果她对此还有什么疑虑，莱斯利·莫恩的第一句话就足以打消它。

---

===Original===
"Lady Raytham has made a statement, and you also will make a statement, Princess," she said, "either before or after the inquest."
The woman surveyed her with an oddly sly look that was unnatural in her.
"I don't know how you can bring me in," she began, and her tone was milder than it had been.
"You employed Druze. Apparently you knew she was a woman, and are acquainted with her early history," said Leslie quietly. "That is quite sufficient to bring you into any inquiry which the police set afoot."
Anita Bellini took out her monocle, polished it on her handkerchief, and returned it to her eye.

===Chinese===
"雷瑟姆夫人已经写了供述，你也要写一份，公主，"她说，"无论是在死因审理之前还是之后。"
那女人用一种古怪的狡黠目光审视着她，这种神态在她身上极不自然。
"我不知道你怎么把我扯进来，"她开口说，语气比之前温和了些。
"你雇用了德鲁兹。显然你知道她是个女人，而且了解她的早期经历，"莱斯利平静地说。"这完全足以让警方的任何调查都把你牵涉进来。"
安妮塔·贝里尼取出单片眼镜，用手帕擦了擦，重新戴上。

---

===Original===
"Possibly I was rather precipitate," she said. "But I think you should make allowance for my﻿—whatever I have said. I have been awfully upset by Druze's death. Would you read the statement again?"
It was a very simple record of the information which Lady Raytham had given to the girl, and, when she had finished reading:
"No, there is nothing in that," said the Princess. "I suppose this evidence has to come out. Does it mean that we shall be called at the inquest? I couldn't stand that, I couldn't!"
In that instant Leslie detected a tremor in the woman's voice. Anita Bellini, the formidable, had a weak spot, after all. But she recovered herself very quickly.

===Chinese===
"也许我有些冲动了，"她说。"但我想你应该体谅我——不管我说了什么。德鲁兹的死让我非常难过。你能再把那份供述读一遍吗？"
那份供述忠实地记录了雷瑟姆夫人告诉女孩的全部信息。读完之后：
"不，这里面没什么，"公主说。"我想这些证据总归要公开的。这是否意味着我们会在死因审理时被传唤？我受不了，我受不了！"
那一瞬间，莱斯利捕捉到了那女人声音中的一丝颤抖。安妮塔·贝里尼，这个令人畏惧的女人，毕竟也有弱点。但她很快就恢复了过来。

---

===Original===
"If everybody had his due, Peter Dawlish would be under arrest," she said, and, ignoring the protests of Jane: "The man hated Druze; you know that quite well, Jane. He threatened her; I can prove it!" And then, in a conciliatory tone: "I hope we're not going to be bad friends, Miss Maughan. If I can help you I will. Is there any more you can tell me than appears in the evening newspaper?"
"Nothing," said Leslie shortly.
They left together soon after, but before they departed, Leslie found an opportunity of speaking a few words to Jane Raytham.

===Chinese===
"如果每个人都得到应有的报应，彼得·道利什早该被逮捕了，"她说，然后不顾简的反对继续说道："那家伙恨德鲁兹；你很清楚这一点，简。他威胁过她；我能证明！"接着，她换上一种和解的口吻说："莫恩小姐，我希望我们不会成为冤家。如果我能帮你，我会的。晚报上的内容之外，你还能告诉我更多吗？"
"没什么，"莱斯利简短地说。
她们不久后一起离开了，但走之前，莱斯利找到机会和简·雷瑟姆说了几句话。

---

===Original===
"I don't want you to tell anybody about the necklace," she said in a low voice, as she accompanied her down the stairs. "Especially about the emerald that was found in Druze's hand. You promise me? Or have you already told?"
Jane Raytham shook her head.
"I wondered why you hadn't put that in the statement," she suggested. "But you may trust me; I shall not speak about it, even to Anita."
At that moment the voice of the Princess hailed her from the foot of the stairs, and further conversation became impossible.

===Chinese===
"关于那条项链的事，我不希望你告诉任何人，"她陪她下楼时低声说。"尤其是关于在德鲁兹手中发现的那颗绿宝石。你能答应我吗？还是你已经说了？"
简·雷瑟姆摇了摇头。
"我正纳闷你为什么没把这件事写进供述里，"她说。"但你可以相信我；我不会说出去的，即使对安妮塔也不会。"
这时公主的声音从楼梯底下传来，更多的谈话已不可能。

---

===Original===
Leslie arrived at Scotland Yard just before twelve, and was mounting the stone stairs as Peter Dawlish came down.
"A clean bill," he said with a smile. "At any rate, that is the impression Coldwell gives me. It seems that your detective man's search was a very thorough one. I suppose you know that he searched me also? And, by the way, Belinda sends her love."
"Belinda?" Leslie was momentarily bewildered. "Oh, you mean the little child, Elizabeth. How wicked! I had almost forgotten her!"
"She hasn't forgotten you," laughed Peter, and with a cheery wave of his hand went on.

===Chinese===
莱斯利在将近十二点时抵达苏格兰场，正走上石阶，彼得·道利什迎面下来。
"清白证明，"他笑着说。"至少，科尔德威尔给我的印象是这样。看来你的那位侦探搜查得相当彻底。想必你知道他也搜了我？顺便说一句，贝琳达（Belinda）向你问好。"
"贝琳达？"莱斯利一时困惑。"哦，你是说那个小女孩，伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）。太过分了！我差点把她忘了！"
"她可没忘了你，"彼得笑着说，然后愉快地挥了挥手，继续走他的路。

---

===Original===
She found Mr. Coldwell in his big, comfortable office, the stub of a cigar between his teeth, his bristling brows gathered in thought.
"Just going to phone you," he grumbled. "I've seen that man of yours, and I'm satisfied that he had nothing to do with the crime."
" 'That man of mine' being Peter Dawlish?" she said calmly. "You give me quite a proprietorial feeling."
From her bag she took the statement that Lady Raytham had signed and laid it on the table before him. He read it through carefully, folded it up, and slipped it into a drawer.

===Chinese===
她在科尔德威尔宽敞舒适的办公室里找到了他，嘴里叼着半截雪茄，浓密的眉头紧锁，陷入沉思。
"正要给你打电话，"他嘟囔着。"我见过你的那位了，确信他与这案子毫无关系。"
"我的那位——是指彼得·道利什？"她平静地说。"你这话说得好像他是我的什么人似的。"
她从包里取出雷瑟姆夫人签过字的供述，放在他面前的桌子上。他仔细读了一遍，折好，塞进抽屉。

---

===Original===
"Did you tell Anita Bellini about the emerald we found in Druze's hand?" he asked.
"No," she said. "That's the last thing in the world I should have told her. I asked Lady Raytham not to tell either. Why?"
He smiled grimly.
"Thought you hadn't," he said. "Her Serenity called me on the phone five minutes ago, and said she'd read in one of the newspapers that something very valuable had been found on Druze's person. I haven't seen all the newspapers, but those I've read make no mention of the emerald, and I don't see why they should, unless they are psychic. The Princess suggested, rather than said, that you had confirmed this mythical newspaper report."

===Chinese===
"你有没有告诉安妮塔·贝里尼我们在德鲁兹手里发现的那颗绿宝石的事？"他问。
"没有，"她说。"全世界最不可能告诉的就是她。我还请雷瑟姆夫人也不要透露。怎么了？"
他冷冷一笑。
"我想你也不会，"他说。"五分钟前那位殿下来电话了，说她在某份报纸上读到在德鲁兹身上发现了非常贵重的东西。我还没看完所有的报纸，但已经看过的几份都只字未提绿宝石，我也不明白为什么会提——除非它们有通灵能力。公主与其说是直接问，不如说是暗示你已经证实了这份虚构的报纸报道。"
莱斯利佩服地摇了摇头。

---

===Original===
"That woman is certainly a quick worker," she said. "What did you tell her?"
Mr. Coldwell relit his cigar with the exasperating deliberation of his age.
"I told her that we *had* found something valuable﻿—a packet of money. She seemed kind of disappointed."
The telephone bell shrilled; he picked up the receiver, listened in silence for a time, and then:
"All right, I'll come down," he said.
"The Lambeth police have got a quaint clue; a kind of ready-made one, but it should be investigated, as it has to do with your Peter. Would you like to come along?"
She looked at him steadily.
"If you refer to him as my Peter again, I shall be very offensive to you, Mr. Coldwell," she said, and Coldwell scratched his chin.

===Chinese===
"那女人动作真够快的，"她说。"你怎么回答她的？"
科尔德威尔以他那个年纪特有的令人恼火的从容重新点燃了雪茄。
"我告诉她我们确实发现了一些贵重的东西——一包钱。她好像有点失望。"
电话铃刺耳地响了起来；他拿起听筒，沉默地听了一会儿，然后说：
"好的，我这就过去。"
"兰贝斯（Lambeth）警方拿到了一条古怪的线索；有点像是现成的，但应该查一查，因为它牵涉到你的彼得。你想一起去吗？"
她定定地看着他。
"如果你再把他叫做'我的彼得'，我会对你很不客气的，科尔德威尔先生，"她说。科尔德威尔挠了挠下巴。

---

===Original===
"Somehow he seems to belong to you; I don't know why I get that impression."
Her eyes wandered to a corner of the room, and for the first time she saw the two big travelling trunks. They were new and bore the label of the Cunard Steamship Company.
"Druze's," he said laconically. "We'll go through those when we come back."

===Chinese===
"不知怎么的，他好像属于你；我说不清为什么会有这种感觉。"
她的目光飘向房间一角，第一次看到了那两个大旅行箱。箱子是新的，贴着冠达航运公司（Cunard Steamship Company）的标签。
"德鲁兹的，"他简短地说。"回来以后再检查。"

---

===Original===
It was at the corner of Severall Street that the taxicab stopped. The local sub-divisional inspector was waiting, and with him a detective.
"Let me have a look at that paper," said Coldwell immediately, and Leslie, who had not heard the one-sided conversation on the telephone, wondered what was coming next.
The inspector took a dirty slip of paper from his pocketbook and gave it into Coldwell's hand. He fixed his glasses and read, then passed the slip to the girl. The message was written in pencil and in an illiterate hand.

===Chinese===
出租车在塞弗拉尔街（Severall Street）的拐角处停下。当地分区警长已经在等着了，旁边还有一名侦探。
"让我看看那张纸，"科尔德威尔立刻说道。莱斯利没有听到电话里那段单方面的对话，不知道接下来会发生什么。
警长从笔记本里取出一张脏兮兮的纸条递到科尔德威尔手中。他戴上眼镜读了一遍，然后把纸条递给女孩。纸条上的字是用铅笔写的，笔迹拙劣不堪。

---

===Original===
> "Dawlish keeps his gun under a loose board in his bedroom just as you go inside the door."
"Where did this come from?" asked Coldwell.
"It was delivered at the station just before I telephoned to you. A street lad brought it along; he said it had been handed to him by a man, who gave him a few coppers for his trouble. I thought it best that you should know."
They walked down the street towards Mrs. Inglethorne's house, and the door was opened immediately by that lady, who was surprisingly clean and spruce. She seemed surprised, but was certainly not agitated by the appearance of the police officers.
"Yes, sir, Mr. Dawlish has just come in. Shall I call him down?"

===Chinese===
> "道利什把他的枪藏在卧室门后一块松动的地板下面。"
"这东西哪来的？"科尔德威尔问。
"就在我给你打电话之前送到警局的。一个街上的小厮送来的；他说是一个男人交给他的，给了他几个铜板当跑腿费。我觉得最好让你知道。"
他们沿着街道走向英格尔索恩夫人（Mrs. Inglethorne）的住处，门立刻就被那位太太打开了——她出人意料地干净利落。她似乎有些惊讶，但见到警官们并没有慌张。
"是的，先生，道利什先生刚进来。要我叫他下来吗？"

---

===Original===
"No, thank you; we'll go up."
Coldwell mounted the stairs and knocked at the door of the front room, and a voice bade them come in. Over the inspector's shoulder, Leslie saw that Peter was sitting at a deal table, pen in hand, a stack of addressed envelopes before him. He shifted his chair round, and his eyebrows rose in astonishment.
"Hullo!" he said, obviously taken aback by the character of the call. "Do you want to see me again, inspector?"
Coldwell took in the room with a glance.
"I have information that you've a gun concealed under this floor," he said. "If you don't mind, I'll make another search."
"Fire ahead," said Peter, without a moment's hesitation.

===Chinese===
"不了，谢谢；我们上去。"
科尔德威尔走上楼梯，敲了敲前屋的门，里面有个声音让他们进来。从警长身后望去，莱斯利看到彼得坐在一张松木桌旁，手里拿着笔，面前是一摞写好地址的信封。他把椅子转过来，惊讶地扬起了眉毛。
"嘿！"他说，显然对这次登门的性质感到意外。"又要见我，警长？"
科尔德威尔扫了一眼房间。
"我得到情报，你在这块地板下面藏了一把枪，"他说。"如果你不介意，我要再搜一次。"
"请便，"彼得毫不犹豫地说。

---

===Original===
Coldwell turned back to the door, lifted a corner of the faded carpet, and saw the loose board immediately. To lift it up was the work of a second. Thrusting in his hand, he pulled out a long black Browning pistol. Peter's face went white; his jaw dropped in an amazement that could not have been simulated.
"Anything more here?" asked Coldwell, and, kneeling, thrust in his hand and groped about. Presently he found a small package wrapped in cloth and brought it to the light. He unwrapped it slowly.
"My Gawd!" gasped a hollow voice.
Mrs. Inglethorne had crept up the stairs and was an interested spectator. And if her profanity was inexcusable, there was reason enough for her astonishment, for in the centre of that dirty rag lay three large diamond rings, the least valuable of which must have been worth a hundred pounds.

===Chinese===
科尔德威尔转身走向门口，掀起褪色地毯的一角，立刻看到了那块松动的地板。掀开它只用了一秒钟。他把手伸进去，抽出了一把长长的黑色勃朗宁手枪。彼得的脸刷地变白了；他的下巴惊得几乎脱臼，那种惊愕不可能是装出来的。
"还有别的吗？"科尔德威尔问道，跪下来把手伸进去摸索。不一会儿他找到了一个小布包，拿到灯光下。他慢慢拆开。
"我的天哪！"一个空洞的声音倒吸了一口凉气。
英格尔索恩夫人已经蹑手蹑脚地上了楼梯，饶有兴致地当起了旁观者。如果说她脱口而出的脏话情有可原，那她震惊也是有充分理由的，因为那块肮脏的破布中央躺着三枚硕大的钻石戒指，最不值钱的一枚也至少值一百英镑。

---

===Original===
"Do you know anything about these, Dawlish?"
Peter shook his head.
"No; I'm not a burglar," he said, with a return of his old good spirits. "That branch of the profession is not my forte﻿—and that little find has every appearance of being the proceeds of a very old burglary."
Coldwell looked at the wrapper; it was thick with dust. Even as he turned back one corner of the rag a fine cloud arose.
"Do you know anything about these, Mrs. Inglethorne?"
She shook her head.
"Or the pistol?"
The woman was paralyzed; her face had gone a ghastly grey as she realized the enormous significance of that find. There they had lain, month after month, at least £500 worth of jewellery, the results of one of her lodger's little coups, and she none the wiser.

===Chinese===
"你知道这些东西吗，道利什？"
彼得摇了摇头。
"不知道；我又不是入室盗窃犯，"他说，恢复了往日的好心情。"那一行不是我的专长——而且这些小玩意儿明显是很久以前某次入室盗窃的赃物。"
科尔德威尔看了看包装布；上面蒙了厚厚一层灰。他刚翻开破布的一角，就扬起了一小片尘雾。
"你知道这些东西吗，英格尔索恩夫人？"
她摇了摇头。
"手枪呢？"
这女人愣住了；当她意识到这批赃物的重大意义时，脸色变得惨白灰暗。它们在那里躺了一个又一个月，价值至少五百英镑的珠宝，是她的某个房客某次小手笔的成果，而她竟浑然不知。

---

===Original===
"Never﻿—seen﻿—it!" Mrs. Inglethorne found a difficulty in breathing.
"This has been used as a hiding-place before," said the inspector, as he laid the pistol and rings upon the deal table.
He examined the Browning, noted its make and number, and, having carefully removed the magazine and dislodged the cartridges from the chamber, smelt at the barrel.
"It has been fired recently, I should imagine; it still smells of cordite. Is this yours, Dawlish?"
"No, sir; I've never seen it before."
"Humph!" The inspector sat down on the bed in exactly the place where the girl had sat the night before. He looked round for Mrs. Inglethorne, but that untidy woman had vanished.
"Nobody told you about that hiding-place?"
"No, sir﻿—"

===Chinese===
"从没——见过——它！"英格尔索恩夫人发现自己呼吸都困难了。
"这地方以前就有人拿来当藏匿处，"警长说着，把手枪和戒指放到了松木桌上。
他检查了勃朗宁手枪，记下了型号和编号，小心翼翼地卸下弹匣、把枪膛里的子弹退出来，然后嗅了嗅枪管。
"我推测这枪最近开过；还能闻到火棉的气息。这是你的吗，道利什？"
"不是，长官；我以前从没见过它。"
"哼！"警长在床沿坐下来，正好是前一晚女孩坐的那个位置。他环顾四周找英格尔索恩夫人，但那个邋遢的女人已经不见了。
"没人告诉过你那个藏匿点？"
"没有，长官——"

---

===Original===
"Hullo, Elizabeth!" It was Leslie's interruption. The frail child stood in the doorway, shyly smiling at the beautiful lady of her dreams.
She whispered something that the girl could not catch, and Leslie went nearer to her, took the two thin hands in hers, and, stooping, kissed the pale cheeks.
"Tea?" she said with a laugh. "No, dear, I don't think we want tea. It was very nice of you to come﻿—"
The child's eyes were fixed on the table; they were wide open, and in their depths Leslie saw a look of fear.
"What is it?" asked Leslie.
"That big gun," whispered the child. "Mother had it this morning, and I was so frightened."

===Chinese===
"嘿，伊丽莎白！"是莱斯利打断了谈话。那个瘦弱的孩子站在门口，腼腆地对这位梦中的美丽女士微笑着。
她低声说了些什么，莱斯利没听清，便走近了些，握住那双纤细的小手，弯下腰亲了亲那苍白的脸颊。
"喝茶？"她笑了笑说。"不了，亲爱的，我想我们不需要茶。你来真是太好了——"
孩子的目光盯着桌子；眼睛睁得大大的，莱斯利在那双眼睛深处看到了恐惧。
"怎么了？"莱斯利问。
"那把大枪，"孩子低声说。"妈妈今天早上拿了它，我好害怕。"

---

===Original===
The sharp-eared Coldwell heard.
"Your mother had it this morning, my dear?" he said kindly. "Where did she have it?"
"In the kitchen. A gentleman left it﻿—a little gentleman with a yellow face. Mother brought it into the kitchen and said we all ought to be killed."
She clapped her hands to her mouth with an exclamation of fright, for only then did she remember the strict injunctions laid upon her. Coldwell strode out of the room to the head of the stairs and called Mrs. Inglethorne in a stentorian voice. It was a long time before he had an answer, and then, by the tremulous voice, he guessed that part of the conversation between himself and the child had been overheard.
"Come up here," he said curtly, and Mrs. Inglethorne came lumbering up the stairs.

===Chinese===
耳尖的科尔德威尔听到了。
"你妈妈今天早上拿了它，亲爱的？"他和蔼地问。"她放在哪儿了？"
"在厨房里。一个先生留下的——一个黄脸的小个子先生。妈妈把它拿到厨房里，说我们都该被杀掉。"
她惊叫一声捂住了嘴，因为她这才想起了那些严厉的叮嘱。科尔德威尔大步走出房间来到楼梯口，用洪亮的嗓音喊英格尔索恩夫人。过了好一会儿才得到回应，从那颤抖的声音来看，他猜到自己和孩子的部分谈话被人偷听到了。
"上来，"他厉声说道。英格尔索恩夫人磨磨蹭蹭地上了楼。

---

===Original===
"This pistol came to your house this morning﻿—from whom?"
The woman's mouth was dry with terror. She blinked from one to the other.
"A gentleman brought it," she gulped. "He said it belonged to Mr. Dawlish﻿—and would I put it under the floor﻿—without a word of a lie, sir, if I never move from here."
Coldwell's gimlet eyes searched her unwholesome face.
"You told me you had never seen the pistol before. Who sent it?"
She shook her head.
"I don't know, sir. I've never seen the man before in my life, if I never move﻿—"
"You'll move!" said Coldwell grimly. "And darned quick, if you don't tell me the truth!"
But to her story she stuck, swearing by numerous gods, some of whom were unfamiliar to Leslie, that she knew nothing whatever of the pistol except that it had been brought there by a perfect stranger who she thought was a friend of Peter Dawlish.

===Chinese===
"这把枪是今天早上送到你家的——谁送的？"
这女人吓得嘴唇发干。她的目光在众人之间来回闪烁。
"一个先生送来的，"她吞了口唾沫说。"他说这是道利什先生的东西——让我把它放到地板下面——我发誓千真万确，长官，骗你我不是人。"
科尔德威尔锐利的目光审视着她那张令人不快的脸。
"你之前告诉我你从没见过这把枪。是谁送的？"
她摇了摇头。
"我不知道，长官。我这辈子从没见过那个人，骗你我不是——"
"你会付出代价的！"科尔德威尔冷冷地说。"而且会很快，如果你不跟我说实话！"
但她一口咬定自己的说法，以无数神灵的名义起誓——其中有些名字莱斯利闻所未闻——说自己对这把枪一无所知，只知道是一个素不相识的人送来的，她以为那是彼得·道利什的朋友。

---

===Original===
To Leslie Maughan's astonishment, the inspector appeared to accept this story, and to find nothing venal in the act of concealment.
"You did a very foolish thing, Mrs. Inglethorne. The next time a perfect stranger comes and asks you to conceal firearms in your lodger's room, you had better notify the police."
He slipped the pistol into his pocket, and looked round for Elizabeth, but she had vanished.
"That lets you out, Dawlish," he said. "At least, it does for the moment. If I were you, I would make an inspection of the room and see if there are other likely hiding-places where stuff could be planted."
He had a consultation with the local inspector, and then he and Leslie walked back to their cab.

===Chinese===
令莱斯利·莫恩惊讶的是，警长似乎接受了这套说法，没有在藏匿行为中发现什么值得追究的动机。
"你做了一件非常愚蠢的事，英格尔索恩夫人。下次有个素不相识的人来让你把枪藏在房客房间里，你最好通知警察。"
他把手枪塞进口袋，环顾四周找伊丽莎白，但她已经不见了。
"道利什，这下你脱身了，"他说。"至少暂时如此。换了是我，我会检查一下房间，看看还有没有其他可能被栽赃的藏匿点。"
他和当地警长商议了一番，然后和莱斯利一起走回出租车。

---

===Original===
"You've let her down rather lightly, haven't you, Mr. Coldwell?"
He gave her a quick sidelong glance.
"Minnow fishing never did appeal to me," he drawled. "Especially while one of the big pikes are hovering around, and it's the pike I'm after. And if this minnow doesn't lead me to him, I'll be astonished."
"You accept Peter Dawlish's story?"
He nodded, as he handed her into the cab. When he had followed and had slammed the rackety door and the machine was in motion, he explained.

===Chinese===
"你对她从轻发落了，科尔德威尔先生？"
他飞快地侧目看了她一眼。
"钓小鱼从来不是我的兴趣，"他慢条斯理地说。"尤其是当一条大梭子鱼在附近游弋的时候，而那才是我想要的。如果这条小鱼不能把我引向他，我倒要奇怪了。"
"你接受彼得·道利什的说法？"
他点了点头，扶她上了出租车。他随后跟上来，砰地关上吱呀作响的车门，车子开动之后，他才开始解释。

---

===Original===
"The detective who searched the house last night found that loose board and the hole underneath. He might have missed the diamond rings, but he couldn't have missed the gun. Therefore, I knew it had been planted since. Peter might have put it there, of course, but the odds were all against that theory. The true story was the one told by the child. The little yellow-faced gentleman was probably one of the three who attacked Dawlish last night."
For the first time she learnt of that surprising outrage which had been committed in Severall Street the night Peter had visited her.
He admitted a little irritably that the case had gone outside his own experience.

===Chinese===
"昨晚搜查房子的那个侦探发现了那块松动的地板和下面的洞。他也许错过了钻石戒指，但不可能错过那把枪。所以我知道枪是后来才放进去的。当然，有可能是彼得放的，但所有迹象都不支持这个说法。真实的情况是那个孩子所说的。那个黄脸的小个子先生很可能就是昨晚袭击道利什的三个人之一。"
她第一次听说了彼得来找她那天晚上在塞弗拉尔街发生的那起令人震惊的暴行。
他有些烦躁地承认，这个案子已经超出了他的经验范围。

---

===Original===
"Here's a woman who has been masquerading as a man for the past fifteen years, found dead, with an emerald in her hand, worth, at a rough guess, a thousand pounds. She was shot at close quarters with the pistol I have in my pocket﻿—"
She gasped.
"You don't mean that?"
He nodded.
"I do mean it. I'd like to bet a month's pay that I'm right. You think a murderer would be crazy to put the very weapon in the hands of the police, knowing that the pistol has a number and its purchase can be traced﻿—unless it was bought in Belgium, which is extremely likely. You haven't seen Druze since she was found, have you? Well, I'm not advising you to﻿—all the details about her can be passed on. There's a big black-powder burn at the base of her right thumb﻿—that is to say, on the back of her hand. First thing I noticed when I examined the body was that powder burn."

===Chinese===
"一个女扮男装十五年的人，被发现死了，手里攥着一颗绿宝石，粗略估计值一千英镑。她是被我口袋里这把枪近距离射杀的——"
她倒吸了一口凉气。
"你是认真的？"
他点了点头。
"我是认真的。我愿意拿一个月的薪水打赌。你觉得一个凶手把凶器交给警察是疯了吗？要知道手枪上有编号，购买记录可以追溯——除非是在比利时买的，这种可能性非常大。德鲁兹被发现之后你还没见过她吧？好吧，我不是建议你去——关于她的所有细节都可以转告。她的右手拇指根部——也就是说手背上——有一个大片火药灼伤的痕迹。我检查尸体时注意到的第一件事就是那个灼伤。"

---

===Original===
"How did that come there?" asked Leslie.
"She fired an automatic﻿—five or six shots in rapid succession﻿—and got the backfire. One shot wouldn't have burnt her; it must have been at least five. Look!" He showed his own hand, and a raw red mark, faintly tinged with black. "I was firing an automatic this morning to see what would happen, and I've got exactly the same mark as she has. I'm only making a guess, Leslie, but my guess is that Miss or Mrs. Druze was killed in self-defence; that she started the gunplay and got the worst of it."
Leslie caught her breath.
"Then where is the other body?" she asked quickly.
He stared at her open-mouthed.
"Other body?"

===Chinese===
"那是怎么弄的？"莱斯利问。
"她开了一把自动手枪——连续快速射击了五六发——被回火灼伤了。一发不会灼伤她；至少要五发才会。看！"他亮出自己的手，上面有一道鲜红的伤痕，微微泛黑。"今天早上我打了一把自动手枪想看看会怎样，结果得到了和她一模一样的痕迹。我只是在猜测，莱斯利，但我的猜测是，德鲁兹小姐或者德鲁兹太太是死于正当防卫；是她先开的枪，结果反遭其害。"
莱斯利屏住了呼吸。
"那另一具尸体呢？"她急切地问。
他张大了嘴瞪着她。
"另一具尸体？"

---

===Original===
"She killed somebody first," said Leslie quickly. "Killed or desperately wounded. Such a woman as Druze would not carry a pistol unless she knew how to use it. If she knew how to use it and fired first, then somebody was badly hurt."
The old man took off his hat and scratched his head.
"That's the natural conclusion to reach," he said; "and I didn't reach it. And why I didn't reach it I don't know. Just let me think this out, will ye?"
The silence was unbroken until they reached Scotland Yard.
"I'm still thinking it out," he said dismally as he stepped out of the cab behind her and paid the taximan.

===Chinese===
"她先杀了一个人，"莱斯利迅速说。"杀了或者重伤了。德鲁兹那样的女人不会随身带枪，除非她会用。如果她会用枪而且先开了火，那肯定有人受了重伤。"
老人摘下帽子挠了挠头。
"这是顺理成章的结论，"他说；"而我竟然没有得出来。为什么没得出来，我自己也不知道。让我好好想想，行吗？"
一路沉默，直到抵达苏格兰场。
"我还在想这个问题，"他跟着她下车付钱时沮丧地说。

---

===Original===
There was a bearded man in the hall, doctor written in every line of him. He was talking to the officer at the desk and evidently Coldwell was being pointed out to him, for he walked to the door to meet the inspector as he entered.
"You're Mr. Coldwell? My name is Simmson. I am Dr. Simmson, of Marylebone Road."
"Yes, doctor?" said Coldwell, politely attentive.
"A friend of mine has suggested that I should go to Scotland Yard and report rather a curious circumstance," he said awkwardly. "I have never done such a thing before, and I'm a little at sea as to how I should begin. But I have a patient who is suffering from a gunshot wound, and I am not quite satisfied as to how she received her injury, which is a slight one"﻿—Coldwell was all attention now﻿—"through the calf; no artery has been injured. And really, I feel I'm being terribly disloyal to a patient﻿—"

===Chinese===
大厅里有一个蓄着胡须的男人，浑身上下透着医生的气质。他正在和值勤的警员说话，显然有人向他指认了科尔德威尔，因为他朝门口走来迎接进来的警长。
"您是科尔德威尔先生？我叫辛普森（Simmson）。我是马里波恩路（Marylebone Road）的辛普森医生。"
"是的，医生？"科尔德威尔礼貌而专注地应道。
"我的一个朋友建议我到苏格兰场来报告一件相当蹊跷的事，"他有些尴尬地说。"我以前从没做过这种事，也不太清楚该怎么开口。但是我的一位病人受了枪伤，我对她受伤的经过不太满意——伤势不重"——科尔德威尔现在全神贯注了——"子弹穿过小腿，没有伤到动脉。真的，我觉得我对病人非常不忠——"

---

===Original===
"What is her name?" asked Coldwell.
"Mrs. Greta Gurden," was the reply.

===Chinese===
"她叫什么名字？"科尔德威尔问。
"格蕾塔·格登太太（Mrs. Greta Gurden），"对方回答道。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
Greta Gurden occupied an apartment on the first floor of a house in Portman Crescent. Hers was one of those artistic little flats that reflected every taste but her own. She slept in a red lacquer bed, ornamented by golden devils, a bargain acquired many years before in the Caledonian Market, and renovated by her own hands. Life is rather a tragedy for the lonely woman; there was a shadowy husband very much in the background, but he had either run away from her or was in a lunatic asylum or something equally unsatisfactory. She was one of the thousands who were endeavouring to keep a ten establishment on a seven and a half income. By profession she was a journalist; edited a mildly scurrilous little paper called *Mayfair Gossip*, which enjoyed a very limited circulation, and in truth took up very little of her time. It was certainly not in the paper's interest that she fostered the delusion that her life was one of hectic gaiety. For she was to be seen occasionally at the most exclusive night clubs; more frequently at less exclusive establishments of the same order, her visitations being governed entirely by her wealth and the taste of her escort. And numerically she had many friends. Her expansiveness and lack of reticence, which had been tersely and uncharitably condensed into the vulgar word "gush," however it might sicken the more sophisticated, was very pleasant to those who discovered from her for the first time how important or good-looking or well-dressed they were, what taste, discrimination, or tact they displayed upon every conceivable occasion, and how anxiously or impatiently Greta was looking forward to their next meeting.

===Chinese===
格蕾塔·格登（Greta Gurden）在波特曼新月街一栋房子的二楼租了一间公寓。她住的是那种充满"艺术气息"的小套房，里头堆满了各种风格的摆设，唯独没有她自己的品味。她睡的是一张红漆大床，上面装饰着金色的魔鬼，这是多年前在喀里多尼亚市场（Caledonian Market）淘到的便宜货，后来她亲手翻新过。对于一个独居的女人来说，生活与其说是一出喜剧，不如说是一场悲剧——她有一个若隐若现的丈夫，但那个人要么是弃她而去，要么是住进了疯人院，总之是下落不明、令人失望。她是那类试图用七个半的收入维持十成排场的万千女人之一。她的职业是记者；编辑一份叫《梅菲尔八卦》（*Mayfair Gossip*）的小报，措辞略带刻薄，发行量极为有限，实际上也占不了她多少时间。她蓄意营造一种生活狂欢热闹的假象，这对报纸本身并无好处。因为偶尔能在最顶级的夜总会看到她的身影；更多时候出入的是同一类但门槛稍低的场所，光顾什么级别的地方完全取决于她的钱包和陪同者的品味。她的朋友在数量上相当可观。她的热情和不拘言辞——被简洁而刻薄地浓缩为一个粗俗的字眼"话痨"——无论多么令老练世故的人反胃，对那些初次从她嘴里得知自己是多么重要、多么英俊、多么衣着得体，以及在每一个可以想见的场合都多么有品位、有鉴赏力、有分寸的人而言，却是极其受用的。格蕾塔总是表现出多么急切或不耐烦地期待着与他们的下一次会面。

===Original===
There were young men who took her out to dinner or to supper or to dances; and there were middle-aged men, fathers of families, whose hearts she fluttered with the promise of adventure never to be fulfilled, who escorted her to the less expensive places of popular amusement. There were, too, women who hovered everlastingly on that no-man's-land between Suburbia and Mayfair, who courted her society and influence, under the mistaken impression that she had the entrée to the most select circles.

*Mayfair Gossip* was entirely the property of Anita Bellini, and it was an unprofitable concern, a fact Anita never failed to emphasize when she called on a Friday for her weekly stipend, her only regular source of income. The Princess was good in other ways: she gave her an occasional dinner, a discarded dress or two, marched her off to afternoon concerts, and employed her as a sort of unpaid secretary. Occasionally, windfalls came the way of Greta Gurden: fifty pounds here and there for some little service which she had rendered. And she had always a use for the money—new curtains to buy, a fascinating Chinese cabinet, or something that looked like a fascinating Chinese cabinet, a carved ivory Madonna, a fair copy of a master's art. She had a passion for picking up entirely valueless articles, and her dining-room was cluttered up with imitation oak, Birmingham-made suits of chain armour, Benares brass from the same enterprising city, a gutted spinet that served only as a sideboard for the display of imitation Bristol ware—there was even a pair of antlers over the doorway, and Greta was not above suggesting to her awestricken visitors that the twelve-pointer had been shot by her when she was the guest of the Duke of Blank at his little hunting-box in Invernessshire.

===Chinese===
有年轻男人带她出去吃晚餐、宵夜或跳舞；也有中年男人——为人父者——她用从未兑现的冒险承诺搅得他们心旌摇曳，领着她去那些不太昂贵的大众娱乐场所。还有那些永远徘徊在郊区与梅菲尔之间的无人地带的女人，她们巴结着她的社交圈子和影响力，错误地以为她能出入最高端的上流圈层。

《梅菲尔八卦》完全属于安妮塔·贝里尼（Anita Bellini），而且是一桩不赚钱的买卖——安妮塔每周五来领取她的固定津贴（这是她唯一稳定的收入来源）时，从不忘记强调这一点。公主在其他方面倒还算大方：偶尔请她吃顿饭，送她一两件淘汰的裙子，带她去听下午的音乐会，还雇她当某种不拿薪水的秘书。偶尔，格蕾塔·格登也能撞上些意外之财：这儿五十镑、那儿五十镑，都是为某个小忙所得的报酬。而这笔钱总能派上用场——买新窗帘，一件令人着迷的中国柜子，或者一件看起来像中国柜子的东西，一尊象牙雕刻的圣母像，一幅名家画作的临摹品。她热衷于搜集完全不值钱的东西，餐厅里堆满了仿橡木家具、伯明翰制造的链甲套装、同一家雄心勃勃的工厂出品的贝拿勒斯铜器、一架被掏空了琴弦只充当餐具柜展示仿布里斯托尔瓷器的古键琴——甚至门口上方还挂着一对鹿角，格蕾塔不介意向她那些目瞪口呆的访客暗示，那头十二叉角鹿是她在因弗内斯郡（Invernessshire）某位公爵的小猎屋做客时亲手猎获的。

===Original===
She enjoyed the services of one who was charlady in the morning and maid in the afternoon, and only to this unemotional lady was the real Greta ever revealed.

Mrs. Gurden lay in bed with a bandaged leg, and between terror that memory brought, fear of blood-poisoning and its horrible consequences (amongst other duties she contributed the health notes to *Mayfair Gossip*), anger at the inaction which her wound imposed, and irritation with the world in general, she was a difficult patient.

Greta could not afford to neglect her daily duty to herself. Her face was indistinguishable under a mud pack, designed to preserve the face from the ravages of age, and her hands were enclosed in complexion gloves. Two dark eyes glared oddly from the mask of grey, and she spoke with some difficulty, due to the dried earth that plastered her cheeks. Just now she had an additional reason for annoyance.

"Tell her I can't see her and I won't see her—tell her to come back at twelve o'clock."

"She's from Scotland Yard, ma'am."

"I don't care, I won't see her."

The obedient charlady disappeared into the outer room. Greta heard the murmur of voices, and after a while the woman came back.

"She says she'll wait till you're ready. She wants to know how you hurt your leg."

Greta had no need to stifle her fury: a sudden panic descended upon her.

===Chinese===
她雇了一个上午做清洁工、下午当女佣的女人。只有在这位面无表情的太太面前，真实的格蕾塔才会露出真面目。

格登太太（Mrs. Gurden）腿上缠着绷带躺在床上，在回忆带来的恐惧、对血液中毒及其可怕后果的担忧（她除了其他职责外，还负责为《梅菲尔八卦》撰写健康专栏）、对伤势迫使她卧床不起的愤怒，以及对整个世界的烦躁之间来回煎熬，是一个很难伺候的病人。

格蕾塔不能荒废她每日对自己的保养功课。她的脸被一层泥面膜覆盖得面目全非——那是用来防止岁月侵蚀的——双手则裹在美白手套里。两只黑眼睛从灰色的面具中怪异瞪视，说话也有些困难，因为脸颊上糊着干硬的泥层。此刻她又多了一个烦恼的理由。

"告诉她我不能见她，也不想见她——让她十二点再来。"

"她是从苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）来的，夫人。"

"我不在乎，我就是不见。"

顺从的清洁女工退到了外间。格蕾塔听到压低嗓音的说话声，过了一会儿，那女人回来了。

"她说她可以等到你准备好。她想知道你的腿是怎么伤的。"

格蕾塔不需要强压怒火：一阵突如其来的恐慌笼罩了她。

===Original===
"Bring me some hot water—"

It took some time to remove the renovating mud, a little longer time to substitute perfumed creams and powder. A brief glimpse through an open door had revealed to Leslie Maughan the cause of the delay. She waited patiently, being a woman having some sympathy with woman's losing fight against the ravages of time and care. When at length she was admitted, it was the old Greta who smiled ecstatically.

"My dear! How wonderfully good of you to come! So sweet of you! I was so hoping that I should have another opportunity of meeting you. The Princess is rather difficult, isn't she? I did so want to have a little chat with you the last time we met. I admire your style awfully. Won't you sit down somewhere? Yes, I've had an awful accident. I was cleaning my husband's pistol and it went off, but fortunately no bones were broken."

"Where did this happen?"

It was on the tip of Greta's tongue to say "here," but she thought better of it.

"At a country house where I was staying for the weekend. People are so careless. Imagine leaving a pistol loaded! I nearly died of fright!"

"What country house was this?" asked Leslie.

Greta knit her brows.

"What was the name of the place?—I don't know the people very well. Somewhere in Berkshire."

"Was your husband there, Mrs. Gurden?"

===Chinese===
"给我打点热水来——"

去掉那层翻新用的泥面膜花了些时间，换上香喷喷的面霜和扑粉又花了更久。莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan）通过一扇半开的门瞥见了延迟的原因。她耐心地等着——作为一个对女人与时光和操劳的抗争中注定失败这一事实怀有几分同感的女人。当她终于获准进入时，又是那个熟悉的格蕾塔在热情洋溢地笑着。

"亲爱的！你来真是太好了！太贴心了！我一直盼着能再见到你。公主有些难相处，是不是？上次见面我真想和你好好聊聊。我特别欣赏你的风格。请坐？是的，我出了个可怕的事故。我在清理我丈夫的手枪时走了火，不过万幸没有伤到骨头。"

"在哪儿发生的？"

格蕾塔差点脱口而出"这里"，但话到嘴边又咽了回去。

"在一个乡间别墅，我周末去那儿度假。人们太粗心了。想想看，居然留着一把上了膛的手枪！我差点吓死！"

"哪个乡间别墅？"莱斯利问道。

格蕾塔皱起眉头。

"那地方叫什么来着？——我和那些人不太熟。在伯克郡（Berkshire）某个地方。"

"你丈夫当时在场吗，格登太太？"

===Original===
"Er—no—but he had been staying at the place; left his box behind. I was rummaging through it and found his pistol, and it looked so awfully rusty and dirty that I thought I would clean it."

"Who else was hurt besides you?" asked Leslie quietly.

Greta shot a swift, suspicious glance at the girl.

"Nobody, thank goodness," she said.

Leslie waited a second, then:

"Was this before or after Druze was killed?"

Under the rouge Greta's face went suddenly grey and pinched. She sat bolt upright in bed and stared at the girl.

"Dead?" she said huskily. "Druze is dead? It's a lie."

"Druze is dead! She was found last night on Barnes Common—shot!"

"She?" The woman's forehead was puckered into lines. "She? What are you talking about? I was speaking of Druze."

"So was I," said Leslie. "Druze was a woman; you know that."

The open mouth, the wide eyes, every visible expression of amazement revealed without question Greta Gurden's ignorance of the "butler's" sex.

"A woman—good God!"

She sank back on the pillows, exhausted by her emotion, her eyes fixed on the ceiling. But for those wide-open pools of darkness, Leslie would have thought that the woman had fainted. Presently she spoke.

===Chinese===
"呃——没有——但他之前在那儿住过；箱子落下了。我在翻箱子的时候发现了他的手枪，看起来锈迹斑斑、脏得不行，我就想把它擦一擦。"

"除了你还有别人受伤吗？"莱斯利平静地问道。

格蕾塔飞快地、疑虑地扫了女孩一眼。

"没有，谢天谢地，"她说。

莱斯利等了一会儿，然后问道：

"这是在德鲁兹（Druze）被杀之前还是之后？"

胭脂底下的脸色骤然变得灰白、紧绷。她从床上猛地坐直，死死盯着那个女孩。

"死了？"她声音沙哑地说，"德鲁兹死了？胡说。"

"德鲁兹死了！昨晚在巴恩斯荒地（Barnes Common）发现的——被枪杀的！"

"她？"女人的额头上拧出了几道褶子，"'她'？你在说什么？我说的是德鲁兹。"

"我说的也是，"莱斯利说，"德鲁兹是女人；你知不知道。"

张大的嘴巴、瞪圆的眼睛、每一个可见的惊讶表情，都毫无疑问地暴露了格蕾塔·格登对这位"管家"性别的无知。

"女人——天哪！"

她跌回枕头上，被自己的情绪耗尽了力气，目光呆滞地盯着天花板。若非那双睁大的黑眼珠，莱斯利几乎以为她昏过去了。过了一会儿，她开口了。

===Original===
"I've nothing to tell you: I shot myself by accident. I know nothing about Druze—nothing. Why should I? The accident occurred when I was in the country. I won't talk to you—I won't."

She almost screamed the words.

Leslie realized that it would be cruel to question her more closely; the woman was so distressed that she might have hesitated even if she had not feared the effects of a further cross-examination upon one who was in the surgeon's hands.

"I will come along and see you when you're a little better, Mrs. Gurden," she said.

Greta made no answer.

As Leslie's cab turned out of the street, it passed a big Rolls swinging round to enter the unpretentious thoroughfare, and the girl had a glimpse of the Princess. How she wished now that on some pretext or other she had stayed, that she might see the meeting between these two.

Anita Bellini mounted the stairs and, entering the apartment without knocking, summarily dismissed the charwoman, and Mrs. Hobbs, not unused to such cavalier treatment, departed meekly.

"Has Maughan been here?" she demanded, as she strode into Greta's room.

Her eyes narrowed as she caught sight of the haggard face.

"I see she has," she said grimly. "What did she come about?"

===Chinese===
"我没什么可告诉你的：我是不小心走了火。我对德鲁兹一无所知——一无所知。我为什么要知道？事故发生在我在乡下的时候。我不会跟你谈——我不谈。"

她几乎是喊出来的。

莱斯利意识到再追问下去就太残忍了；这女人如此痛苦，即使不担心进一步盘问对一个尚在外科医生照料下的人可能造成的后果，她也可能会犹豫。

"等你好些了我再来看你，格登太太，"她说。

格蕾塔没有回答。

莱斯利的出租车刚拐出街道，就与一辆驶入这条不起眼小路的大型劳斯莱斯擦肩而过，女孩瞥见了公主的身影。她多么希望刚才找了个借口留下来，好看看这两个人见面时的情形。

安妮塔·贝里尼走上楼梯，不敲门就闯进了公寓，三言两语就把清洁女工打发走了。霍布斯太太（Mrs. Hobbs）对这种粗暴的待遇早已习以为常，乖乖地离开了。

"莫恩来过没有？"她大步走进格蕾塔的房间，劈头就问。

一看到那张憔悴的脸，她的眼睛就眯了起来。

"看来来过，"她冷冷地说，"她来干什么？"

===Original===
Greta raised herself on her elbow and pushed her pillow to support her; she was trembling so that after a second she rolled back on the pillow with a groan.

"She wanted to know how I was wounded," she said at last.

"What did you tell her?" asked the Princess impatiently. "For Heaven's sake, pull yourself together, my good woman! How did she know you were wounded, anyway? Did you send an announcement to the newspapers?"

"I don't know how she knew, but she did. I told her that it was an accident, that I was cleaning my husband's pistol and it went off. Anita, is it true?"

"Is what true?" asked the Princess roughly.

"Is it true that Druze is dead?"

"Yes," curtly.

"And that she was a woman?"

"I thought you guessed," said the Princess Anita. "Of course she was a woman."

"My God, how awful!"

Anita Bellini's cold glance transfixed the invalid.

"What is the matter with you?" she demanded harshly. "Druze was—"

She stopped short.

"How long are you going to be in bed?"

Greta shook her head.

"I don't know; the doctor says another week, at least."

"Did you tell her anything more? Really, Greta, you're not to be trusted—though I never dreamt that nosy little devil would find out about your being shot. I suppose the doctor reported it."

She stared down at the woman speculatively.

===Chinese===
格蕾塔用胳膊肘撑起身子，拽过枕头靠着；她浑身发抖，过了一会儿呻吟着又倒回了枕头。

"她想知道我的伤是怎么来的，"她终于说道。

"你跟她说了什么？"公主不耐烦地问，"看在老天的份上，你给我镇定点，我的好太太！她怎么知道你受伤的？难道你在报纸上登了广告？"

"我不知道她怎么知道的，但她就是知道了。我告诉她那是一场意外，我在清理丈夫的手枪时走了火。安妮塔，是真的吗？"

"什么是真的？"公主粗声问道。

"德鲁兹真的死了？"

"死了，"干脆利落。

"而且她是女人？"

"我还以为你猜到了，"安妮塔·贝里尼公主说，"她当然是女人。"

"天哪，太可怕了！"

安妮塔·贝里尼冷冷的目光射穿了病人。

"你怎么回事？"她厉声质问，"德鲁兹——"

话到嘴边戛然而止。

"你还要在床上躺多久？"

格蕾塔摇了摇头。

"不知道；医生说至少还要一个星期。"

"你还跟她说了什么？说真的，格蕾塔，你这人靠不住——虽然我万万没想到那个爱管闲事的小魔鬼会查出你挨了枪子。大概是医生上报的。"

她居高临下地打量着这个女人，若有所思。

===Original===
"I suppose I'd better give you some money," she said, with no great enthusiasm. "You look awful—you know that? You're not wearing well, Greta. All the mud in the world will not take those wrinkles from under your eyes. Why, you're old."

The red in Greta Gurden's face was natural; it came and went. Fury blazed in the dark eyes, for now Anita Bellini had touched her upon the rawest place of Greta's self-esteem, and put into words, at this incongruous moment, all that this poor little poseuse feared. But it was Anita Bellini's way, to go off at spiteful tangents, to sting and hurt those from whom she expected unswerving loyalty, and it was characteristic of her that at this moment, when her mind and spirit were tensed to meet the very real dangers which threatened her, she could go out of her way to humiliate her creature.

"You aren't able to attend to *Gossip*, of course. You're having the letters sent here?" she asked, and, when the woman nodded silently: "The last batch were valueless—there was a little bit about the Debouson woman, but I knew all about that. She isn't worth a penny; in fact, there's a bankruptcy petition out against her husband. You had better write a spicy paragraph about her—that is all the information is worth."

She was walking about the room as she spoke, stopping now and again to look, with a contemptuous lift of her lips, at the tawdriness of the imitations with which the room was stocked.

"I'm going to Capri in the spring," she said. "The new villa has been bought—I suppose I'd better take you along with me."

She did not see the malignity that shot from the dark eyes.

===Chinese===
"我最好还是给你点钱，"她说，兴致缺缺，"你看上去糟透了——你自己知道吗？你不行了，格蕾塔。全世界的泥面膜也去不掉你眼睛下面的那些皱纹。说真的，你老了。"

格蕾塔·格登脸上的红色是自然的红晕，忽来忽去。怒火在那双黑眼睛里燃烧——因为安妮塔·贝里尼此刻戳中了格蕾塔自尊中最脆弱的地方，把这个可怜的小丑角最害怕的一切在不合时宜的时刻化为了言语。但这正是安妮塔·贝里尼的作风——信口刺人，用恶毒的言辞伤害那些她期望绝对忠诚的人，而且她的典型之处在于：此刻她的心智和精神正绷紧着准备应对威胁她的真实危险，她却能腾出手来羞辱自己的仆从。

"你当然顾不上《八卦》了。你让他们把信件送到这儿来？"她问道。当女人默默点头后，她接着说："上一批没什么用——有一小段关于德布森（Debouson）夫人的，但我早就知道了一切。她一文不值；事实上，有张破产申请状正在针对她丈夫。你最好写一段关于她的花边新闻——那些信息也就值这么点。"

她一边说一边在房间里踱步，不时停下来，嘴角挂着轻蔑的笑意，打量房间里堆满的那些廉价仿制品。

"我春天要去卡普里岛（Capri），"她说，"新别墅已经买好了——我想我最好带上你。"

她没有看到从那双黑眼睛里射出的恶意。

===Original===
"The paper will have to go. It is becoming more and more useless. If you had had a spark of genius in you, Greta, you would have made that into a property. You are sure you told that detective girl nothing?"

"Nothing," said Greta, regaining control of her voice.

"What is this?"

Anita had stopped before a big secretaire, pulled down the flap, and was examining a number of letters, neatly tied in bundles.

"Are those the papers of mine that I asked you to put in order?"

"Yes."

The Princess detached one letter from a bundle, read it, and tossed it back.

"Most of these things can be burnt," she said. "You found nothing of importance?"

"No—nothing."

Something in Greta's tone made the other turn her head.

"What's the matter with you?"

And then the pent-up fury of Greta Gurden burst forth. She was sobbing with rage, almost unintelligible in her anger.

"You treat me as if I was a servant—patronizing. I hate your beastly way of talking to me! I'm not a dog. I've served you like a slave for twelve years, and I won't be talked to as you talk to me—I won't! I'd sooner starve in the gutter! I suppose I am getting old—I know I am—but you needn't throw it in my face. You're always talking about my looks. If you can't say anything nice, say nothing at all. I'm tired of it."

===Chinese===
"这份报纸也得关掉了。越来越没用。格蕾塔，你但凡有一点天才，早就能把它做成一桩赚钱的买卖了。你确定你什么都没跟那个女侦探说？"

"什么都没说，"格蕾塔说，声音重新镇定了下来。

"这是什么？"

安妮塔停在一张大写字台前，翻开盖板，审视着一叠叠捆扎整齐的信件。

"那是我让你整理的那些文件吗？"

"是。"

公主从一叠信中抽出一封，读了读，扔了回去。

"这些东西大部分可以烧掉了，"她说，"你没发现什么重要的东西？"

"没有——什么也没有。"

格蕾塔语气中的某种东西让对方转过头来。

"你怎么了？"

然后，格蕾塔·格登压抑已久的怒火爆发了。她愤怒地抽泣着，气得几乎语无伦次。

"你对待我就像对待一个佣人——居高临下。我恨你跟我说话的那副德行！我不是一条狗。我像奴隶一样伺候了你十二年，我不接受你用这种语气跟我说话——我受不了！我宁可饿死在阴沟里！我是老了——我知道我老了——但你没必要往我脸上扔。你总是拿我的容貌说事。你要是说不出什么好话，就闭嘴。我受够了。"

===Original===
"Don't be a fool!" scoffed the Princess. "And don't be hysterical. You've got your future to consider, and you're not going to help by quarrelling with me. You can't go back to the chorus."

"That's the sort of horrible thing you would say!" stormed Greta. "I think you're loathsome! I won't do another stroke of work for you—"

She ended in a passion of weak tears, and Anita Bellini did not attempt to mollify her, knowing from past experience that in an hour or two she would have a penitent message from her slave asking forgiveness for this outburst; for this was not the first time that Greta had revolted, only to come to heel at the snap of Anita's whip.

With this assurance she took her ungracious leave, and had hardly left the street before all thought of Greta was out of her mind. The Princess Anita Bellini had other matters, more weighty, to think of.

===Chinese===
"别犯傻了！"公主嗤之以鼻，"别歇斯底里了。你得考虑你的将来，跟我吵架对你没半点好处。你回不了合唱队了。"

"你就会说这种可怕的话！"格蕾塔怒吼道，"我觉得你真恶心！我不会再替你干一件事了——"

她以一阵虚弱的泪水收场，安妮塔·贝里尼并不打算安抚她——凭借过往的经验，她知道一两个小时内这个奴隶就会发来忏悔的信息，为这场爆发请求宽恕；因为格蕾塔造反已经不是第一次了，每次都在安妮塔挥鞭的啪嗒声中乖乖就范。

带着这份确信，她不客气地告辞了。一离开那条街道，关于格蕾塔的一切念头就从她脑中消散了。安妮塔·贝里尼公主还有其他更重大的事务需要考虑。

===Original===
There was very little for Leslie Maughan to tell to her chief, but he did not seem greatly disappointed.

"We'll leave her alone for a while. If you once start badgering these people, they build up an unbreakable alibi, and that's bad for trade."

He looked glumly at the trunks in the corner of his room.

"We'd better dispose of these," he said. "I'll get in a clerk to write down the inventory as you call them out."

He rang for his secretary, the girl who had taken Leslie Maughan's place on her promotion, and, stooping before the first of the cabin trunks, he unlocked it and threw back the lid. For half an hour Leslie was lifting out articles of wearing apparel, and one little mystery was solved when she came upon a parcel of men's clothing. They were of the ready-to-wear type, the parts roughly tacked. One of them, however, must have been fitted, for it was half-sewn, and a small tailor's roll in a pocket of the trunk explained how Druze had avoided the embarrassment of a tailor's fitting. She was evidently a good sewing woman, for the half-finished garment was beautifully tailored. There was nothing, however, in the first trunk that threw any light upon the mystery of her death.

===Chinese===
莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan）能向上司汇报的内容很少，但他似乎并不太失望。

"我们先放她一阵子。你一旦开始纠缠这些人，他们就会构筑一个滴水不漏的不在场证明，那对生意可不好。"

他闷闷不乐地看着房间角落里的几只箱子。

"我们最好把这些处理掉，"他说，"我叫个文员来，你报一件他记一件。"

他按铃叫来秘书——就是莱斯利·莫恩升职后接替她位置的那个女孩——然后在第一只航海箱前弯下腰，打开锁，掀起箱盖。莱斯利花了半个小时往外取出一件件衣物。当她翻到一包男装时，一个小小的谜团解开了。这些衣服都是成衣型的，粗略地缝了几针。但其中一套一定是量身定做的，因为它只缝了一半。箱底口袋里一个小裁缝卷轴解释了德鲁兹如何避开了裁缝量体的尴尬——她显然是个手艺不错的缝纫工，因为那件半成品的做工相当精细。然而第一只箱子里没有任何线索能为她的死亡之谜提供蛛丝马迹。

===Original===
The second box held a surprise; it was filled with women's clothing.

"She was going to drop her disguise when she got to the United States," Leslie concluded, and with this view Mr. Coldwell agreed.

At last the second box was emptied, but again there was nothing that could afford the slightest clue.

"There's a suitcase; we only discovered it this morning. It was in the parcels office at Waterloo," said Coldwell.

He opened a cupboard and took out a crocodile-skin travelling grip and put it on the table. It was locked, but suitcase locks respond to almost any key, and at the second attempt it was opened. Here the girl found such articles as she would expect an ocean traveller to carry: sponge-bag, soap, a small jewel-case containing a gold watch and guard, a diamond-encircled wristwatch, and a small diamond bar brooch. A silk dressing-gown, a pair of slippers, and a few odds and ends completed the contents.

"Nothing here," said Leslie.

She ran her hand round the silken lining of the case, and suddenly her fingers stopped. She felt a thin oblong package under the silk. Reaching out she took Coldwell's scissors from his desk and cut through the silk. Inserting her fingers, she drew forth an envelope. It was closed and bore no inscription. She tore off the end and drew out an oblong document. It was a marriage certificate, performed apparently by the Rev. H. Hermitz, of Elfield, Connecticut.

"Good Lord!" said the startled Coldwell, reading over her shoulder.

===Chinese===
第二只箱子令人意外——里面装满了女装。

"她打算到了美国就卸下伪装，"莱斯利推测道，科尔德威尔先生（Mr. Coldwell）也同意这个判断。

第二只箱子终于被清空了，但同样没有任何能提供哪怕最微弱线索的东西。

"还有一只手提箱；我们今天早上才发现的。寄存在滑铁卢车站（Waterloo）的行李寄存处，"科尔德威尔说。

他打开一个柜子，取出一只鳄鱼皮旅行手提包，放在桌上。手提包上了锁，但行李箱锁对几乎任何钥匙都有反应，第二次尝试就打开了。女孩在里面找到了一个远洋旅客通常会携带的物品：海绵袋、肥皂、一个小首饰盒（里面有一块带表链的金表、一块镶钻腕表和一枚小巧的钻石条形胸针）。一件丝绸晨袍、一双拖鞋和几样零碎物件构成了箱子里的全部内容。

"没什么，"莱斯利说。

她的手指沿着箱子的丝绸衬里摸过去，突然停了下来。她感觉到丝绸下面有一包薄薄的长方形物件。她伸手从科尔德威尔的办公桌上拿起剪刀，剪开丝绸。将手指伸进去，她抽出一个信封。信封没有封口，也没有任何字迹。她撕开一端，取出一份长方形文件。那是一份结婚证书，显然由康涅狄格州（Connecticut）埃尔菲尔德（Elfield）的H.赫米茨牧师（Rev. H. Hermitz）主持公证。

"天哪！"吃了一惊的科尔德威尔从她肩后探头读道。

===Original===
For a moment the words swam before the eyes of the girl, and then out of the blur they appeared with staggering clearness. It was a document certifying that Peter James Dawlish had been joined in holy matrimony with Jane Winifred Hood—and Hood was Lady Raytham's maiden name!

She read it again, then put the document into the inspector's hands.

"Then they were married," she said evenly. "That was the thing I wasn't sure about."

===Chinese===
一时间，那些文字在女孩眼前模糊成一片，然后从朦胧中以惊人的清晰度浮现出来。这是一份文件，证明彼得·詹姆斯·道利什（Peter James Dawlish）与简·威妮弗雷德·胡德（Jane Winifred Hood）在神圣的婚姻中结合——而胡德（Hood）正是雷瑟姆夫人（Lady Raytham）的娘家姓！

她又看了一遍，然后把文件交到督察手中。

"那么他们确实结过婚，"她语气平稳地说，"这正是我一直不确定的事。"

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
Peter Dawlish found it very difficult to concentrate his mind upon his work, and although his task was purely mechanical he stopped from time to time and allowed his thoughts to wander; and inevitably they wandered towards that grey building on the Thames Embankment, and a room somewhere in its dark interior where a girl was sitting. He could see her face very clearly. He sighed and took up his pen again, and cursed himself for the folly of dreams.

===Chinese===
彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish）发现很难把心思集中在工作上。尽管他的任务纯粹是机械性的，但他还是不时停下来，任凭思绪飘散。思绪不可避免地飘向泰晤士河南岸那座灰色建筑，以及建筑幽暗内部某个房间里坐着的那个姑娘。他能清晰地看见她的脸庞。他叹了口气，重新拿起笔，诅咒自己做了这些愚蠢的梦。

===Original===
Far better for him, he thought, that, if he could not concentrate upon his work, he let his mind go roving westwards to the bleak moor and those ugly prison buildings that are set in a fold of it; to the carved sneer on the stone arch under which he had walked, heavy-footed, towards the golden-bearded warder who stood by the iron gates and counted the prisoners in and out; to the long, smelly "ward," and the vault-like cells with their gaily coloured blankets; to the stretch of bog-land from which the convict workers returned soaked to the skin to their lukewarm dinners; to the barnlike laundry, the silent punishment cells, and the cracked asphalt where the prisoners walked in a ring on Sunday mornings. An ugly memory, but at least one of accomplishment, and substantially past. Much better than letting your fancies go straying towards the straight figure of a girl with violet eyes and red lips that curved everlastingly in laughter.

===Chinese===
他想，与其这样，倒不如让思绪飘向西边那片荒凉的沼泽和那些矗立在沼泽褶皱中的丑陋监狱建筑；飘向那座石拱门上雕刻的嘲讽笑容，他曾脚步沉重地穿过那道拱门，走向站在铁门旁清点囚犯进出的金胡子狱卒；飘向那条又长又臭的"牢房"，以及那些像坟墓一样的牢房，里面铺着色彩鲜艳的毯子；飘向那片沼泽地，被判苦役的工人们浑身湿透地从那里回来，吃着温吞的午餐；飘向谷仓般的洗衣房、寂静的惩罚牢房，以及囚犯们周日早晨绕圈行走的破裂柏油路。这是一段丑陋的记忆，但至少是已经完成的，基本上已成为过去。这总比让自己的幻想飘向那个身姿挺拔、紫罗兰色眼睛、红唇永远弯成笑意的姑娘要好得多。

===Original===
He had reached the S's in the list, the Simpsons and Sims and Sinclairs. It was ill-paid work, his employer being a bookmaker of dubious probity; but, so far as he was concerned, he had been paid something in advance, and he had been promised another job to follow.

===Chinese===
他已经抄到了名单里的S部分，辛普森、西姆斯和辛克莱。这是报酬微薄的工作，雇主是个信誉可疑的赌注登记员；但就他而言，已经预支了一些报酬，并且还被承诺了后续的工作。

===Original===
Very resolutely he had dismissed from his mind all thought of his mother. Even in Dartmoor he had excluded her from his thoughts. If he remembered at all, it was by the letter that had come to him on the day of his conviction. His father had died that week; he had been sinking for months, and had never been conscious of his son's shame. That had been Peter's one comfort, until he received his mother's letter, telling him that in a lucid hour of consciousness old Donald Dawlish had struck his name from his will. So Peter went down from the dock with the bitterness of death in his heart—beside that knowledge of his father's last act, the seven years' sentence was as nothing.

===Chinese===
他非常坚决地把关于母亲的所有念头从脑海中驱逐出去。即使在达特穆尔监狱，他也把她排除在自己的思绪之外。如果他真的想起了什么，那也是通过他定罪那天收到的那封信。他的父亲在那周去世了；他已经病入膏肓好几个月了，从未意识到儿子的耻辱。这是彼得唯一的安慰，直到他收到母亲的信，信中说在唐纳德·道利什（Donald Dawlish）清醒的一刻，老人把他的名字从遗嘱中划掉了。于是彼得带着死亡般的苦涩走下被告席——与父亲最后的这一行为相比，七年的刑期算不了什么。

===Original===
At six o'clock, Elizabeth brought him his tea. She was unusually solemn and silent, and when he attempted to start a little conversation with her, she was so embarrassed that he did not attempt to pursue this course.

===Chinese===
六点钟，伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）给他端来了茶。她异常严肃和沉默，当他试图与她聊几句时，她显得如此尴尬，以至于他没有继续这个话题。

===Original===
He went out for an hour, strolling through the Lambeth Cut amidst a medley of hawkers' stalls with their glaring acetylene lights. He had some comfort from this contact with his fellows. As he returned, he was opening the door of the house with a key which the woman had given him that day, when he remembered that he had not seen Mrs. Inglethorne since the visitation of the police.

===Chinese===
他出去了一个小时，在兰贝斯运河（Lambeth Cut）漫步，周围是小贩摊位混杂的刺眼乙炔灯光。与同伴们的接触给了他一些安慰。当他回来时，正用那女人当天给他的钥匙打开房门，这时他想起自从警察搜查以来，他就没见过英格尔索恩太太（Mrs. Inglethorne）。
===Original===
He went upstairs, lit the oil-lamp and, putting a paper bag full of biscuits which he had bought on the table before him, he settled down to his task. Eight o'clock was striking when he heard the squeaking of motorcar brakes as it stopped before the door, and, going to the window, he pulled aside the shade and looked down. It was too dark to distinguish the visitor, but his heart leapt at the thought that it might be Leslie Maughan, and he opened the door and waited. This time he heard Mrs. Inglethorne's voice, and after a while she called up to him sourly.

===Chinese===
他上了楼，点亮油灯，把买来的一纸袋饼干放在面前的桌子上，然后安顿下来继续工作。八点钟敲响时，他听到汽车刹车在门前停下的吱嘎声。他走到窗边，拉开窗帘向下望去。天太暗了，看不清来访者是谁，但想到可能是莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan），他的心就跳了起来。他打开门等着。这次他听到了英格尔索恩太太的声音，过了一会儿，她酸溜溜地朝他喊道。

===Original===
"A lady to see you, Mr. Dawlish."
"Will you ask her to come up, please?"
He went back into the room and waited. The step on the stairs was slower and heavier than Leslie's. And then there came through the open doorway the last woman in the world he expected to see—his mother.

===Chinese===
"有位女士要见你，道利什先生。"
"请她上来好吗？"
他回到房间等着。楼梯上的脚步声比莱斯利的更慢、更沉重。然后，从敞开的门口走进来一个他万万没想到会见到的女人——他的母亲。

===Original===
Her cold eyes went from him to the littered table.
"Fine work for the son of a gentleman!" she said in a hard voice.
"I've known worse," he replied coolly.
She closed the door behind her, as though she knew something of Mrs. Inglethorne's irrepressible curiosity.

===Chinese===
她冰冷的目光从他身上移到凌乱的桌子上。
"绅士的儿子干的好活儿！"她用冷硬的语气说道。
"我见过更糟的，"他冷冷地回答。
她随手关上门，仿佛知道英格尔索恩太太那种无法抑制的好奇心。

===Original===
"I never expected I should see you again," she said, declining with a gesture the chair he pushed forward to her; "but having given the matter a great deal of thought, I have decided that I ought to do something for you. I am buying and stocking a small farm for you in Western Canada, and I am making you a small allowance to enable you to live, even if the farm fails, as it probably will. You will leave for Quebec on Saturday week; I have booked a second-class passage for you." And, when he was about to speak: "I don't want you to thank me. I shall feel happier when you have left the country. You have brought everlasting disgrace upon your father's name, and I do not wish to be reminded constantly of the fact."

===Chinese===
"我从没想过会再见到你，"她说着，用手势拒绝了他推过来的椅子；"但经过深思熟虑，我决定应该为你做点什么。我正在加拿大西部为你购置和装备一个小农场，并且给你一小笔津贴，让你即使在农场失败后——这很可能发生——也能生活下去。你下周六动身去魁北克；我已经为你预订了二等舱。"他刚要开口说话，她又说："我不需要你感谢我。你离开这个国家后，我会感到更舒心。你给你父亲的名字带来了永恒的耻辱，我不希望不断被提醒这件事。"

===Original===
Here she stopped.
"You were altogether wrong when you thought I was about to thank you," he said quietly. "In the first place, I have no intention of accepting your charity, and in the second place, I have no aptitude for farming either in Canada or in England."
"I have booked your passage," she said, with an air of finality.
"Then there will be a vacant bed going cheap on the Atlantic Ocean!" replied Peter with a half-smile.

===Chinese===
她停了下来。
"你以为我要感谢你，那你就大错特错了，"他平静地说，"首先，我没有接受你施舍的打算；其次，无论在加拿大还是在英格兰，我都没有务农的天赋。"
"我已经给你订了船票，"她带着不容置疑的语气说。
"那大西洋上就会有一张空床位廉价出售了！"彼得带着半丝微笑回答道。

===Original===
She looked round the room contemptuously, and again her eyes went to the table.
"So you'd rather do this waster's work?"
"Waster's work, I agree," he nodded; "but infinitely more intellectual than mending boots or washing convicts' laundry—my last occupation. I expect nothing from you, mother. For some reason which I have never quite understood, you have hated me ever since I was a child. I have no wish to reproach you with being 'unnatural.' You have been under the thumb of Anita Bellini ever since I can remember."

===Chinese===
她轻蔑地环视房间，目光又落在桌子上。
"所以你宁愿干这种废物干的活儿？"
"废物干的活儿，我同意，"他点点头；"但比修靴子或洗囚犯的衣服——我上一份工作——要有智慧得多。我对你无所求，母亲。出于某种我从未完全理解的原因，你从我小时候就讨厌我。我不想指责你'不近人情'。在我记忆所及的范围内，你一直受制于安妮塔·贝里尼（Anita Bellini）。"

===Original===
"How dare you!" Her voice was vibrant with anger. "'Under the thumb!' What do you mean?"
"I only know that Anita Bellini has withered every good feeling in every good woman who has been brought into contact with her. I only know that she is evil—what hold she has over you, God knows. It has been sufficiently strong to rob me of the one gift which is every man's right—a mother's love. I dare say that sounds a piece of sickly sentimentality, but it is a big thing—a very big thing."

===Chinese===
"你怎么敢！"她的声音因愤怒而颤抖。"受制于她！你什么意思？"
"我只知道安妮塔·贝里尼摧残了每一个与她接触过的善良女人身上的每一份善意。我只知道她很邪恶——至于她对你有什么控制力，只有天知道。它强大到足以剥夺我每个人与生俱来的权利——母亲的爱。我敢说这听起来像是多愁善感的废话，但那是一件大事——一件非常大的事。"

===Original===
"You have had what you deserved," she interrupted brusquely. "And I did not come here to discuss my duty. If you prefer to go to Australia instead of Canada—"
"I prefer Lambeth to either place at the moment," he said coldly.
She shrugged her shoulders ever so slightly.
"You have made your bed and you must lie on it. I have done all that is humanly possible, more than could be expected, remembering how you have humiliated me and made my name—"
"My father's name," he corrected.

===Chinese===
"你活该如此，"她粗暴地打断了他。"我来这里不是为了讨论我的责任。如果你更愿意去澳大利亚而不是加拿大——"
"此刻我更愿意留在兰贝斯，而不是去那些地方，"他冷冷地说。
她微微耸了耸肩。
"你自作自受，咎由自取。我已经尽了人之常情所能做的一切，甚至超出了预期，想想你是怎样羞辱我、败坏我的名声——"
"是我父亲的名声，"他纠正道。

===Original===
He got under her guard there, and to his wonder the comment to which irritation drove him produced a remarkable effect. Her face flushed; the hard mouth grew harder.
"Your father's name is my name," she said harshly.
Her eyes were blazing; he had never seen her so moved.
"I will give you twenty thousand pounds to leave the country," she said. "That is my final offer."
He shook his head.
"I shall never want money from you," he said, and, walking to the door, opened it, and she left the room without another glance at him.

===Chinese===
他这一下戳中了她的痛处，令他惊讶的是，恼怒驱使他说出的这番话产生了显著的效果。她的脸涨红了；冷硬的嘴巴变得更加严厉。
"你父亲的名声就是我的名声，"她恶狠狠地说。
她的眼睛里燃烧着怒火；他从未见过她如此激动。
"我给你两万英镑让你离开这个国家，"她说。"这是我的最后条件。"
他摇了摇头。
"我永远不会向你要钱，"他说着走到门口，打开门，她头也不回地离开了房间。

===Original===
Why had she come? He wasted half an hour of precious time puzzling over this extraordinary action on her part. He had spoken no more than the truth when he had said that from his childhood she had displayed an antagonism towards him which, in maturity, had puzzled him more than any other experience in his life. Antagonistic? She hated him! And, curiously enough, his father had known of her feeling, and though he had never made any direct reference to the enmity, had gone out of his way to make up for the affection the mother denied him. It was his father with whom he had corresponded throughout the days of the war; his father who had met him when he came home from France on leave; his father who had come day after day to the hospital to sit by the bedside of his wounded son; and when Peter had been discharged from the army, it was Donald who had found him the secretaryship, and had planned for him a great career in the world of politics. It was a puzzle beyond unravelment. Peter took up his pen again and tried, by a concentration of his exigent present, to forget the bitter past.

===Chinese===
她为什么来？他浪费了半小时宝贵的时光，苦苦思索她这一反常举动。他说的没有错，从他小时候起，她就对他表现出一种敌意，成年后的他对此比生活中任何其他经历都更困惑。敌意？她恨他！而且，奇怪的是，他的父亲知道她的这种感情，尽管从未直接提及这种敌意，却总是特意弥补母亲拒绝给予他的关爱。战争期间，他一直与父亲通信；是他父亲在他从法国休假回来时去接他；是他父亲日复一日来到医院，坐在受伤儿子的病床旁；当彼得从军队退伍后，是唐纳德为他找到了秘书职位，并为他规划了在政治界大展宏图的前途。这是一个无法解开的谜。彼得再次拿起笔，试图通过专注于眼前紧迫的工作，忘掉苦涩的过去。

===Original===
It was twelve o'clock when he put down his pen and rubbed his cramped hands. Throwing up the window to let out the smoke, he munched a biscuit and meditated; and then his face brightened, and his thoughts went unresistingly towards Leslie Maughan. Then through the open window he heard unsteady steps coming along the paved sidewalk. It paused before the door of the house; there was a rattle of the key. Mrs. Inglethorne often went out at night, and as often returned with that same unsteady footstep. Presently the door slammed, and her muttering came up to him from the passage.

===Chinese===
十二点钟时，他放下笔，揉了揉酸痛的手。他推开窗户让烟散出去，一边嚼着饼干一边沉思；然后他的脸亮了起来，思绪不由自主地飘向了莱斯利·莫恩。接着，他透过敞开的窗户听到蹒跚的脚步声沿着铺砌的人行道走来。脚步在房门前停了下来；钥匙咔嗒作响。英格尔索恩太太经常在晚上出去，回来时总是一样的蹒跚脚步。不一会儿，门砰地关上了，她的嘟囔声从走廊传了上来。

===Original===
It was the first time he had seen or heard her that day. Usually, she did not go out at nights, but stayed at home to receive the curious callers who came at odd moments. They always knocked once with the knocker, and once with the flat of their hands, and generally they carried a parcel or package, big or small. There was a whispered colloquy in the passage, the chink of money, or, more rarely, the rustle of Treasury notes, and they went out again—without their parcels. This, Peter had seen and had not seen. Prison had taught him the wisdom of blindness, and he had not spoken to Mrs. Inglethorne of the furtive men and women who came slinking down Severall Street at those hours when the police patrol was well out of the way.

===Chinese===
那天是他第一次见到或听到她的动静。通常她晚上不出门，待在家里接待那些在奇怪时刻来的访客。他们总是先敲一下门环，再用手掌拍一下门，通常还带着大大小小的包裹或行李。走廊里会有窃窃私语，钱币叮当响，或者更少见的是钞票沙沙作响，然后他们又走了——包裹不见了。这些，彼得看在眼里却装作没看见。监狱教会了他装聋作哑的智慧，他从未对英格尔索恩太太提起过那些在警察巡逻远离时鬼鬼祟祟溜进塞弗拉尔街（Severall Street）的男人和女人。

===Original===
Leslie Maughan! He smiled a little at the thought of her, more at his own madness. What barriers separated them—barriers more real, more invincible, than the difference between Scotland Yard and Dartmoor Prison! It was worse than madness to think about her—

===Chinese===
莱斯利·莫恩！想到她，他微微一笑，更多的是笑自己的痴狂。横亘在他们之间的障碍——比苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）和达特穆尔监狱（Dartmoor Prison）之间的鸿沟更真实、更不可逾越的障碍！想到她简直是疯了——

===Original===
The scream that brought him to his feet was shrill and charged with fear and mortal agony. In two strides he was at the door and had pulled it open.

===Chinese===
一声尖叫让他跳了起来，尖锐刺耳，充满了恐惧和致命的痛苦。他两步就冲到门口，把门拉开了。

===Original===
Now he heard it plainly—the whistle and fall of a whip, the terrified, frantic cries for mercy. He ran down the stairs in the dark and tapped at Mrs. Inglethorne's door. From inside the room came a deep, heartbreaking sound of sobbing.

===Chinese===
现在他听清楚了——鞭子呼啸和抽打的声音，恐惧、疯狂的求饶声。他在黑暗中跑下楼梯，敲了敲英格尔索恩太太的门。房间里传来令人心碎的深沉啜泣声。

===Original===
"Who's that?" asked Mrs. Inglethorne defiantly. "Go away and mind your own business!"
"Open the door, or I'll break it open!" cried Peter in a cold fury.
"I'll send for the police if you interfere with me!" yelled the woman.

===Chinese===
"谁？"英格尔索恩太太挑衅地问道。"走开，少管闲事！"
"把门打开，不然我就把它砸开！"彼得冷冷地怒吼道。
"你敢管我，我就叫警察！"那女人尖叫道。

===Original===
His answer was to throw his weight against the flimsy door. The catch broke with a snap, and he was in the foul bedroom. Elizabeth lay cowering on a filthy camp bed, clad only in a coarse nightdress. Her head was pillowed in the crook of her arm, and convulsive sobbing shook the thin shoulders. Her face aflame, Mrs. Inglethorne stood at the foot of a big brass bedstead, one hand holding herself steady, the other grasping an old dog-whip.

===Chinese===
他的回答是猛地撞向那扇薄薄的门。门闩啪的一声断了，他冲进了那间恶臭的卧室。伊丽莎白蜷缩在一张肮脏的行军床上，只穿着一件粗糙的睡衣。她的头枕在弯起的臂弯里，剧烈的啜泣使瘦弱的肩膀颤抖着。英格尔索恩太太满脸通红，站在一张大铜床的床脚旁，一只手扶着自己稳住身子，另一只手握着一条旧的狗鞭。

===Original===
"I'll learn her to go talking about me!" she said thickly. "After all I've done for her!"
There was another child there, a girl who was apparently the same age as Elizabeth. She, however, enjoyed the luxury of Mrs. Inglethorne's ample bed, and was so used to this exhibition of the woman's wrath that she was asleep.

===Chinese===
"我要让她知道嚼舌根的下场！"她粗声粗气地说。"我为她做了那么多！"
还有另一个孩子，一个看起来和伊丽莎白同龄的女孩。不过，她享受着英格尔索恩太太宽大床铺的舒适，已经习惯了这个女人的怒火展示，已经睡着了。

===Original===
"Where is your coat, Elizabeth?" asked Peter gently.
The child looked up, her eyes swollen, her face red, and cast one fearful glance at her mother.
"Whatcher goin' to do?" asked Mrs. Inglethorne unsteadily.
"She will sleep in my room for the night," replied Peter. "Tomorrow I will make other arrangements for her, and if you give any trouble I shall send for the police."

===Chinese===
"你的外套在哪儿，伊丽莎白？"彼得温和地问。
孩子抬起头，眼睛红肿，脸上通红，恐惧地瞥了母亲一眼。
"你想干什么？"英格尔索恩太太不稳定地问。
"她今晚睡我的房间，"彼得回答。"明天我会为她做其他安排，如果你再找麻烦，我就叫警察。"

===Original===
Mrs. Inglethorne was amused in her way.
"Send for the police!" she scoffed. "I like that! An old lag sending for the police! And they'll come, won't they?"
