"I think so," said Peter quietly. "They will come, if only to discover why you never use the back room upstairs as a bedroom; why it is always kept locked, except after your visitors' calls."

===Chinese===
英格尔索恩太太以她特有的方式觉得好笑。
"叫警察！"她嘲笑道。"说得真好！一个老犯人叫警察！他们会来吗？"
"我想会的，"彼得平静地说。"他们会来的，即使只是为了查明你为什么从不把楼上的后屋当卧室用；为什么它总是锁着，只有在你的访客来过之后才打开。"

===Original===
The smile died from the woman's face.
"As far as I'm concerned," Peter went on, "you can 'fence' till the cows come home. But I'm not going to have you beating this child while I'm in the house. And when I'm out of it, and out of it for good, I'll see that she is well looked after."

===Chinese===
那女人脸上的笑容消失了。
"就我而言，"彼得继续说，"你可以继续'销赃'直到天荒地老。但只要我住在这里，就不许你再打这个孩子。等我搬走，永远搬走之后，我会确保她得到妥善照顾。"

===Original===
The woman's face was mottled with fear.
"'Fence'!" she spluttered. "I don't know what you mean by that low word! If you mean I receive stolen property, then you're a liar!"
"Let me call the police and settle the matter," said Peter.
The threat sobered her.

===Chinese===
那女人的脸因恐惧而斑驳陆离。
"销赃！"她唾沫横飞地说。"我不知道你这个下流词是什么意思！如果你是指我接收赃物，那你就是个骗子！"
"让我叫警察来解决这件事，"彼得说。
这个威胁让她清醒了。

===Original===
"I don't want any police in my house. The kid annoyed me, and it's a hard thing if a mother can't cane her own children without being interfered with. If she wants to sleep upstairs she can, but she'd be better off down here, Mr. Dawlish. You haven't got any accommodation for a little gel."
Which was true.
"All right, get into bed, Elizabeth." He covered her up with the pitifully thin bedclothes, and, without apology, took Mrs. Inglethorne's heavy coat that lay over the bedrail and put it on top. "Sleep well," he smiled, and patted her cheek.

===Chinese===
"我不想让任何警察进我的房子。这孩子惹恼了我，如果一个母亲不能管教自己的孩子而不受干涉，那也太说不过去了。如果她想睡楼上也可以，但在这里会更好，道利什先生。你没有地方安置一个小姑娘。"
这是事实。
"好了，上床睡觉吧，伊丽莎白。"他用薄得可怜的被子给她盖好，然后毫不客气地拿起搭在床栏上的英格尔索恩太太的厚大衣，盖在上面。"睡个好觉，"他微笑着拍了拍她的脸颊。

===Original===
She was safe for the night. What happened in the morning depended entirely on the view which Leslie Maughan took of a scheme that was beginning to take definite shape.

===Chinese===
她今晚安全了。明天早上会发生什么，完全取决于莱斯利·莫恩对一个正在成形的计划的看法。

===Original===
Mrs. Inglethorne was a fence, a buyer of stolen property. He had lived too long in association with the worst criminals of England to have any doubt upon the point, and, squinting through the keyhole one day in his curiosity, he had seen enough to remove the last remnants of doubt that remained.

===Chinese===
英格尔索恩太太是个销赃者，一个赃物买家。他与英格兰最恶劣的罪犯相处得太久了，对此毫不怀疑。有一天出于好奇，他眯着眼从锁孔里看进去，所见足以消除心中残存的最后一丝怀疑。

===Original===
He went to bed, determined to interview Leslie at the earliest opportunity, and it was not only on Elizabeth's account that the thought pleased him.

===Chinese===
他上了床，决心尽早与莱斯利面谈。这个想法令他高兴，不仅仅是因为伊丽莎白的缘故。

===Original===
When he arrived at the flat in Charing Cross Road next morning, Lucretia did not recognize him, and scowled fearfully at the suggestion that he should be admitted. She looked at his shabby attire and shook her head.
"It's no good your trying to see Miss Maughan. You'd better call on her at Scotland Yard. She's very busy now."
"Who is it, Lucretia?"
Leslie was leaning over the rails of the landing; she could not see the visitor, but she could hear the uncompromising note in Lucretia's voice.
"A young man wants to see you, miss. What's your name again? Dawlish."
"Oh, is that you, Peter Dawlish? Come up, will you?"

===Chinese===
第二天早上他来到查令十字路（Charing Cross Road）的公寓时，卢克丽霞（Lucretia）没有认出他，并对他提出进门的要求报以可怕的怒视。她看了看他寒酸的衣着，摇了摇头。
"你见莫恩小姐没有用。你最好去苏格兰场找她。她现在很忙。"
"是谁，卢克丽霞？"
莱斯利正靠在楼梯平台的栏杆上；她看不到访客，但能听出卢克丽霞声音中不妥协的语气。
"有个年轻人要见你，小姐。你叫什么来着？道利什。"
"哦，是你吗，彼得·道利什？上来吧，好吗？"

===Original===
Peter ran up the stairs, followed by the muttered protests of the maid.
"You're in time for breakfast. How are the envelopes going?"
"They're melting!" he said.

===Chinese===
彼得跑上楼梯，身后传来女仆嘀嘀咕咕的抗议声。
"你赶上吃早餐了。信封抄写得怎么样了？"
"简直要融化了！"他说。

===Original===
He was conscious of a certain indefinable change in her tone. It was not that she was more serious, but there seemed some listlessness about her, as though she were tired. It was almost an effort to talk. She looked weary, he saw, when they passed from the dark landing, and he commented on this.
"I've been up half the night," she said, "wandering about in a very cold garden, watching an elderly lady searching the ground with an electric lamp. That sounds mysterious, doesn't it?"
She pointed to a chair and Peter sat down.
"It sounds almost romantic. Where was this?"
"At Wimbledon." She waved the matter out of discussion. "Well," she asked, "what brings you to West Central London at this unholy hour?"

===Chinese===
他意识到她的语气中有一种难以名状的变化。并不是她更严肃了，而是她似乎有些无精打采，好像很疲倦。说话几乎要费很大劲。他们走过黑暗的楼梯平台时，他看出她很疲倦，并对此发表了评论。
"我大半夜没睡，"她说，"在一个很冷的花园里转悠，看着一位老太太用电灯搜寻地面。听起来很神秘，不是吗？"
她指了指椅子，彼得坐了下来。
"听起来几乎很浪漫。这是在哪儿？"
"在温布尔登（Wimbledon）。"她挥挥手，不想再讨论这个话题。"好吧，"她问，"什么风把你吹到西伦敦中心来了，还在这种不合适的时候？"

===Original===
Her grave eyes were fixed on his; there was something of reproach in them, something of hurt. He was puzzled; he felt that he had fallen short in her estimation, that she was disappointed with him for some reason. So strong was this impression that he grew uncomfortable under her gaze, and as though she were aware of this, she dropped her eyes to the table and began slowly to stir her coffee.

===Chinese===
她严肃的目光注视着他；眼中带着责备，带着受伤。他很困惑；他觉得自己在她心目中的形象有所减损，她因为某种原因对他感到失望。这种感觉如此强烈，以至于他在她的注视下变得不自在，仿佛她意识到了这一点，她垂下眼睛看着桌子，开始慢慢搅动她的咖啡。

===Original===
"I've come on a fool errand, with a wild and impossible suggestion."
And then he told her of what had happened overnight, of the merciless flogging which Mrs. Inglethorne had administered.

===Chinese===
"我来是做一件蠢事，带着一个疯狂而不可能的建议。"
然后他告诉她昨晚发生的事，英格尔索恩太太施加的无情鞭打。

===Original===
"The woman is a fence," he said, "not in a very big way. I think she specializes in furs and silk lengths."
She knew something of the genus fence, but he told her what he had learnt in Dartmoor, of fences who visited the scene of prospective robberies and priced the lot, practically paying for it, before it was stolen; of skilful men and women who would stand outside a small jeweller's shop and with one comprehensive glance assess the thieving value of the whole. He told her of "dead" stores—stores which were locked up at night, where nobody lived on the premises, and of "live" stores, where there was either a watchman or a proprietor and his family sleeping on the floors above.

===Chinese===
"这个女人是个销赃者，"他说，"规模不大。我想她专做皮草和丝绸布料的生意。"
她对销赃者这类人有所了解，但他在达特穆尔了解到的情况还是告诉了她：有些销赃者会在预期的抢劫现场查看，估定整批货物的价值，几乎是在盗窃发生前就付了款；还有一些精明的男人和女人，会站在一家小珠宝店外，一眼就能估出整家店被盗物品的价值。他告诉她"死"商店——那些晚上锁门、没人住在店内的商店，以及"活"商店——那些有看守人或店主及其家人睡在楼上的商店。

===Original===
"I am not reporting this officially—I mean the fence part of it, but the child is ill-used. The other little kids get a whacking now and again, but I should think she gets hers all the time."
"What do you wish me to do?" she asked, looking up at him.
"I don't know." He had a sense of awkwardness. "I had a wild idea that possibly you might be able to find—to do something with her."
"You mean take charge of her?"
She was smiling at him.
"Yes, I suppose I did mean that," he said after a second's thought. "It sounds fantastic and impossible now, but Elizabeth has got a grip on me. Probably it is my own rather unhappy childhood which is responding to her wretchedness."

===Chinese===
"我不是在正式举报——我是指销赃的部分，但这个孩子受到了虐待。其他小孩偶尔挨打，但我看她是天天挨打。"
"你希望我做什么？"她抬起头看着他问。
"我不知道。"他感到有些尴尬。"我有个疯狂的想法，也许你能找到——为她做点什么。"
"你是说照顾她？"
她对他微笑着。
"是的，我想我确实是这个意思，"他想了一下说。"这听起来很荒谬、不可能，但伊丽莎白抓住了我的心。也许是我自己不太幸福的童年在回应她的不幸。"

===Original===
She laughed.
"I'll make your mind easy at any rate," she said. "I had already considered the possibility. In fact I discussed the matter with Lucretia last night before I went out to dinner, and Lucretia was wildly enthusiastic. I have a spare room here; she could go to the Catholic school in Leicester Square. The only point is that we get Mrs. Inglethorne's consent."
"She had better," he said grimly, and her lips twitched.
"Really, you're almost ferocious when you're taking up the causes of other people," she said. "I wish you'd be a little energetic in your own."
"Aren't I?"
She shook her head.
"Not very," she answered, in her quiet way. "Why don't you see your mother—"
He grinned.
"She saved me the trouble and came last night."
"To Severall Street?" she asked in astonishment, and when he nodded: "Was it—a pleasant—encounter?"

===Chinese===
她笑了。
"无论如何，我会让你放心的，"她说。"我已经考虑过这种可能性。事实上，昨晚我出门吃饭前和卢克丽霞讨论过这件事，卢克丽霞非常热情。我这里有一间空房；她可以上莱斯特广场（Leicester Square）的天主教学校。唯一的问题是，我们要征得英格尔索恩太太的同意。"
"她最好同意，"他冷冷地说，她的嘴唇抽动了一下。
"真的，当你为别人的事挺身而出时，你简直有点凶，"她说。"我希望你对自己也能积极一点。"
"我没有吗？"
她摇了摇头。
"不太够，"她用她一贯的平静语气回答。"你为什么不和你母亲谈谈——"
他咧嘴一笑。
"她省了我的麻烦，昨晚自己来了。"
"来塞弗拉尔街？"她惊讶地问，当他点头时，"那是一次——愉快的——会面吗？"

===Original===
"A normally strained interview," he answered cheerfully. "She endeavoured to instil in me a passion for agriculture, and Canadian agriculture at that. I love Canada: you can't even take a weekend trip into C
anada without loving it. But the prospect of milking cows in Saskatchewan didn't appeal."
"She wanted you to go abroad? Why?"
He shrugged his shoulders.
"I suppose she rather feels there isn't room enough for both of us in London."

===Chinese===
"一次正常的紧张会面，"他愉快地回答。"她试图向我灌输对农业的热情，而且是加拿大的农业。我热爱加拿大：即使只是周末去加拿大旅行也会爱上它。但在萨斯喀彻温省（Saskatchewan）挤牛奶的前景并不吸引我。"
"她想让你出国？为什么？"
他耸了耸肩。
"我想她觉得我们俩在伦敦待不下去了。"

===Original===
She thought the matter over for a minute.
"Didn't your father leave you any money?"
"He cut me off without the proverbial shilling."
The lightness of his tone, she suspected, was assumed. Coldwell had told her how much Peter had loved his father.
"He altered his will at the eleventh hour—the day before I was sentenced—and left me nothing. Poor old dear! I haven't the slightest grudge; how could I? He was the best father that ever lived."

===Chinese===
她考虑了一会儿这个问题。
"你父亲没有给你留下任何钱吗？"
"他把我扫地出门，一个子儿都没留。"
她怀疑他语气中的轻松是装出来的。科尔德威尔（Coldwell）曾告诉她，彼得有多爱他的父亲。
"他在最后一刻改了遗嘱——在我宣判的前一天——什么都没留给我。可怜的老好人！我丝毫不怨恨他；怎么可能呢？他是世界上最好的父亲。"

===Original===
She had said she rarely smoked; she took a cigarette from her bag now and lit it without looking at him. Indeed, for the next four minutes, as he talked about his envelope addressing and his future, it seemed that she was more interested in the blue vapour that floated from the end of her cigarette than in his narrative.
"You're unfortunate."
She put down the cigarette, carefully took out a spoonful of coffee from the cup and dropped it on the glowing end as it lay in the saucer.
"You're unfortunate, Peter Dawlish, both as a son—and as a husband!"
He did not speak.
"Terribly unfortunate," she went on moodily. "I think you must have been born under a very unlucky star. I'm not asking you for confidences—you'd hate me if I did."

===Chinese===
她曾说她很少抽烟；此刻她从包里取出一支烟，没有看他一眼就点上了。事实上，在接下来的四分钟里，当他讲述信封抄写和他的未来时，她似乎对从烟头飘出的蓝色烟雾比对他的叙述更感兴趣。
"你真是不幸。"
她放下香烟，小心翼翼地从杯子里舀出一勺咖啡，滴在搁在茶碟上还在燃烧的烟头上。
"你真是不幸，彼得·道利什，无论是作为儿子——还是作为丈夫！"
他没有说话。
"非常不幸，"她闷闷不乐地继续说。"我想你一定是生在一颗非常不吉利的星星下。我不是在要求你吐露心事——如果你吐露了，你会恨我的。"

===Original===
Presently:
"How did you know?"
She fetched a long sigh.
"How did I know? Oh, I only knew yesterday for sure. I'd guessed for a long time—ever since I went on my holidays into Cumberland and found a little volume of Elizabeth Barrett Browning's with an inscription in doggerel blank verse on the flyleaf. It was when I saw that the first letter of every line reading from below upwards made the words 'Jane Dawlish' that I first guessed. But I wasn't certain—about the marriage. There was no record at Somerset House."

===Chinese===
过了一会儿：
"你怎么知道的？"
她长叹一声。
"我怎么知道的？哦，我直到昨天才确定。我猜了很久了——自从我去坎伯兰（Cumberland）度假，发现了一本伊丽莎白·巴雷特·勃朗宁（Elizabeth Barrett Browning）的小册子，扉页上用蹩脚的无韵诗写着一行题词。当我看到每行诗句从下往上读的第一个字母组成了'简·道利什'（Jane Dawlish）时，我才第一次猜到。但我并不确定——关于婚姻。萨默塞特宫（Somerset House）没有记录。"

===Original===
"We were married in America."
She nodded.
"I know that now; but why?"
He stared past her out of the window. Here, she thought, was a man who really regarded life as a terribly serious business. She was glad of that.

===Chinese===
"我们是在美国结婚的。"
她点点头。
"我现在知道了；但为什么呢？"
他的目光越过她，望向窗外。她想，这是一个真正把生活看作极其严肃之事的人。她对此感到欣慰。

===Original===
"Jane was very unhappy at home; her people were rotten. Her father kept a gambling house, and her mother—" He shrugged. "I fell in love with her. If I hadn't been a fool I would have gone to my father and told him the truth and then, in all probability, there would have been no cause for unhappiness. But I was aware that he knew Jane's people and knew that they were rotten. We went away to America together and were married in a little town in Connecticut. I suppose you know that? Her father was American born. From the first day the marriage was a ghastly mistake. Jane thought I had unlimited money. I had to pawn her jewels to get home, and there was a fearful scene when we landed at Liverpool. We were both a little crazy, and agreed then and there to separate. I went back to Lord Everreed's house to find detectives awaiting me at the railway station. I haven't seen or spoken with Jane since."

===Chinese===
"简在家里很不快乐；她的家人很糟糕。她父亲开了一家赌场，她母亲——"他耸了耸肩。"我爱上了她。如果我不是个傻瓜，我就会去找我父亲，告诉他真相，那么很可能就不会有不幸了。但我意识到他知道简的家人，知道他们很糟糕。我们一起去美国，在康涅狄格州的一个小镇结了婚。我想你知道吧？她父亲是美国出生的。从第一天起，这段婚姻就是一个可怕的错误。简以为我有花不完的钱。我不得不典当她的珠宝才得以回家，我们在利物浦上岸时发生了可怕的争吵。我们都有点疯了，当场就同意分手。我回到埃弗里德勋爵（Lord Everreed）家，发现侦探在火车站等着我。从那以后我再没见过简，也没和她说过话。"

===Original===
"Has she divorced you?"
He shook his head.
"I don't know. Things like that are possible in America, but I've had no notification."
Leslie bit her lip.
"If she hasn't—she's committed bigamy. You realize that?"
"I realize that," he said shortly. "Which means that I cannot free myself without betraying her—I can't do that. I couldn't expose her to imprisonment."
There was a tense and painful silence.

===Chinese===
"她和你离婚了吗？"
他摇了摇头。
"我不知道。在美国这种事是可能的，但我没有收到任何通知。"
莱斯利咬了咬嘴唇。
"如果她没有——她就犯了重婚罪。你意识到这点了吗？"
"我意识到了，"他简短地说。"这意味着我无法在不背叛她的情况下解脱自己——我不能那样做。我不能让她面临监禁。"
一阵紧张而痛苦的沉默。

===Original===
"Is that all?" she asked. "All you have to tell me?"
"You did not need telling, I think," he said, a little bitterly.
"No." She lit another cigarette; the flame of the match quivered unsteadily. "You're very unfortunate, Peter Dawlish."
She blew out the match with deliberation and put it carefully in her saucer by the side of the sodden cigarette.

===Chinese===
"就这些吗？"她问。"你没有别的要告诉我了？"
"我想你不需要我告诉你，"他有些苦涩地说。
"不。"她又点了一支烟；火柴的火焰不稳地摇曳着。"你真是不幸，彼得·道利什。"
她故意吹灭火柴，小心翼翼地把它放在茶碟里，放在那支湿透的香烟旁边。

===Original===
"You knew nothing about Druze, of course, or you would have told me. When did you say your father disinherited you?"
"The day before I went to prison."
She considered this.

===Chinese===
"你当然不知道德鲁兹（Druze）的事，否则你会告诉我的。你说你父亲是什么时候剥夺你的继承权的？"
"在我入狱的前一天。"
她考虑着这一点。

===Original===
"Tell me, Peter—you don't mind my calling you Peter? I feel rather sisterly towards you just now—what was the relationship between your father and mother? Cordial?"
He shook his head.
"No; they were never cordial, they were polite."
She bit her lip, looking at him absently.

===Chinese===
"告诉我，彼得——你不介意我叫你彼得吧？我现在对你有一种姐姐般的感觉——你父母的关系如何？亲密吗？"
他摇了摇头。
"不；他们从不亲密，只是客客气气。"
她咬着嘴唇，心不在焉地看着他。

===Original===
"Did you ever see the Princess Bellini at your father's house?"
"Only once," he replied. "Father disliked her—"
"She was a sort of aunt, wasn't she?" Leslie interrupted.
"I've never exactly fathomed the relationship. I've always understood that the Princess Bellini's brother married my mother's sister."

===Chinese===
"你在你父亲家见过贝里尼公主（Princess Bellini）吗？"
"只见过一次，"他回答。"父亲不喜欢她——"
"她是某种姑妈，对吗？"莱斯利打断道。
"我从未完全弄清这种关系。我一直以为贝里尼公主的哥哥娶了我母亲的姐姐。"

===Original===
She rose from the table abruptly, for no apparent reason.
"Peter Dawlish," she said, and her voice shook a little in spite of her assumption of banter, "if you were cursed with my intense curiosity you might be a very much happier man."
"What do you mean?" he asked.
"I'll tell you—some day. And now let us get back to our muttons; and our muttons for the moment are poor Elizabeth. The only difficulty in the way is Mrs. Inglethorne. As a loving mother, she may very well object to her child being taken from her. Obviously, I cannot use the same argument as you have done. If she is a 'fence' and a lawbreaker, it is my duty to inform Mr. Coldwell and have her arrested. If she isn't a lawbreaker, we shall have to get after her from another angle. That sounds terribly businesslike. I think I'll go back with you to Severall Street and see Mrs. Inglethorne myself. She may be amenable to reason."

===Chinese===
她毫无缘由地突然从桌边站起来。
"彼得·道利什，"她说，尽管她故作戏谑，声音还是微微发颤，"如果你像我一样充满好奇，你可能会是一个幸福得多的人。"
"你什么意思？"他问。
"我以后会告诉你的——总有一天。现在让我们回到正题；我们的正题是可怜的伊丽莎白。唯一的障碍是英格尔索恩太太。作为一个慈爱的母亲，她很可能反对她的孩子被带走。显然，我不能用你那样的论据。如果她是个'销赃者'和违法者，我有责任通知科尔德威尔先生并逮捕她。如果她不是违法者，我们就得从另一个角度下手。这听起来太公事公办了。我想我和你一起回塞弗拉尔街，亲自见见英格尔索恩太太。她也许会通情达理的。"

===Original===
They went by bus to the southern end of Westminster Bridge and walked along York Road together. Just before they reached Severall Street they saw a small motor-lorry turn into the main road, and mechanically, Leslie, who had a weakness for such mental registrations, turned her head to note the number. It was a favourite trick of hers to carry fifty or sixty motorcar numbers in her head and jot them down at the close of the day—a practice into which Mr. Coldwell had initiated her. As she looked round—

===Chinese===
他们乘公共汽车到威斯敏斯特桥（Westminster Bridge）南端，一起沿着约克路（York Road）走。就在他们到达塞弗拉尔街之前，他们看到一辆小型机动卡车拐进了大路，莱斯利出于习惯——她有记录车牌号的癖好——转过头去记下号码。她喜欢在脑子里记住五六十个车牌号，然后在一天结束时记下来——这是科尔德威尔先生教给她的习惯。就在她回头的时候——

===Original===
"Lady!"
A shrill voice called her.
"Who was that?" she asked, but Peter had not heard.
They reached the house, and he opened the door and called Mrs. Inglethorne, but it was one of the children who answered.
"Mother's gone out. Her and Elizabeth."
Sometimes the woman took the child with her when she went shopping, Peter explained.
"I'm afraid I've brought you on a long job," he said. "She may be out for hours."

===Chinese===
"小姐！"
一个尖利的声音在叫她。
"是谁？"她问，但彼得没听见。
他们到了房子前，他打开门喊英格尔索恩太太，但回答的是一个孩子。
"妈妈出去了。她和伊丽莎白一起。"
彼得解释说，有时那女人出门购物会带上孩子。
"恐怕我带你来干了一件费时的活儿，"他说。"她可能要出去好几个小时。"

===Original===
Leaving her for a moment in the passage, he ran upstairs to his room, intending to show her one of his small treasures, the photograph of his dead father. He reached the head of the stairs, and then stopped, aghast. The door of the mysterious locked room which adjoined his own was wide open, and when he strode in he saw it was empty. Mrs. Inglethorne was a quick worker, and, in the space of time between his departure and his return, had removed all evidence of her guilt.

===Chinese===
他让她在走廊里稍等，自己跑上楼去他的房间，打算给她看一件他的小宝贝——他已故父亲的照片。他走到楼梯顶端，然后惊愕地停住了。那间与他房间相邻的神秘上锁房间的门大敞着，当他大步走进去时，发现里面空空如也。英格尔索恩太太手脚真快，在他离开和返回的这段时间里，已经把所有罪证都清除了。

===Original===
He went into his own room, pulled open the drawer of the table where he kept his few treasures, and had taken out the small leather-covered portfolio when he saw some writing on the pad; a few scribbled words in a childish hand.

===Chinese===
他走进自己的房间，拉开桌子的抽屉——他把几件宝贝都放在那里——正要拿出那个小皮面文件夹时，看到了拍纸簿上的字迹；几个孩子气的潦草字迹。

===Original===
> "She has taken me away.—Elizabeth."
He tore off the corner of the blotting-paper and went back to the girl.
"I was afraid of this," she said in a low voice. "Do you remember the cry 'Lady!' as we passed the motor-van? Where is the nearest telephone booth?"

===Chinese===
> "她把我带走了。——伊丽莎白。"
他撕下吸墨纸的一角，回到姑娘身边。
"我早就担心会这样，"她低声说。"你还记得我们经过那辆货车时那声'小姐！'吗？最近的电话亭在哪儿？"

===Original===
At the corner of the street was a little general shop, which had a telephone sign, and Leslie almost ran into the shop. There was some delay before the instrument was disengaged, but in a few minutes she was connected with Scotland Yard and was talking to Coldwell.
"The number of the car is XY 63369," she said. "There is no doubt whatever that it contains stolen property, but it is the little girl I want."
"I'll send out a call," was Coldwell's reply. "We may not pick it up before tonight; on the other hand, we may be lucky."

===Chinese===
街角有一家杂货小铺，挂着电话标志，莱斯利几乎是跑进了店里。电话等了一会儿才空出来，但几分钟后她就接通了苏格兰场，和科尔德威尔通上了话。
"车牌号是XY 63369，"她说。"毫无疑问，车里装的是赃物，但我想要的是那个小女孩。"
"我会发出通报，"科尔德威尔回答。"我们今晚之前可能抓不到；但另一方面，我们可能会走运。"

===Original===
"Where are you going now?" asked Peter when they were outside the shop.
"Back to the house," said Leslie. "I want to look at that room."
"They cleared everything."
She nodded.
"Thieves in a hurry are very careless people, and perhaps Mrs. Inglethorne isn't so clever as she imagines."

===Chinese===
"你现在要去哪儿？"他们在店外时彼得问。
"回房子去，"莱斯利说。"我想看看那个房间。"
"他们把东西都清理了。"
她点点头。
"匆忙的贼是很粗心的人，也许英格尔索恩太太没有她自以为的那么聪明。"

===Original===
The room was apparently bare; the only article of furniture it contained was a long table, and by the dust-marks on this Leslie was able to judge the extent of the property that had been stored. On either side of the rusty fireplace was a cupboard. Both of these she opened and found empty, except for a little heap of rubbish at the bottom. The second, however, was locked. With a table-knife borrowed from the kitchen she forced back the catch and pulled open the door. There was nothing very much there, but enough. Three bolts of silk, one still bearing the label of the wholesaler from whom it had been stolen.
"Thieves in a hurry are very careless," she said, with the light of battle in her eyes; "and it really doesn't matter whether Mrs. Inglethorne is hanged for a sheep or a lamb, so long as she's well and truly hanged!"

===Chinese===
房间显然被清空了；里面唯一的家具是一张长桌，从桌上的灰尘痕迹，莱斯利能够判断出储存赃物的规模。生锈的壁炉两侧各有一个橱柜。她把两个都打开了，发现都是空的，只有底部有一小堆垃圾。不过第二个是锁着的。她从厨房借来一把餐刀，撬开锁扣，拉开了门。里面东西不多，但足够了。三匹丝绸，其中一匹还贴着被盗批发商的标签。
"匆忙的贼是很粗心的，"她眼中闪着战斗的光芒说；"英格尔索恩太太是因大罪还是小罪被绞死并不重要，只要她被好好地、真正地绞死就行！"

===Original===
She sent Peter to the police station, and went down to interview the children. A grubby lot of little people they were, very pale, very starved looking, except one, who apparently was in charge in Mrs. Inglethorne's absence. She was the little girl, Leslie learnt later, who had slept in the woman's bed, and, unlike the others, she bore a striking facial resemblance to her mother.
"You didn't find nothing, did you?" She was frankly hostile. "You've got to be up very early to catch my old woman, missis!"
And then, turning to the silent semicircle of children who constituted the remainder of Mrs. Inglethorne's family, she ordered them peremptorily away.
"Go and play in the back yard."
Poor little starvelings! Leslie's heart bled to see them. She sought, by delicate inquiry, to discover where Elizabeth had been taken, but the preternatural cunning of the child she questioned baffled her.

===Chinese===
她派彼得去警察局，自己则去盘问那些孩子。他们是一群邋遢的小家伙，非常苍白，非常面黄肌瘦，只有一个例外——那个在英格尔索恩太太不在时显然负责的孩子。后来莱斯利得知，那就是睡在那个女人床上的小女孩，与其他孩子不同，她长得非常像她母亲。
"你什么也没找到吧？"她公开表示敌意。"你想抓到我老娘，起得可不够早，太太！"
然后，她转向围成半圆的沉默孩子们——英格尔索恩太太其余的家庭成员——专横地命令他们走开。
"去后院玩。"
可怜的小饿鬼！莱斯利看到他们心都碎了。她试图通过巧妙的询问找出伊丽莎白被带到哪里去了，但被问的孩子那种超乎常人的狡猾让她无计可施。

===Original===
Peter came back in a very short time, accompanied by a uniformed inspector and a plain clothes officer. They made an inspection of the silk and carried it off with them to the station.
"This may affect you a little, Peter Dawlish," said Leslie when they were alone. "The children will be removed to the workhouse this afternoon, and Mrs. Inglethorne will be arrested immediately on her return, so that you will have the house to yourself."
He laughed.
"I'm not depressed," he said.

===Chinese===
彼得很快就回来了，跟着一名穿制服的警官和一名便衣警察。他们检查了丝绸，把它带回了警察局。
"这可能会对你有些影响，彼得·道利什，"他们单独在一起时莱斯利说。"孩子们今天下午会被送到济贫院，英格尔索恩太太一回来就会被逮捕，这样整栋房子就归你了。"
他笑了。
"我没有不开心，"他说。

===Original===
He walked with her as far as Westminster Bridge, and at parting she asked him a curious question.
"What would you do if you had half a million pounds?"
He looked at her in astonishment and laughed.
"That isn't my favourite dream," he said. "But I think the first thing I should do would be to send to America to discover whether I have been, as you would say, 'well and truly' divorced."
"Indeed?" Her tone was a trifle cold. "Is that necessary—when Jane Raytham is within a penny bus ride?"
And with a nod she was gone.

===Chinese===
他陪她走到威斯敏斯特桥，分手时她问了他一个奇怪的问题。
"如果你有五十万英镑，你会做什么？"
他惊讶地看着她，笑了。
"那不是我最喜欢的梦，"他说。"但我想我首先要做的就是去美国查查我是否如你所说的，被'真正地'离婚了。"
"是吗？"她的语气有点冷。"那有必要吗——简·雷瑟姆（Jane Raytham）不是就在一便士公共汽车车程之内吗？"
她点了点头，走了。

===Original===
Peter returned to the house and found it very difficult to resume his work or concentrate his mind upon lists. He had hardly started before the police officials came with an omnibus to take away the children, and they departed with no visible reluctance, except in the case of the girl whom Leslie had interviewed.

===Chinese===
彼得回到房子里，发现很难继续工作，也很难把心思集中在抄写名单上。他刚开始不久，警察就开了一辆大车来带走孩子们，他们毫无明显不情愿地离开了，除了那个莱斯利盘问过的女孩。

===Original===
At four o'clock in the afternoon Mrs. Inglethorne came into the house in triumph, and without going into the kitchen, mounted the stairs and stood, arms akimbo, her red face made hideous by a self-satisfied smirk, confronting her lodger.
"Well, did you bring in the police?" she demanded. "And what are you going to do with Elizabeth?" And, when he did not answer, she shook her fist at him. "Out you go, out of my house, you 'nose'! I'll learn you to go prying around and threatening me! You leave this room at once, or I'll send for a policeman."

===Chinese===
下午四点钟，英格尔索恩太太凯旋般地走进房子，没有进厨房，就直接上了楼梯，双手叉腰站着，自鸣得意的假笑让她那张红脸显得更加丑陋，面对着她的房客。
"怎么样，你叫警察了吗？"她质问道。"你打算怎么处置伊丽莎白？"见他没有回答，她朝他晃着拳头。"滚出去，滚出我的房子，你这个告密者！我要让你知道窥探和威胁我的下场！你马上离开这个房间，否则我就叫警察。"

===Original===
"I think I'll stay," he said good humouredly.
"Oh, will you?"
She went to the door and roared for Emma. There was no answer.
"I can save you a lot of trouble, Mrs. Inglethorne," said Peter, putting down his pen. "Your children have been taken away to the workhouse."
She staggered back against the wall, her big mouth open wide.
"W-why?" she stammered.
"It is usual to take children to the workhouse when their parents are arrested and there are no other relatives to look after them," he said.
"Arrested?" she screamed.

===Chinese===
"我想我会留下的，"他和颜悦色地说。
"哦，是吗？"
她走到门口大声叫艾玛。没人回应。
"我可以替你省不少麻烦，英格尔索恩太太，"彼得放下笔说。"你的孩子们已经被送到济贫院去了。"
她踉跄着退到墙上，大张着嘴。
"为——为什么？"她结结巴巴地说。
"当父母被捕而没有其他亲属照管孩子时，通常会把孩子送到济贫院，"他说。
"被捕？"她尖叫道。

===Original===
He nodded to the window, and she staggered past him and, pulling up the sash, looked out. Two men were standing on the opposite sidewalk, and one nodded as to an old friend. She recognized the detective-sergeant who had arrested her husband.
"They can't touch me!" she screamed. "They can't touch me! It's my word against yours."
"Unfortunately you left a few bolts of silk behind in the cupboard," answered Peter.
Mrs. Inglethorne was in a state of collapse when the detectives came in to arrest her.

===Chinese===
他朝窗户点了点头，她踉跄着走过他身边，拉起窗扇向外望去。两个男人站在对面人行道上，其中一个像老朋友一样点了点头。她认出了逮捕她丈夫的那位探长。
"他们不能碰我！"她尖叫道。"他们不能碰我！我的话和你的话哪个更可信。"
"不幸的是，你在橱柜里留下了幾匹丝绸，"彼得回答。
当侦探进来逮捕她时，英格尔索恩太太已经瘫倒了。

===Original===
The motor-lorry had been traced; the driver and a man who accompanied the car had been driven to the nearest police station, where the plunder was checked and exhibited in preparation for the charge which would follow. They either could not or would not, however, give any information concerning the child, and when Leslie went to Lambeth to interview Mrs. Inglethorne in her cell, she was no more successful.
"Find her!" rapped the woman. "She's in good hands, that's what she is. I'm not saying anything. If you want her, find her—that's my last word to you!"

===Chinese===
那辆机动卡车已经被追踪到了；司机和车上的一个人被带到了最近的警察局，赃物在那里被清点和展示，为随后的指控做准备。然而，他们要么不能、要么不愿提供任何关于那个孩子的信息。当莱斯利去兰贝斯在牢房里盘问英格尔索恩太太时，也没有成功。
"找到她！"那女人厉声说。"她在好人家手里，我不会说任何话的。如果你想要她，就去找她——这就是我对你说的最后一句话！"

===Original===
Leslie did not notify Peter that she was coming to Lambeth. Passing up Severall Street on her way home, she saw the light in the upstairs window and guessed that he was still working hard. A postman rapped at the door, and she waited a while until it was opened, as she guessed, by Peter, and almost turned back just to say a word to him. And if she overcame this deplorable weakness, it was not lightly done.
"Leslie Maughan," she said to herself, mounting the steps of Hungerford Bridge, "do you know what you are doing? Shall I tell you in the vulgarest terms? You're chasing a married man! Leslie, that isn't done—not in the best society."

===Chinese===
莱斯利没有通知彼得她要去兰贝斯。在回家的路上经过塞弗拉尔街时，她看到楼上的窗户亮着灯，猜他还在努力工作。一个邮递员敲了敲门，她等了一会儿，直到门开了——她猜是彼得开的门——几乎要转身回去和他说句话。如果她克服了这种可悲的软弱，那也是经过一番挣扎的。
"莱斯利·莫恩，"她一边说一边踏上亨格福德桥（Hungerford Bridge）的台阶，"你知道你在做什么吗？让我用最粗俗的话告诉你？你在追一个有妇之夫！莱斯利，这种事是不能做的——在上流社会是不行的。"

===Original===
She was uncommonly weary when she dragged herself into her own sitting-room, deciding to forego the duty she had planned. This was a second call upon Greta Gurden. That afternoon there had been a consultation at Scotland Yard, but matters had not developed sufficiently to justify the issue even of a search warrant.

===Chinese===
她拖着疲惫的身子走进自己的客厅时，感到异常疲倦，决定放弃她计划好的任务。这是对格蕾塔·格登（Greta Gurden）的第二次拜访。那天下午苏格兰场开了一个会，但事情还没有发展到足以签发搜查令的程度。

===Original===
After a light dinner she took out the letter she had received two nights before, spread the foolscap on her desk and examined it carefully. It was a queer story she read, even in the stilted terminology of an elderly country parson, who employed such words as "primogeniture" and felt it necessary to sprinkle his pages with quotations from Horace, mostly in Latin. The writer was the vicar of a small Devonshire vicarage near Budleigh Salterton, and he had, as he said in a preliminary flourish, "reached the four score of the prophet." He wasted a page in explaining how he came to reach these years, and employed mens sana in corpore sano at least twice in the first folio.

===Chinese===
吃了一顿简单的晚餐后，她拿出两天前收到的那封信，在书桌上展开信纸，仔细阅读。即使从一位年迈的乡村牧师那种刻板的措辞中读来，这也是一个奇怪的故事——他使用"长子继承制"（primogeniture）这样的词，并觉得有必要在书页中穿插贺拉斯（Horace）的引语，大多是拉丁文。作者是德文郡（Devonshire）巴德利索尔顿（Budleigh Salterton）附近一个小教区的牧师，正如他在开场白中所说，他已经"达到了先知的八十高龄"。他花了整整一页纸解释自己是如何活到这个年纪的，并且在第一页上至少用了两次"健全的精神寓于健全的体魄"（mens sana in corpore sano）。

===Original===
He knew the Druze family very well; they lived in his village and had done so for hundreds of years. He himself had baptized Alice Mary Druze and Annie Emily Druze, and several other members of the Druze family which he thought it was necessary to enumerate by their full names (it had necessitated long researches in ancient registers). The Druze family had for generations farmed some 40 acres of poor land on the edge of Dartmoor. They were "a wild family with a bad history," and here the reverend gentleman, who was also something of a scientist, branched away from the main track to a discourse upon heredity which would have done credit to a Lombroso.

===Chinese===
他非常了解德鲁兹家族；他们住在他的村子里，已经住了好几百年。他亲自为爱丽丝·玛丽·德鲁兹（Alice Mary Druze）和安妮·艾米丽·德鲁兹（Annie Emily Druze）施了洗，还有其他几个他认为有必要列出全名的德鲁兹家族成员（这需要在古老的登记簿中进行长期的研究）。德鲁兹家族世代在达特穆尔边缘耕种约40英亩贫瘠的土地。他们是一个"有着糟糕历史的野蛮家族"，这位可敬的绅士同时也是一位科学家，在这里偏离了主线，开始论述遗传学，其水平足以让龙勃罗梭（Lombroso）自愧不如。

===Original===
Old father Druze was a lunatic and had died mad; his grandfather had committed suicide (there was a record in the parish registry and a note that he had been buried at the crossroads, in the proper manner for such as take their lives). Druze's grandmother had also a history of sorts. The clergyman remembered her as a "respectable woman," though inclined to gaiety, and he even felt it necessary to retail a hundred-year-old piece of scandal, something that had happened at Widdicombe Fair.

===Chinese===
老德鲁兹是个疯子，疯着死的；他的祖父自杀身亡（教区登记处有记录，并注明他被埋葬在十字路口，这是自杀者应有的待遇）。德鲁兹的祖母也有一段不光彩的历史。牧师记得她是个"体面的女人"，虽然喜欢寻欢作乐，他甚至觉得有必要复述一段百年旧丑闻——发生在威德科姆集市（Widdicombe Fair）上的事。

===Original===
Alice was illiterate; he had extracted a note of this fact from the register of the church school. Annie, on the other hand, was a diligent scholar and "showed surprising proficiency in the study of the so-called dead languages," so that she "speedily secured a respectable situation with a haberdasher in Exeter, a Mr. Watson. She was a Godfearing young woman, a communicant, and eventually married a well-to-do farmer in the neighbourhood of Torquay." The farmer's nam
e Leslie jotted down on her pad.

===Chinese===
爱丽丝是文盲；他是从教会学校的登记簿中查到这个事实的。另一方面，安妮是一个勤奋的学生，"在所谓的死语言学习中表现出惊人的天赋"，因此"很快在埃克塞特（Exeter）一家杂货店找到了一份体面的工作，店主是沃森先生（Mr. Watson）。她是一个敬畏上帝的年轻女子，是圣餐参与者，最终嫁给了托基（Torquay）附近一位富裕的农民。"莱斯利把那个农民的名字记在了拍纸簿上。

===Original===
The third of the daughters, Martha, was of an "exemplary character, though of no great educational attainments." About her the clergyman was very explicit, for it was he who had obtained her a post, first as stillroom maid at a Plymouth hospital, and afterwards, on his recommendation, as a probationary nurse. It was believed that she went to South Africa and "married a prosperous carpenter."

===Chinese===
第三个女儿玛莎（Martha）"品格模范，虽然教育程度不高。"关于她，牧师写得非常详细，因为正是他帮她找到了一份工作，先是在普利茅斯（Plymouth）一家医院当储藏室女仆，后来在他的推荐下成为见习护士。据信她去了南非，"嫁给了一个生意兴隆的木匠。"

===Original===
When Leslie had traced Druze to that little Devonshire village, and wrote, with no great hope, to the vicar, she hardly expected so voluminous and conscientious a record of the family history; for he even sent photographs of tombstones which marked the departed Druzes of the eighteenth century!
If she had only read this before, she thought, she could not have been shocked by the discovery that "Arthur Druze" was a woman; for apparently there was no male member in that family, except the semi-lunatic father and a remote uncle who for some reason wasn't called Druze at all. She read through carefully, took down an atlas and a gazetteer from her bookshelf, and finally locked letter and data in the drawer. Her work was by no means finished for the night, though she was dropping with weariness. She had a number of letters to write. Before she had left the office, Mr. Coldwell had given her the names and addresses of a dozen people who would be helpful to her in the search she was making.

===Chinese===
当莱斯利追溯到德文郡那个小村庄，并不抱太大希望地写信给牧师时，她几乎没指望会得到如此详尽而认真的家族史记录；他甚至寄来了标记着十八世纪已故德鲁兹家族成员的墓碑照片！
她想，如果她早点读到这些，就不会对"亚瑟·德鲁兹"（Arthur Druze）是个女人这一发现感到震惊了；因为显然这个家族中没有男性成员，除了那个半疯的父亲和一个不知为何不姓德鲁兹的远房叔叔。她仔细读完信，从书架上取下一本地图册和地名词典，最后把信和资料锁进了抽屉。尽管她已经疲惫不堪，但今晚的工作远未结束。她还有许多信要写。离开办公室之前，科尔德威尔先生给了她十几个对她的调查有帮助的人的姓名和地址。

===Original===
At eleven o'clock they phoned from Scotland Yard to tell her that there was no news of Elizabeth. Mrs. Inglethorne, confronted as she was with a long term of imprisonment, possibly of penal servitude, refused any information about the child, except that she had gone to "her aunt's."
Lucretia brought her coffee. The girl had an irritating trick of expressing her disapproval by audible tut-tuts, and twice did she tut-tut into the room and out again. At last she extinguished all the lights in the room save the table lamp.
"You've got to go to bed, miss," she said firmly. "I'll have you on my hands if I'm not careful. And what about this young girl?"

===Chinese===
十一点钟时，苏格兰场打电话告诉她没有伊丽莎白的消息。英格尔索恩太太面对长期监禁——可能是苦役监禁——拒绝提供任何关于那个孩子的信息，只说她去了"她姑妈家"。
卢克丽霞给她端来了咖啡。这个女孩有个令人恼火的习惯，用能听见的"啧啧"声表示不赞成，她两次"啧啧"着进出房间。最后她熄灭了房间里所有的灯，只留下桌灯。
"你必须上床睡觉了，小姐，"她坚定地说。"要是不小心，我会被你折腾死的。那个小女孩怎么办？"

===Original===
Leslie rose stiffly from her desk, gathered the letters together and stamped them.
"She is not coming tonight," she said. "Post these, Lucretia. I'll wait for you to return and then you can go to bed."
She heard the door open and guessed, by the cold draught that swept up the stairs, that Lucretia had followed her usual practice of leaving the door ajar whilst she went to the nearest pillar-box, which was some distance from the flat.
It was part of the night's routine that Lucretia should take the letters; almost a ritual that Leslie should stand in the open doorway of her sitting-room until she heard the girl return.

===Chinese===
莱斯利僵硬地从桌边站起来，把信件收拢并盖上邮戳。
"她今晚不会来了，"她说。"把这些信寄出去，卢克丽霞。我会等你回来，然后你就可以去睡觉了。"
她听到门开了，从冲上楼梯的冷风判断，卢克丽霞又像往常一样把门虚掩着去最近的邮筒了——邮筒离公寓有一段距离。
晚上让卢克丽霞寄信是例行公事的一部分；几乎成了一种仪式，莱斯利会站在客厅敞开的门口，直到听到女孩回来。

===Original===
The maid could not have been gone half a minute before the street door below closed softly. She heard the gentle thud of it.
"Is that you, Lucretia?" she called down into the dark hall.
There was no reply.
Her flesh crept, for no reason that she could understand; a cold shiver went down her spine. Leslie Maughan was not a nervous girl. Her duty and association with Coldwell had taken her into many uncomfortable situations, and unless it was because she was very tired, there was no particular reason for nervousness. But her sensation was something more than the unease which comes to the strongest nerves when they are left alone in a house. It was a premonition, a warning, indeed a certain knowledge that there was somebody in the hall below who should not be there.

===Chinese===
女仆走了不到半分钟，楼下大门就轻轻关上了。她听到了柔和的关门声。
"是你吗，卢克丽霞？"她朝黑暗的门厅喊道。
没有回应。
她的皮肤起了鸡皮疙瘩，虽然她不明白是什么原因；一阵寒意顺着她的脊梁骨流下来。莱斯利·莫恩不是个神经质的女孩。她的职责和与科尔德威尔的合作使她经历了很多不舒服的场合，除非是因为她太累了，否则没有特别的理由感到紧张。但她的感觉不仅仅是在独自待在房子里时最强壮的神经也会产生的不安。这是一种预感，一种警告，确实是一种确信——楼下大厅里有个不该在那里的人。

===Original===
She went back into the room, closed the door quietly and slipped in a bolt she had had fitted. She switched on the lights that Lucretia had extinguished, and, going to the window, pulled the curtains apart and lifted the sash. Charing Cross Road was fairly well crowded with people. It was a clear night and a few paces away she saw two policemen patrolling, and presently she discerned Lucretia making her way hurriedly across the road. The maid came beneath the window simultaneously with a policeman; Leslie called her and she looked up.
"Tell the policemen I want them to come in," she said. "Here is the key—catch!"
One of the officers caught the key deftly.
"Anything wrong, miss?" he asked, knowing her.
"I think somebody has come into the house whilst my maid went out to post a letter. You left the door open, did you, Lucretia?"
"Yes, miss, I did," confessed the agitated Lucretia. "I forgot to take the key."
"Well, hurry—" she began.
At that moment all the lights in the room went out.

===Chinese===
她回到房间里，轻轻关上门，插上她安装的门闩。她打开卢克丽霞关掉的灯，走到窗边，拉开窗帘，抬起窗扇。查令十字路相当拥挤。这是一个晴朗的夜晚，几步之外她看到两个警察在巡逻，很快她辨认出卢克丽霞正匆匆穿过马路。女仆和一个警察同时来到窗下；莱斯利叫了她，她抬起头来。
"告诉警察我要他们进来，"她说。"这是钥匙——接住！"
其中一个警察灵巧地接住了钥匙。
"有什么不对吗，小姐？"他认识她，问道。
"我想在我女仆出去寄信时有人进了房子。你没锁门，对吗，卢克丽霞？"
"是的，小姐，我没锁，"慌张的卢克丽霞承认。"我忘了拿钥匙。"
"那快点——"她刚说。
就在这时，房间里所有的灯都灭了。

===Original===
She sat on the sill and swung out her legs, her eyes fixed on the door, which was visible in the light of a street standard. A faint creaking sound came to her ears and she saw the door move slightly—the bolt was straining under some enormous pressure. Then a voice from the pavement below hailed her.
"The street door won't open, miss," said the policeman's voice.
She looked back at the door. The slot of the bolt was giving under the strain.
"Can you catch me?" she asked.
The two men ran to the pavement beneath her.
"Jump!"
Again she looked back. At that moment, with a crash, the door opened. She had a dim vision of two stunted figures, then, bracing her hands on the sill, she jumped.

===Chinese===
她坐在窗台上，把腿伸出去晃荡着，眼睛盯着门——在街灯的光线下能看见门。一阵轻微的嘎吱声传入她的耳朵，她看到门微微移动——门闩在巨大的压力下绷紧。接着，下面人行道上传来一个声音向她喊道。
"大门打不开，小姐，"是警察的声音。
她回头看了看门。门闩的插槽在压力下正在变形。
"你能接住我吗？"她问。
两个男人跑到她下面的人行道上。
"跳！"
她又回头看了一眼。就在这时，随着一声巨响，门开了。她模糊地看到两个矮小的身影，然后她双手撑在窗台上，跳了下去。

===Original===
It was not a dignified landing, but for the moment Leslie Maughan was less interested in her dignity than her safety. A crowd had already gathered, attracted by the unusual happening, and there appeared from nowhere an inspector of police, a resourceful man who, having heard the story, immediately stopped an omnibus and ordered the driver to bring his big machine on to the sidewalk immediately beneath the window. Standing on the rail of the bus, one of the policemen reached the windowsill and climbed inside, and was followed by the inspector. There was no sound of the struggle which the morbid crowd expected. A few minutes later the door below was unbolted and Le

===Chinese===
落地姿势不太优雅，但此刻莱斯利·莫恩更关心安全而非尊严。人群已经聚集起来，被这不寻常的事件吸引，不知从哪里冒出一个警官，他是个足智多谋的人，听完事情经过后，立即拦下一辆公共汽车，命令司机把这辆大车开到窗下的人行道上。一个警察站在公共汽车的栏杆上，够到了窗台爬了进去，警官也跟着进去了。病态的人群期待的打斗声并没有出现。几分钟后，楼下的门被打开了锁，莱斯利和颤抖的卢克丽霞走进了走廊。
他们发现一楼楼梯平台上的大厅窗户敞开着。街上传来警哨的嗡嗡声；很快整个街区就会被包围。

===Original===
"No, they haven't cut the wire, as far as I can see," said the inspector, examining the wall of the passage with his lamp. "Where do you keep your fuse-box?"
"I think it is near the door," said the girl.
It proved to be within easy reach. The flat had been darkened by the simple expedient of removing the fuses. They found them intact on the floor and replaced them, and an inspection was possible. Except for the broken door, no damage had been done to the flat. Whoever the intruders were, their time had been too short to conduct a search of the room. The drawers of the desk were untouched.
"They hadn't much time, had they?" said the puzzled inspector. "I can't understand this job—if they were ordinary burglars they would have cleared just as soon as they knew you had spotted them."

===Chinese===
"不，据我观察，他们没有切断电线，"警官用灯检查着走廊的墙壁说。"你们的保险丝盒放在哪里？"
"我想就在门旁边，"女孩说。
保险丝盒就在伸手可及的地方。公寓之所以变黑，是因为保险丝被拔掉了这个简单的手法。他们发现保险丝完好无损地放在地板上，便把它们装回去，检查得以进行。除了破损的门，公寓没有受到任何损坏。无论入侵者是谁，他们待的时间太短，没能搜查房间。书桌的抽屉没有被动过。
"他们时间不多，对吧？"困惑的警官说。"我不明白这桩案子——如果他们是普通窃贼，一发现你发现了他们就会溜之大吉的。"

===Original===
Half an hour later, and before the police had departed, Mr. Coldwell came on the scene. By this time every roof and yard in the vicinity had been searched; night watchmen had been aroused from their surreptitious sleep, and a small army of police detectives had examined every window that might afford a possible means of escape. But no sign of the intruders was discovered.
"I don't like this," said Leslie.
Mr. Coldwell shook his head.
"You'll have to find other lodgings for a while. Tomorrow you had better transfer your belongings and Lucretia to my house at Hampstead."
For five minutes he discussed in a low voice the theories he had formed, the plans he had made.
"I don't think it is necessary to leave a policeman in the house," he said at last, and a little yellow man, curled up on the top of the high bureau in Leslie's room, screened from observation by the old-fashioned friezes of the wardrobe, was relieved.
He heard the policeman go clattering down the stairs, and after a while:
"Just phone me if you're at all nervous, Leslie—good night."
Coldwell's voice sounded from the hall; there was the slam of a door—the little yellow man, who spoke and understood English very well, did not smile to himself, because he was of a race that seldom smile.

===Chinese===
半小时后，在警察离开之前，科尔德威尔先生来到了现场。此时，附近所有的屋顶和院子都已被搜查过；夜间看守人被从偷偷摸摸的睡眠中叫醒，一小队警察侦探检查了每一扇可能提供逃跑通道的窗户。但没有发现入侵者的任何踪迹。
"我不喜欢这样，"莱斯利说。
科尔德威尔先生摇了摇头。
"你得另外找个地方住一段时间。明天你最好把你的东西和卢克丽霞转移到我在汉普斯特德（Hampstead）的房子里去。"
他低声讨论了他提出的理论和制定的计划，持续了五分钟。
"我认为没有必要在房子里留一个警察，"他最后说。这时，莱斯利房间高柜顶上蜷缩着的一个小黄种人松了一口气——他被衣柜上老式的飞檐挡住了视线，没有被发现。
他听到警察咔嗒咔嗒地走下楼梯，过了一会儿：
"如果你有任何不安，就给我打电话，莱斯利——晚安。"
科尔德威尔的声音从大厅传来；接着是门砰地关上的声音——那个小黄种人英语说得很好，也听得懂，但他没有暗自微笑，因为他属于一个很少微笑的种族。

===Original===
Leslie came into her bedroom with a yawn, gathered her sleeping things and disappeared into the bathroom. The listener heard the sound of running water, heard her bid a reassuring good night to the tremulous servant, and then the door of the bedroom opened and closed, the light was extinguished, there was the creak of a bed, and after a while the sound of deep, regular breathing.
For an hour the yellow man lay, not moving a muscle, and then, reaching up, he caught hold of the wooden moulding, testing its strength, and was satisfied. He felt the long, queer-shaped knife that was in his belt, running his thumb along the edge, before he gripped it between his teeth and, with the agility of a cat, and supported only by his sinewy fingers, drew himself clear of the wardrobe, and dropped noiselessly on to the carpet.
The wardrobe hardly creaked as he moved; save for the soft pad of his bare feet and the breathing of the sleeper, there was no sound. Holding the knife lightly in his right hand, he groped along the pillow with his left, ready to pounce upon and strangle the scream before it rose.

===Chinese===
莱斯利打着哈欠走进卧室，收拾好睡觉用的东西，消失在浴室里。偷听者听到流水声，听到她安慰那个颤抖的女仆道晚安，然后卧室的门开了又关上，灯熄灭了，床发出嘎吱声，过了一会儿传来深沉、有规律的呼吸声。
那个黄种人躺了一个小时，一动不动，然后他伸手抓住木质装饰条，测试其强度，感到满意。他摸了摸腰带里那把细长、形状古怪的刀，用拇指沿着刀刃试了试，然后把它叼在嘴里，以猫一般的敏捷，仅靠他强健的手指支撑，把自己从衣柜上移开，悄无声息地落在地毯上。
他移动时衣柜几乎没发出声响；除了他赤脚柔软的脚步声和沉睡者的呼吸声，没有任何声音。他右手轻轻握着刀，左手沿着枕头摸索，准备在尖叫发出之前就扑上去掐住它。

===Original===
There was no head on the first pillow, none on the right—the bed was empty. He straightened himself up quickly, half-turned as he heard a sound from behind him, but it was too late. An arm of steel flung round his throat, the knife hand was gripped at the wrist and twisted so sharply that the weapon fell to the floor.
"I want you!" It was Coldwell's voice.
He lifted the little figure without difficulty, and reached out his hand to turn on the light. At that moment the prisoner recovered himself, and with amazing strength twisted round to face the detective. Coldwell realized that he had in his hands something with the ferocity and suppleness of a wild cat, something that growled and clawed and kicked so that not a limb of him was still. The unexpectedness of that furious onslaught threw him for a second off his balance. He drove out with his right, but as though he could see in the dark, the assassin dodged, and in another second he was free and had flown through the open door. Coldwell followed, but too late. With one leap the little man crashed through sash and pane and dropped unharmed to the street below. A policeman made a dive at him, but he ducked, flew across the road, and disappeared down a court by the side of a theatre towards St. Martin's Lane.
"Didn't even see him," said Coldwell bitterly, when he called the girl in from Lucretia's room. The detective's face was scratched, his collar torn. "It was rather like tackling a young tiger."

===Chinese===
第一个枕头上没有头，右边也没有——床是空的。他迅速直起身来，听到身后有声音，半转过身，但为时已晚。一条钢铁般的手臂勒住了他的喉咙，握刀的手腕被抓住并猛地一扭，刀掉在了地上。
"我抓住你了！"是科尔德威尔的声音。
他毫不费力地举起了那个小个子，伸手去开灯。就在这时，囚犯回过神来，以惊人的力量扭转身面对侦探。科尔德威尔意识到他手中抓着的东西有着野猫般的凶猛和柔韧，那东西又抓又咬又踢，没有一处是安静的。这狂暴的攻击出其不意，使他一时失去了平衡。他右手击出，但那个刺客仿佛能在黑暗中视物，躲开了，一秒钟后他就挣脱了，冲出了敞开的门。科尔德威尔紧随其后，但太迟了。那个小个子纵身一跃，撞碎了窗框和玻璃，毫发无损地落到下面的街道上。一个警察朝他扑去，但他躲开了，飞速穿过马路，消失在剧院旁边通往圣马丁巷（St. Martin's Lane）的一条小巷里。
"连他的样子都没看清，"科尔德威尔苦涩地说，一边把女孩从卢克丽霞的房间叫出来。侦探的脸被抓伤了，领子被撕破了。"简直像是在对付一只小老虎。"

===Original===
Leslie had turned on the lights and they saw the extent of the damage. He must have dived for the lower sash head first, for the upper window was untouched. There was not a scrap of glass remaining, and the cross supports of wood were smashed to splinters.
"I've heard of such things being done," said Coldwell, "and I've seen them done—on the stage. But never in real life and through three-quarter inch moulding."
Leslie was still dressed. She had been waiting in the maid's room, a pistol on her lap, till the sound of the struggle brought her out, just too late. Mr. Coldwell disappeared into the bedroom and returned with the ugly and curious-shaped knife which the man had dropped.
"Eastern," he said, as he felt the edge gingerly. "Malayan, I guess."
He also had been sitting on a chair immediately to the right of the wardrobe, but until he made an examination later he had not known from what place his assailant had come.
"I thought he'd come back through the window," he mused. "That's one of the curiosities of human nature, Leslie; jot it down in your notebook. We always look under things for hidden criminals—we never look over; and yet the cleverest fellow that ever got away from the police was a steeplejack who hid for a fortnight at the top of a smoke stack. Ever wear garters, Leslie?"
She laughed softly.
"That sounds almost indelicate to me," she said. "No, I won't go very deeply into the question, but I don't wear garters."
He was quite serious.
"Wish you would—just to oblige me. One garter, anyhow. I meant to give it to you today."
He drew something out of his pocket, and she gasped.
"You really wish me to wear this?"
He nodded.
"A little heavy, but I wish you would," he said.

===Chinese===
莱斯利打开了灯，他们看到了损坏的程度。他一定是头朝下扑向下面的窗扇的，因为上面的窗户完好无损。没有一块玻璃剩下，木质的十字支撑被砸成了碎片。
"我听说过这种事情，"科尔德威尔说，"我也见过——在舞台上。但在现实生活中，而且是穿过四分之三英寸厚的装饰条，我从未见过。"
莱斯利还穿着衣服。她一直在女仆的房间里等着，腿上放着一把手枪，直到打斗声把她引出来，但为时已晚。科尔德威尔先生走进卧室，拿出了那个人掉落的那把丑陋而形状古怪的刀。
"东方的，"他小心翼翼地摸着刀刃说。"我猜是马来亚的。"
他后来检查时才知道，他的袭击者是从衣柜右边的一把椅子上出来的。
"我以为他会从窗户回来，"他沉思道。"这是人性的奇妙之处，莱斯利；记在你的笔记本上。我们总是往东西下面搜寻隐藏的罪犯——从不往上看；但有史以来最聪明的逃脱警察的人是一个烟囱修理工，他在烟囱顶上躲了两个星期。你戴吊袜带吗，莱斯利？"
她轻声笑了。
"这对我来说听起来几乎有点不雅，"她说。"不，我不想深究这个问题，但我不戴吊袜带。"
他非常认真。
"我希望你戴——就当是为了我。反正要一条。我本来打算今天给你的。"
他从口袋里掏出什么东西，她倒吸了一口气。
"你真的要我戴这个？"
他点了点头。
"有点重，但我希望你戴，"他说。

===Original===
He insisted upon staying the night, and to make doubly sure, had a policeman put on duty in the hall below. Early as the hour was when she went out to her bath, she found him up and dressed, studying the morning newspaper.
"Wonderful how you miss things when you're away from the Yard for a few hours," he drawled.
She turned back from the open door of the bathroom; when Mr. Coldwell drawled there was something sensational to come.
"What have we missed?" she asked. It was not entirely curiosity which made her ask.
He looked at the newspaper again and took off his glasses.
"Peter Dawlish was arrested last night."
She gazed at him in horror and amazement.
"Arrested? On what charge?"
"Threatening to murder Princess Anita Bellini," was the staggering reply.

===Chinese===
他坚持要留下来过夜，为了确保万无一失，还安排了一个警察在楼下大厅值班。尽管她起床去洗澡的时间很早，却发现他已经起床穿好衣服，在看晨报了。
"离开苏格兰场几个小时就会错过这么多事，真是不可思议，"他拖着长腔说。
她从浴室敞开的门边转过身来；当科尔德威尔先生拖着长腔说话时，通常会有惊人之事。
"我们错过了什么？"她问道。促使她问这个问题的并不完全是好奇。
他再次看了看报纸，摘下了眼镜。
"彼得·道利什昨晚被捕了。"
她惊恐而惊讶地注视着他。
"被捕了？什么罪名？"
"威胁谋杀安妮塔·贝里尼公主，"这令人震惊的回答道。

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
Mrs. Greta Gurden seldom permitted herself the luxury of brooding upon her injuries. She was no philosopher, and it was sheer necessity which made her disregard the irritations, petty and great, of life, and concentrate her mind upon pleasant things. But she found herself helpless with a leg that throbbed and throbbed, and the memory of Anita Bellini's insolence rankled as sorely. She was propped up in bed with a heap of papers on her lap, and though there was no immediate need for the work she had taken in hand, and, in truth, sought it only as a relief from boredom, she permitted herself the illusion that she was the victim of a taskmistress who was not satisfied with her normal and heavy exactions, but must needs add to her offence this tormentation of a sick woman.

===Chinese===
格蕾塔·格登夫人（Mrs. Greta Gurden）很少让自己沉溺于咀嚼所受委屈的奢侈中。她不是什么哲学家，纯粹是迫于生计才无视生活中大大小小的烦扰，把心思放在愉快的事情上。但她此刻无能为力，腿伤阵阵作痛，安妮塔·贝里尼（Anita Bellini）的傲慢无礼也像扎在肉里的刺一般令人耿耿于怀。她靠坐在床上，膝上堆着一摞文件，虽说手头的活计并无急迫之处，她做这些不过是为了打发无聊，却还是说服自己相信，她是一个苛刻女主人的受害者——那人连正常繁重的差事都不满足，非要再加一项折磨病人的勾当。

===Original===
Old letters, old bills, a receipt or two, a few ancient telegrams about nothing in particular, dozens of letters dealing earnestly with forgotten accounts, an interminable correspondence between Anita and a house agent—she turned the pages one by one, sorting the sheep from the goats.
Presently she came to an old letter typewritten on plain paper—Anita, like her dependent, used a small portable typewriter for years. The letter was unfinished; halfway through the Princess had changed her mind, or probably substituted this for another, and had tossed the rejected scrap aside, to be gathered to the heap which had accumulated and was now being sorted.

===Chinese===
旧信件、旧账单、一两收据、几封年代久远且无关紧要的电报、数十封认真处理早已遗忘账目的信函、安妮塔与房产中介之间没完没了的通信——她一页页翻过去，把有用的挑出来，没用的丢一边。
翻着翻着，她看到一封用普通纸张打印的旧信——安妮塔和她的依附者一样，多年来一直用一台小型便携打字机。信没写完，中途公主改了主意，或许是用另一封替掉了这封，然后把这页废纸随手一扔，混入了堆积如山的文件堆里，如今她正在这些文件中翻拣。

===Original===
She read the letter through as far as it went; she was sourly amused. Anita must have been in a careless mood when she threw this away. The old instinct of service told her that it ought to be destroyed at once; she gripped the paper to tear it, thought better of her impulse, and began to consider certain possibilities. To say that she felt bitterly against Anita Bellini at that moment would be to grade her emotion charitably. She was "getting old," was she? She had lost her looks and was unlikely to get a job in the chorus. Anita had taken it for granted that she would be forever satisfied with the humiliating position of companion. Capri was to be a kind of bonus.

===Chinese===
她把信从头读到能读的地方；心中涌起一股酸涩的快意。安妮塔扔掉这封信时一定心不在焉。多年当佣人的本能告诉她应该立刻毁掉它；她攥紧纸张想要撕毁，转念一想又改变了主意，开始琢磨某些可能性。此刻要说她对安妮塔·贝里尼心怀怨恨，那都是往轻了说。她"老了"，是吗？她已经失去了美貌，也不可能在歌舞团找到工作了。安妮塔理所当然地认为她会永远满足于当个屈辱的陪侍。卡普里岛（Capri）之行不过是给她的额外奖赏罢了。

===Original===
The Princess was a woman of temperament, sometimes feverishly elated, sometimes savagely depressed. Yet in all her permutations of mood, she had been consistently contemptuous of her hireling. Greta grew red and hot and cold at the memory of the insults which this woman had heaped upon her, and the hand that held the letter shook. And then an idea began to take shape in her mind; it was half formed when she called Mrs. Hobbs.
"Get my address book."

===Chinese===
这位公主是个情绪化的人，时而亢奋得近乎癫狂，时而暴躁地陷入低落。然而无论情绪如何起伏，她对雇来的仆人始终如一地轻蔑。想到这个女人对自己百般羞辱，格蕾塔的脸一阵红一阵白一阵青，拿着信的手也颤抖起来。一个念头开始在她脑中成形；还没完全想清楚，她就叫来了霍布斯夫人（Mrs. Hobbs）。
"把我通讯录拿来。"

===Original===
She was a systematic woman, and entered without fail the location even of chance acquaintances who might be of no value to her. She ran her thumb down the index till it stopped at D; the last entry on the crowded page was "Peter Dawlish."
"Give me an envelope, please, and my fountain pen; and take this letter to the post—no, bring my little typewriter."
The obedient Mrs. Hobbs carried the tiny machine, which was a replica of Anita's, and laid it on the invalid's lap. Greta inserted the envelope, typed the address, and while the instrument was being removed, inserted the torn sheet of paper and licked down the flap of the envelope.
"Go to the General Post Office—you'd better take a bus each way—and post this. If anybody asks you whether you've posted a letter for me, you're to say no."
It was not the first time Mrs. Hobbs had received similar instructions.

===Chinese===
她是个做事有条理的人，即使是偶然结识且可能毫无利用价值的人，她也会把对方的地址一笔不落地记下来。她用拇指顺着索引翻到D；那页密密麻麻的最后一个条目是"彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish）"。
"给我拿个信封，还有我的钢笔；把这封信拿去寄——不，先把我的小打字机拿来。"
顺从的霍布斯夫人搬来了那台小巧的打字机——是安妮塔那台的复制品——放在病人腿上。格蕾塔装好信封，打上地址，趁机器被搬走的当口，把撕下来的那张纸塞进去，舔湿信封封口粘好。
"去邮局——最好来回都坐公交车——把这封信寄了。要是有人问你替我寄过信没有，你就说没有。"
霍布斯夫人已不是第一次接到类似的指示了。

===Original===
The houses in Severall Street are not equipped with letter-boxes, and postmen have learnt by experience that inserting letters under doors which are backed by coarse fibre mats is a difficult and sometimes an impossible proposition.
Peter heard the heavy *rat-tat* of the postman, and, going downstairs, opened the door.
"Dawlish?" asked the postman.
"That is my name," said Peter, in surprise. He took the letter and closed the door. Had he followed the practice of Severall Street and its people, which is never to go to the door without making a scrutiny up and down the street, he could not have failed to see Leslie on her way home.

===Chinese===
西弗拉尔街（Severall Street）的房子没有信箱，邮差凭经验知道，把信塞进背面垫着粗纤维门垫的门缝是一项艰难且有时根本不可能的任务。
彼得听到邮差沉重的"咚咚"敲门声，下楼打开了门。
"道利什？"邮差问。
"正是我的名字。"彼得惊讶地说。他接过信，关上了门。要是他遵循西弗拉尔街居民的习惯——每次走到门口都要先左右张望一番，他不可能看不到正走在回家路上的莱斯利（Leslie）。

===Original===
His first thought was that it was a letter from her, but when he brought it to the light of his room, he saw that it was typewritten and had been posted in the City. He opened the envelope and took out a sheet of typewriting paper. It was discoloured, and one corner had been torn off. He looked at the date and had a mild shock.
"July 7th, 1916."

===Chinese===
他第一个念头是她写来的信，但拿到房间灯光下一看，发现是打字的，寄自伦敦市区。他拆开信封，抽出一张打字纸。纸张已经发黄，一个角被撕掉了。他看了看日期，微微吃了一惊。
"一九一六年七月七日。"

===Original===
1916! And yet (as he saw) it had been posted that afternoon. There were just three or four lines, the last of which stopped abruptly in the middle of a sentence. Only dimly did he comprehend the significance of the fragment.
"Dear Jane,
"Druze has found a very good home for your son in a middle-class family. There are no other children. He will be well cared for. And—"
Scribbled below in pencil, and almost indecipherable, were the words: "Martha's servant."

===Chinese===
一九一六年！然而（他看到）这封信是当天下午才寄出的。只有寥寥三四行，最后一行戛然断在一个句子中间。他只是隐约意识到这段残文的分量。
"亲爱的简：
"德鲁兹为你儿子找了一个很好的中产家庭寄养。那家没有其他孩子。他会得到很好的照顾。而——"
下方用铅笔潦草地写了几个几乎无法辨认的字："玛莎的仆人。"

===Original===
He must have read the letter a dozen times before he understood.
"Jane's son—Jane's little son." He came to his feet slowly, his limbs trembling, the paper swimming before his eyes.
Jane's son—his son! The consciousness of fatherhood momentarily overwhelmed him. Jane had had a child. He had never dreamt—somewhere in the world was a little boy, fatherless—his little boy! He grew hot at the thought. And then, in a frenzy of imputeness, he took up his coat, struggled into it, and, not stopping to extinguish the lamp, ran down the stairs and out of the house.

===Chinese===
他一定把这封信读了十几遍才明白过来。
"简的儿子——简的小儿子。"他慢慢站起身，四肢发颤，信纸在眼前模糊成一片。
简的儿子——他的儿子！为人父的意识瞬间将他淹没。简有过一个孩子。他做梦也想不到——世上的某个角落有一个小男孩，没有父亲——他的小男孩！想到这里他浑身燥热。紧接着，他急不可耐地抓起外套，手忙脚乱地套上，连灯都没顾得上灭，就跑下楼梯冲出了家门。

===Original===
The bus that carried him to Piccadilly seemed to crawl. He got down at a traffic block at Bond Street, half walked, half ran, into Berkeley Street, and came at last to the dark portals of Lady Raytham's house. It was past ten. She might be out. But he would wait for her—all night if necessary. He hated her at that moment, and there was jealousy behind the hate. He hated her for not telling him, for excluding him from the knowledge and inspiration of their gift. Perhaps he was being brought up as Raytham's child, to call him "father." Peter grew insanely furious at the thought.
To the new butler who opened the door all callers were as yet strange; Peter seemed no stranger than others, and he was met civilly.
"What name shall I tell her ladyship?" he asked.
"Mr. Peter," said Peter, after thought.

===Chinese===
载他去皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）的公交车似乎在爬行。他在邦德街（Bond Street）的一处堵车点下了车，半走半跑地穿过伯克利街（Berkeley Street），终于来到了雷瑟姆夫人宅邸幽暗的大门前。已经过了十点。她可能不在家。但他会等她——等一整夜也在所不惜。那一刻他恨她，恨意背后还藏着嫉妒。他恨她不告诉他，恨她把他排除在那份礼物——他们的骨肉——之外。也许那孩子被当作了雷瑟姆的孩子养大，管他叫"爸爸"。想到这里彼得怒不可遏。
新来的管家对所有访客都还不熟悉；彼得看起来并不比别人更可疑，因此受到了礼貌的接待。
"请问贵姓，我好向夫人通报？"他问道。
"彼得先生（Mr. Peter）。"彼得想了想说。

===Original===
He was shown into the small drawing-room, and paced up and down like a caged animal until he heard the door open and, turning, met face to face, for the first time in eight years, the woman of The Adventure.
She was pale but very calm and sure of herself as she closed the door behind her. For a while they stood, looking at one another. She had matured, grown more beautiful; the old graceful carriage was unchanged; the enticing lines of her had come to a greater perfection. He had grown older, she thought; was much more of a man than when she had known him before. His face had formed; resolution and strength and a balance that had been missing; in his eyes she read something that chilled her.

===Chinese===
他被领进了小客厅，像一头困兽般来回踱步，直到听见门开了，他转过身，与那个奇遇中的女人面对面地重逢了——时隔八年。
她脸色苍白，但非常沉着，关上门时显得从容不迫。两人对视了片刻。她已成熟，更加美丽；往昔优雅的仪态依旧；那诱人的身段愈发完美。他老了，她想；比她记忆中的样子更有男子气概。他的面部轮廓分明了；多了一份此前所欠缺的果决、力量和沉稳；她在他眼中读到了某种令她不寒而栗的东西。

===Original===
"You wish to see me—Peter?" she asked.
He nodded.
He was trembling; feared to speak lest his voice betrayed him.
"What is it you wish to see me about?"
"I want my child." His voice was low; the words seemed to choke him, so that he ended on a cough.
"You want—your child?"
She shook her head so slightly that if he had not been watching her closely the gesture would have escaped him.
"Will you tell me what you mean?"
She was fencing. She wanted time to take all this in. He had shocked her very badly.

===Chinese===
"你想见我——彼得？"她问道。
他点了点头。
他在发抖；不敢开口，唯恐声音出卖了自己。
"你想跟我谈什么事？"
"我要我的孩子。"他的声音很低；这句话似乎噎住了他，最后以一声咳嗽收尾。
"你要——你的孩子？"
她的头微微摇了一下，幅度之小，若非他紧盯着她，几乎察觉不到。
"你能告诉我你什么意思吗？"
她在闪避。她需要时间消化这一切。他的话给了她极大的震动。

===Original===
"Why pretend, Jane? You know what I want, and what I mean. Where is our child?"
She passed her hand wearily across her eyes.
"I don't know," she said. She made no attempt to evade the question, accepted his knowledge, startling as it was. "I don't know. Is it worth while knowing? He is very happy. I did what was best, Peter. I told nobody. When I went to Reno—"
"You have divorced me?"

===Chinese===
"何必装糊涂，简？你知道我想要什么，也知道我在说什么。我们的孩子在哪里？"
她疲惫地用手揉了揉眼睛。
"我不知道。"她说。她没有试图回避这个问题，坦然接受了他知道真相的事实——尽管这令人震惊。"我不知道。有必要知道吗？他过得很好。我做了最好的安排，彼得。我没有告诉任何人。我去里诺（Reno）的时候——"
"你跟我离婚了？"

===Original===
She did not answer. A lie trembled on her lips and was instantly rejected impatiently.
"No, I have not divorced you," she said. "They would not grant me a divorce because you had not been served with the papers or something of the sort. I don't understand the law very well. I was a fool, of course."
Another intense silence.
"That puts me in your hand, doesn't it?" she went on. "Though I don't imagine you will—"

===Chinese===
她没有回答。一个谎言在她唇边犹豫了一瞬，随即被不耐烦地否决了。
"不，我没有跟你离婚，"她说，"他们不批准我的离婚申请，因为你没有收到传票之类的东西。我不太懂法律。我当然是个傻瓜。"
又是一阵令人窒息的沉默。
"这下我落在你手心里了，是吧？"她接着说，"虽然我不认为你会——"

===Original===
He stopped her with an impatient gesture.
"I'm not thinking of you and I'm not thinking of me," he said. "I am thinking of the boy. Jane, you horrify me! You don't know where your own child—! Good God! I thought he might not be here, but that you should tell me so quietly and calmly that you've lost track of him—as if he were a—"
She shook her head.

===Chinese===
他不耐烦地一挥手，打断了她。
"我想的不是你，也不是我自己，"他说，"我在想那孩子。简，你让我毛骨悚然！你竟然不知道自己孩子的下落——！老天！我想到他可能不在这儿，但你竟然如此平静地告诉我你跟他断了联系——就好像他是一——"
她摇了摇头。

===Original===
"I don't know. Honestly, Peter, I don't know. I was terrified when I knew he was coming. I just dimly remember seeing the little thing, and then they took him away—we had arranged it beforehand."
"Who are 'we'?"
"Anita was very good to me, and so was Druze. It was only then I discovered that Druze was a woman. I had to pay for it afterwards—Druze's knowledge, I mean. I don't really remember the child—only just that vague, queer impression like the elusive memory of a dream. Peter, be a little pitiful. I was in a terrible condition; my father was writing asking me to make up my mind about Raytham. You knew he wanted to marry me? Raytham had lent father a lot of money, and I was afraid, terribly afraid, of what would happen if father came to learn—about the marriage and everything. He knew I'd been to America, of course; I was supposed to have taken an engagement to sing—you remember that, don't you, Peter? But he didn't know I'd returned, or what had become of me. I had to send all my letters to a friend in New York to be posted back to him."

===Chinese===
"我不知道。真的，彼得，我不知道。知道自己要生了的时候我吓坏了。我只隐约记得看了那小东西一眼，然后他们就把他带走了——我们事先安排好的。"
"'我们'是谁？"
"安妮塔对我很好，德鲁兹也是。直到那时我才发现德鲁兹是个女人。后来我不得不为此付出代价——我是说为德鲁兹的知情。我真的不太记得那孩子——只有一种模糊的、奇怪的印象，像一个转瞬即逝的梦。彼得，你可怜可怜我吧。我当时处境很糟；我父亲写信来催我拿定主意关于雷瑟姆的事。你知道他想让我嫁给他吗？雷瑟姆借给父亲一大笔钱，我害怕极了，非常害怕父亲知道——知道婚事和其他一切。他当然知道我去过美国；我假装接了个演唱合约——你还记得吧，彼得？但他不知道我回来了，也不知道我的遭遇。我不得不把所有寄给他的信都先寄到纽约一个朋友那里，再由朋友转寄回去。"

===Original===
She stopped.
"Where is the child? That is all I want to know."
She shook her head.
"Druze knew. She told me something just before she went out—she had been drinking, Peter. She told me a ghastly thing." Her voice broke. "Terrible, terrible!" She covered her eyes again, and he waited, his heart a heavy stone.

===Chinese===
她停了下来。
"孩子在哪里？我只想知道这个。"
她摇了摇头。
"德鲁兹知道。她临死前不久告诉我一件事——她喝醉了，彼得。她告诉我一件可怕的事。"她的声音哽咽了。"可怕，太可怕了！"她再次捂住眼睛，他等着，心像一块沉甸甸的石头。

===Original===
"This ghastly thing—what was it?" he said at last.
"She said"—this needed courage to think; it was a torture to say—"that even she didn't know where the child was; that she had handed the boy to the first person who, for a consideration, offered to adopt it; and all the time I had been comforting myself with the thought that—that he at least was being brought up happily, however much a blackguard his foster-father was."
"What do you mean?" he demanded.
"I've been paying money, big sums of money," she said at last, "as I supposed to the man who had adopted him, and who, learning of my marriage to Raytham, had for years blackmailed me. Too late I discovered that this blackmailer was mythical, that it was Druze who was robbing me all the time."

===Chinese===
"那件可怕的事——是什么？"他终于开口。
"她说"——想到这件事需要勇气；说出口更是一种折磨——"连她也不知道孩子在哪里；她把男孩交给了第一个出钱要收养他的人；而我一直在安慰自己，说——说至少他被养得很好，不管他的养父是个多么卑劣的人。"
"你什么意思？"他追问。
"我一直在付钱，大笔大笔的钱，"她终于说道，"我以为是付给那个收养了他的人，那人得知我和雷瑟姆结婚后，多年来一直在勒索我。后来我才发现，那个勒索者根本不存在，一直是德鲁兹在偷我的钱。"

===Original===
Peter drew a deep breath.
"How awful! How perfectly awful!" he whispered. "Just disappeared into the mass—and you allowed him to go. I can't understand that. I thought that women—"
She stopped him with a weary gesture.

===Chinese===
彼得深深地吸了一口气。
"太可怕了！简直太可怕了！"他低声说，"就这样消失在茫茫人海——而你就由他去了。我无法理解。我以为女人——"
她疲惫地一摆手，打断了他。

===Original===
"I don't understand women either. I wish I'd kept him and had faced all the trouble that would have followed. You know about it for the first time, Peter, and you have the support of your righteousness. It has been a bad dream for me—an eight-year-long discomfort. And now it is a nightmare." She pressed her throbbing temples. "I can't sleep for thinking of him. That little mite of a boy—my boy and yours—perhaps being starved, or dead perhaps, or suffering."
She screwed her eyes tight as though to shut out a horrible vision.

===Chinese===
"我也不了解女人。我真希望当初留下了他，面对随之而来的一切麻烦。你是第一次知道这件事，彼得，你有正义感做后盾。这对我来说一直是一场噩梦——长达八年的不适。而现在它变成了真正的噩梦。"她按住疼痛的太阳穴。"我一想到他就睡不着觉。那个小男孩——你我的孩子——也许正在挨饿，也许已经死了，也许在受苦。"
她紧紧闭上眼睛，仿佛要将可怕的幻象挡在外面。

===Original===
"Does Bellini know?" He was like ice now.
"Anita?" She looked at him in surprise. "No; why should she? You hate Anita, of course. I'm not really—fond of her. She's difficult. But she was very helpful to me, Peter."
He looked at her steadily.
"Who was Martha?"
He saw from her frown that she did not understand him.

===Chinese===
"贝里尼知道吗？"此刻他的语气冷若冰霜。
"安妮塔？"她惊讶地看着他，"不；她为什么会知道？你当然恨安妮塔。我也不是——很喜欢她。她很难相处。但她对我帮助很大，彼得。"
他定定地看着她。
"玛莎是谁？"
从她紧锁的眉头他看出她不明白他在说什么。

===Original===
"Do you know a woman called Martha?"
She shook her head.
"I don't remember anybody of that name. Why?"
"Martha's servant had the child. Bellini knows. And what Bellini knows, I will know."
He made as though to leave the room, but she barred the way.
"Peter, will you forgive me? I've been a fool—a wicked fool, Peter. I'd gladly change places with my own kitchen-maid to undo all the past. You loathe me, don't you?"
"No, I don't loathe you," he said quietly. "I'm awfully sorry for you in a way; but I'm disappointed in you too, Jane. You've been a weakling."
"Have I? I suppose I have." She saw him, a blurred figure, through a mist of tears. "I suppose I have. And one pays dearer for weakness than for wickedness, I think. Where are you going?"

===Chinese===
"你认识一个叫玛莎的女人吗？"
她摇了摇头。
"我不记得有这个人。为什么？"
"玛莎的仆人收养了那孩子。贝里尼知道。而贝里尼知道的事，我会查出来。"
他作势要走，她却挡住了去路。
"彼得，你能原谅我吗？我是个傻瓜——一个可恶的傻瓜，彼得。我宁愿跟厨房女佣互换身份来弥补过去的一切。你恨我，对吧？"
"不，我不恨你，"他平静地说，"我为你感到万分难过，从某种意义上说；但我也对你很失望，简。你太软弱了。"
"是吗？我想是的。"透过盈眶的泪水，她看到他模糊的身影。"我想是的。软弱的代价比邪恶更大，我想。你去哪儿？"

===Original===
"I'm going to find the child."
She threw out her arms in a gesture of despair.
"Find the child! If only you could! Peter, if you could bring him to me—"
"You!" He laughed harshly. "The child belongs to me! To me—do you hear? You had him and lost him. If I find him I will keep him."
He brushed past her, threw open the door, and stalked through the hall into the night.

===Chinese===
"我要去找那个孩子。"
她绝望地张开双臂。
"去找那个孩子！只要你能找到！彼得，如果你能把他带回来——"
"你！"他冷笑一声，"那孩子是我的！是我的——你听见没有？你生了他，又弄丢了他。如果我找到他，我会留下他。"
他擦身而过，拉开门，大步穿过门厅，走进了夜色中。

===Original===
He had still the greater part of the twenty pounds left that Leslie had given to him, and at this moment of crisis he must spend; he could not afford to economize. A taxi-driver accepted with some reluctance his order to drive to Wimbledon Common. It was a long journey, and he had time to put in order the confusion of his mind.
Anita Bellini knew; he was confident of that. And if she knew, he should know. Her residence was a mansion standing in two acres of ground on the fashionable side of Wimbledon Common; a big, somewhat old-fashioned house, garnished with the square towers and big Gothic turrets which were the joy of the Victorian architects. It had something of a medieval appearance, and seemed to be a veritable castle of despair when he ordered the cab to wait. The cautious man demanded something on account, and wisely, as it proved.

===Chinese===
莱斯利给他的二十英镑还剩大半，此刻正处于危急关头，他必须花钱；不能再节省了。一个出租车司机勉勉强强地接受了他去温布尔登公地（Wimbledon Common）的订单。路途遥远，他有时间理清纷乱的思绪。
安妮塔·贝里尼一定知道；他对此很有把握。如果她知道，他就必须知道。她的住所是温布尔登公地时髦一侧占地两英亩的一座豪宅；一栋颇为老旧的大房子，装饰着方形塔楼和巨大的哥特式角楼，那是维多利亚时代建筑师们引以为豪的元素。它颇有几分中世纪城堡的气派，当他吩咐出租车在门口等候时，它简直成了一座名副其实的绝望堡垒。那个谨慎的司机要求先预付部分车费，事后证明这是明智之举。

===Original===
He strode up the gravelled drive. No light showed in any window; even the transom above the massive front door was lifeless. He pulled the bell and the faint clang of it came back to him. After a long time he heard the rattle of chains, the shooting back of a bolt, and a faint light was reflected behind the fanlight. The door was opened a few inches by a very old man with dirty white hair and wearing the slovenly uniform of a footman. Peter saw that the longer chain was still fastened to the door, and that the aperture was not big enough to squeeze through.

===Chinese===
他大步走上碎石车道。没有一扇窗户亮着灯；连厚重前门上方的气窗也毫无生气。他拉响了门铃，微弱的铃声隐隐传来。过了好一阵子，他听到铁链哗啦作响，门闩咔嚓滑开，半圆形的气窗后映出一线微光。一个非常年迈的男人开了门，只开了几英寸。老人头发花白脏污，穿着邋遢的男仆制服。彼得看到长铁链仍然拴在门上，缝隙窄得根本钻不进去。

===Original===
"You're Simms, aren't you?" He remembered the ancient. "I want to see the Princess."
The old man made the grimace that Peter remembered.
"You can't see the Princess; she's not at home," he said, in a loud, cracked voice.
"Tell her Peter Dawlish wishes to see her, and if she will not let me in she can come to the door," he said.

===Chinese===
"你是西姆斯（Simms），对吧？"他认出了这个老仆。"我要见公主。"
老人做了个彼得记忆中熟悉的鬼脸。
"你不能见公主，她不在家。"他用沙哑的大嗓门说。
"告诉她彼得·道利什想见她，如果她不肯让我进去，就让她到门口来。"他说。

===Original===
He was not prepared to have the door slammed in his face, yet that was what happened. He waited for five minutes, and then he heard the lock turn. This time he saw Anita. She wore a long green dress, smothered as usual with beading which glittered in the dim hall light.
"What do you want?" she asked.
"I want to speak to you privately."
"This is as private an interview as you'll get," she said coolly.
The reflection of the hall light on her monocle produced an eerie illusion. It was as though she was glaring at him with one malignant, golden eye.

===Chinese===
他没料到门会砰地一声关上，但事实正是如此。他等了五分钟，然后听到锁转动的声音。这次他看到了安妮塔。她穿着一件绿色长裙，一如既往地缀满珠饰，在昏暗的门厅灯光下闪闪发亮。
"你想干什么？"她问。
"我想跟你单独谈。"
"这已经是你能得到的最私密的谈话了。"她冷冷地说。
门厅的灯光映在她的单片眼镜上，产生了一种诡异的幻觉。仿佛她正用一只邪恶的金色眼睛瞪着他。

===Original===
"What do you want?" she repeated. "If it's money, you can't have it. This is not a charitable institution or a home for convicts."
In the pause that followed he made a mental calculation as to the strength of the chain that held him from admission. He might at a pinch break it and force an entrance—he was prepared to go to any mad lengths to get the information he sought.
"Where is my child?" he asked.

===Chinese===
"你想干什么？"她重复道，"要钱的话，门都没有。这里不是慈善机构，也不是罪犯收容所。"
在接下来的沉默中，他在心里盘算着那根把他挡在门外的铁链的强度。紧急情况下他可以弄断它强行闯入——为了得到他想要的信息，他愿意不择手段。
"我的孩子在哪里？"他问。

===Original===
Not a muscle of the big face moved.
"I didn't know you'd been raising a family. Surely I'm the last person in the world to be acquainted with your vicarious progeny."
"Where is Jane's child? Perhaps you'll understand that."
She had been taken aback by the first question, he was sure. The length of time that elapsed before she answered betrayed her.
"So you know that, do you? The child? I'm afraid I can't tell you. I have something better to do than to keep track of the indiscretions of my friends, and certainly I do not concern myself with the bastards of convicted forgers."

===Chinese===
那张大脸上的肌肉纹丝不动。
"我还不知道你已经在养儿育女了。我恐怕是世界上最不可能了解你那些冒牌后代的人了。"
"简的孩子在哪里？也许这样你就听懂了。"
他确信第一个问题让她措手不及。她迟迟才开口，出卖了她。
"你知道了，是吗？那个孩子？恐怕我无可奉告。我有比追踪朋友们的风流韵事更重要的事要做，而且我当然不会关心一个被判有罪的伪造犯的私生子。"

===Original===
"You lie," said Peter quietly. "You know I was married to Jane."
Anita Bellini chuckled.
"The marriage was illegal—didn't you know that? You didn't comply with certain formalities—"
"I have seen Jane tonight. She has no doubt about its legality. Where is my son?"
"Where you will never find him." All the pent-up malignity of the woman suddenly took expression. Her face, never attractive, was contorted by rage to an appearance that was almost ludicrous. "Where you will never find him! In the slime and the mud where his father belongs—dead, I hope!"

===Chinese===
"你撒谎，"彼得平静地说，"你知道我和简结过婚。"
安妮塔·贝里尼咯咯笑了。
"那婚姻是非法的——你不知道吗？你没有履行某些手续——"
"我今晚见过简了。她对婚姻的合法性毫无疑问。我的儿子在哪里？"
"在一个你永远找不到的地方。"女人积压已久的恶毒终于爆发出来。她那张本就不讨喜的脸因愤怒而扭曲到近乎滑稽的程度。"在一个你永远找不到的地方！在他父亲所属的污泥浊水中——我巴不得他已经死了！"

===Original===
A sudden insane fury possessed him. He was scarcely human, saw the hateful face of this woman through a redness, and flung himself against the door. It jerked back with a crash and suddenly flew open. The chain was broken.
To him she was no longer a woman, but some obscene devil that had taken human shape. He wanted to kill her, to grip that big throat and choke the life out of her. As the chain broke, she stepped back, and he found himself looking into the black muzzle of a pistol.
"Don't move," she said gratingly. "Don't move, Peter Dawlish. I am justified in shooting you in defence of my life."

===Chinese===
一股疯狂的暴怒突然攫住了他。他几乎不成人形，眼前一片血红，看不清那张可憎的脸。他猛地撞向门。门被撞得咣当一声弹回，随即哗啦打开。铁链断了。
在他眼中，她已不再是女人，而是一个化作人形的淫邪恶魔。他想杀了她，掐住那粗壮的喉咙把她掐死。铁链断裂时她向后退了一步，他发现自己正对着一支手枪黑洞洞的枪口。
"别动，"她刺耳地说，"别动，彼得·道利什。我有权为了自卫而开枪打死你。"

===Original===
She did not see his hand move. The pistol was struck down from her grip and fell with a clatter on the floor, and, in his mad anger, with murder in his heart, his hand was outflung. Then somebody called him.
"Peter!"
At the sound of the voice his arm dropped, paralyzed with amazement. A woman was in the hall; she had come out of a room at the foot of the broad stairway; a woman in black silk, white-haired, hard-faced—it was his mother!
"Come in here."

===Chinese===
她没看到他的手怎么动的。手枪从她手中被击落，哐啷一声掉在地上。在疯狂的暴怒中，杀意充塞胸膛，他的手已挥了出去。这时有人喊他。
"彼得！"
听到这个声音，他的手臂骤然垂下，惊愕得浑身瘫软。一个女人站在门厅里；她从宽阔楼梯底部的一个房间里走了出来；一个穿着黑色丝绸衣服、白发苍苍、面容冷峻的女人——是他的母亲！
"进来。"

===Original===
She pointed to the open door of the room, and he walked past her without another glance at Anita Bellini—shrinking back against the wall, frightened for the first time in her life.
It was a small study furnished in the Oriental fashion; there was a great silken divan, and a shaded lantern hung from the ceiling. Something more modern he saw; a telephone on the tiny octagonal table. The receiver was off; he had interrupted her in the act of telephoning.
"What is the meaning of this?"

===Chinese===
她指着敞开的房门，他从她身边走过，再没看安妮塔·贝里尼一眼——她正贴着墙壁退缩，有生以来第一次感到了恐惧。
这是一间以东方风格装饰的小书房；有一张宽大的丝绸沙发，天花板上悬着一盏带灯罩的灯笼。他还看到了一样更现代的东西；八角小桌上放着一部电话。听筒还搁在一边；他打断了她打电话。
"这是什么意思？"

===Original===
Mrs. Dawlish had assumed the old pontifical air that he knew so well and detested so much.
He was still shaking, but he was calmer.
"I presume you don't need to be told—you must have heard. I came to your friend—"
"To the Princess Bellini," interrupted the woman. "Yes?"
"—to discover where was my child."
"Really?" The grey eyebrows rose, "I was not aware that I was a grandmother."
The old devil rose again in him.

===Chinese===
道利什夫人（Mrs. Dawlish）摆出了那副他再熟悉不过却深恶痛绝的傲慢架势。
他还在发抖，但已经平静了许多。
"我想不需要我多说了——你一定听到了。我来找你的朋友——"
"找贝里尼公主，"女人打断道，"怎么了？"
"——是为了打听我孩子的下落。"
"真的吗？"灰白的眉毛挑了起来，"我还不知道自己当了祖母呢。"
他心中的那个老恶魔又冒了出来。

===Original===
"Then your hearing is affected," he said harshly. "You know—of course you know! The whole damned gang of you know! You know about Jane, you know about my marriage, you know about the child. Perhaps you know where he is."
And then, to add to the fire of his fury, he saw her smile.
"You have always been a fool, Peter. I suppose you will be a fool to the end of your days," she said. "You had better go back to your envelope addressing and forget there are such things in the world as children. I have been trying very hard to do the same for the past seven years."
She was a surprising woman, for without warning, she came back to the offer she had made to him.

===Chinese===
"那你耳朵有问题，"他厉声说，"你知道——你当然知道！你们这群该死的家伙全都知道！你知道简的事，知道我的婚姻，知道那个孩子。也许你也知道他在哪里。"
接着，令他的怒火更加炽烈的是，他看到她笑了。
"你一直是个傻瓜，彼得。我想你会傻到死的那天，"她说，"你最好回去干你那信封地址的活儿，忘了这世上还有孩子这种东西。过去七年里我一直在努力做到这一点。"
她是个令人意想不到的女人，因为她毫无征兆地又回到了曾经向他提出的那个提议。

===Original===
"You would be well advised to go to Canada or Australia, or any other place that takes your fancy," she said, and went on in a conversational tone to discuss the advantages which might accrue.
He was puzzled. Then it occurred to him that she was talking to gain time—for what? His back had been to the door, and now he edged round until it was under his view. But if Anita Bellini contemplated any treachery, there was no visible or audible evidence.

===Chinese===
"你最好去加拿大或澳大利亚，或者任何你中意的地方，"她说，接着用聊天般的口吻讨论起可能获得的好处。
他困惑了。然后他想到她是在拖延时间——为了什么？他一直背对着门，现在他悄悄挪动身体，直到能把门纳入视线。但安妮塔·贝里尼似乎没有任何图谋的迹象，既看不到也听不到任何蛛丝马迹。

===Original===
He heard the front door bell ring, and an exchange of voices in the hall, and then the door opened, and two men entered, and it was not necessary that he should be very experienced in such matters to realize that they were detectives. His mother's narrative stopped automatically. Her white, skinny finger pointed to him.
"This man is Peter Dawlish—an ex-convict!" she said. "I charge him with threatening to murder my friend, Princess Anita Bellini."
A quarter of an hour after, the taxicab which Peter had employed to bring him to Wimbledon deposited him at the police station, and he was sitting, dazed and wrathful, behind the locked door of a police cell.

===Chinese===
他听到前门门铃响了，门厅里传来几声交谈，接着门开了，两个男人走了进来——即便在这类事上没什么经验的人也能看出他们是侦探。他母亲的叙述自动停了下来。她那干瘦的白手指向他。
"这个人是彼得·道利什——一个前科犯！"她说，"我控告他威胁要谋杀我的朋友，安妮塔·贝里尼公主。"
一刻钟后，彼得搭乘来温布尔登的出租车将他送到了警察局，他神情恍惚、满腔怒火地坐在一扇锁着的牢房门后面。

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===
"I can't believe it."

Leslie stared at the inspector.

"His own mother charged him? How monstrous!"

Mr. Coldwell had reached an age where it was almost impossible to surprise him.

===Chinese===
"我简直不敢相信。"

莱斯利瞪着探长。

"亲生母亲告发自己的儿子？太骇人听闻了！"

科尔德威尔先生（Mr. Coldwell）已经到了几乎不可能被任何事情吓到的年纪。

===Original===
"Queer, isn't it? But, Lord bless you, mothers do rum things! I've known cases—but you've heard about 'em too, Leslie. Peter went down to Wimbledon to raise hell for some reason or other. It appears his mother had heard the fuss he was making at the door and telephoned for the police before he broke in. It might have been bad for him if he were a convict on licence, but fortunately he's time-expired, and he has only to say that it was a family quarrel to get bound over. I don't think he will be called upon for a defence, anyway."

===Chinese===
"是挺蹊跷的，对吧？可老天保佑，当妈的什么事干不出来！我见过类似的案子——你也听说过吧，莱斯利。彼得跑到温布尔登（Wimbledon）去闹事，也不知道为什么。好像是他母亲听到他在门口闹腾，在他闯进去之前就报了警。要是他还在假释期，这可够他受的了，但好在他已经服满了刑期，只要说这是家庭纠纷，交保候审就完了。我看他根本不用请律师辩护。"

===Original===
Leslie Maughan nibbled at the end of her glove, a devastating habit of hers in moments of perturbation.

"I really can't believe it, though, of course, it must have happened. What was his mother doing down there? And why on earth did Peter do such a mad thing?"

Coldwell smiled.

"Go down and ask him," he said. "I'll give you a note to the inspector, and you might have a few minutes' talk with him before he appears in court. It is very unlikely that they will remand him to Brixton. If the Princess has got horse sense she will get him acquitted. Mrs. Dawlish is pretty sick and sorry that she allowed herself to charge him. I can tell you that because, as soon as I heard about the case, I phoned up the station and the sergeant in charge told me that Mrs. Dawlish came to the police station at seven o'clock this morning to see if she could get her name taken out of the record.
