"是的，安妮塔，我找到了，"另一个说。"他是个很好的人，如果他认为我的健康状况有危险，就不会让我出去。而且，真的，我有时必须考虑自己，安妮塔。我身体一点都不好，很长时间以来我一直考虑把自己交给医生——"
"你请了谁？"
"埃尔福德·韦斯利医生（Dr. Elford Wesley）。他过去是老道利什先生（Mr. Dawlish）的医生——"
她听到一声野兽般的低吼，惊恐地瞪着安妮塔。安妮塔的眼睛睁得大大的；她露出牙齿，狰狞地笑了。
"你这个没脑子的傻瓜！"她嘶嘶地说。"你为什么请他？"

===Original===
Demoniacal, terrifying, she towered above the frightened woman, and Greta cowered and held up her hand as though to ward off a blow.
"Get on the telephone, quick, and tell him he needn't come. Invent any excuse you like—hurry!"
In a trembling voice Greta called the number.
"He's gone," she said, and looked up at her mistress.

===Chinese===
她像恶魔一般骇人地俯视着那个惊恐的女人，格蕾塔（Greta）畏缩着，抬起手仿佛要抵挡一击。
"快去打电话，告诉他不用来了。随便编个理由——快！"
格蕾塔用颤抖的声音拨通了电话。
"他走了，"她说，抬头看着女主人。

===Original===
"All right, hang up, you fool!" Anita was breathing quickly. New lines showed in her face; she looked like an old woman.
"Send somebody down to the door and tell him he needn't come."
"But, Anita," wailed the other, "I can't do that. I must see him, Nita. What a stupid thing you are! What difference does it make? If you don't like him you needn't show yourself. And if I send down a message like that he'll be awfully suspicious. You remember how the police came just because my wretched doctor told somebody I had a gunshot wound in the leg?"
There was reason and intelligence in this, and though the woman was quivering between fear and fury, she had no course but to consent, and when, ten minutes later, the doctor's foot sounded on the stairs outside, Anita Bellini disappeared into the bedroom, but did not go beyond earshot.

===Chinese===
"好吧，挂掉，你这蠢货！"安妮塔（Anita）急促地呼吸着。她脸上添了新的皱纹；看起来像个老妇人。
"派个人下楼去门口，告诉他不用来了。"
"可是，安妮塔，"另一个哀叹道，"我不能那样做。我必须见他，妮塔。你真是个蠢货！这有什么区别？如果你不喜欢他，你大可不必露面。而且如果我传这样的话下去，他会非常起疑的。你还记得吗，就因为我那讨厌的医生告诉别人我腿上有枪伤，警察就来了？"
这番话有理有智，尽管那女人在恐惧与狂怒间颤抖，她别无选择，只得同意。十分钟后，医生的脚步声在门外楼梯上响起时，安妮塔·贝里尼（Anita Bellini）消失在卧室里，但并未走远到听不见声音。

===Original===
He was an elderly man, rather talkative and fussy, short and stout, with a cherubic face, and short, white side-whiskers.
"Bless my soul, I remember you now!" he said. He was one of the loud and jovial race of doctors that is fast dying out. "I remember you very well. You used to be a friend of the Dawlishes, didn't you? Poor old Donald! What a good sort! Now, let me look at this leg of yours."
He examined the wound, which was little more than a scar, and, to Greta's dismay, pronounced her fit to travel.

===Chinese===
他是个上了年纪的人，相当健谈且有些小题大做，身材矮胖，长着一张天使般的脸，留着短短的白色连鬓胡子。
"天哪，我现在想起你了！"他说。他是那种喧闹而快活的老派医生，如今已快绝迹了。"我记得你很清楚。你以前是道利什（Dawlish）家的朋友，对吧？可怜的老唐纳德（Donald）！多好的人啊！来，让我看看你的腿。"
他检查了伤口，那不过是一道疤痕，令格蕾塔沮丧的是，他宣布她可以旅行了。

===Original===
"You'll have to take care of yourself for a week or two," he said conventionally, and returned to the topic his examination had interrupted. "Yes, I was with old Donald two days before he died, from morning till night, hoping against hope that I could do something for him. For twenty-four hours I never stirred from his side. Poor old Donald! He died six hours after I left him, with my dear friend, Sir Paul Grayley, one of the best doctors that ever lived."
Old Mr. Wesley was blessed with this disposition, that all the people he knew were the best people that ever lived, and all who were bereft of his acquaintance came under the generic heading of "poor souls."

===Chinese===
"你得照顾自己一两个星期，"他照例说完，然后回到了他检查时打断的话题。"是的，老唐纳德去世前两天，我从早到晚都守着他，抱着一线希望看能为他做点什么。整整二十四小时我没离开他身边。可怜的老唐纳德！我离开他六小时后他就去世了，身边有我亲爱的朋友保罗·格雷利爵士（Sir Paul Grayley），他是有史以来最好的医生之一。"
老韦斯利先生（Mr. Wesley）天性如此，他认识的人都是有史以来最好的人，而那些与他失去联系的人则统统归入"可怜的人"这一泛称。

===Original===
"Very bad business about his boy, poor soul!" He shook his hoary head. "Terribly bad business. I didn't know Peter personally—never met him. But when I heard of this fearful thing he'd done, I said to myself, 'My boy, if the news has to be broken to Donald, you're the man to do it.'"
He was very talkative, very delightful, very human, but Greta was annoyed with him and gave him little encouragement to stay. As for the woman standing in the darkness of the bedroom, had her wishes materialized, old Mr. Wesley would have been swept from the face of the earth.

===Chinese===
"他儿子的事真是糟糕，可怜的人！"他摇着花白的头。"太糟糕了。我不认识彼得（Peter）本人——从未见过他。但当我听说他做的那件可怕的事时，我对自己说，'我的孩子，如果这消息必须告诉唐纳德，你就是去说的人。'"
他非常健谈，非常讨人喜欢，非常有人情味，但格蕾塔被他惹恼了，几乎没给他什么留下来的理由。至于那个站在卧室黑暗中的女人，如果她的愿望能够实现，老韦斯利先生早就从地球上被扫除了。

===Original===
Presently he was gone, and she came out from her listening post.
"Apparently you can move without dying," she said sarcastically.
"Apparently I can, if I want to move." Greta's voice was husky. She was back in her last trench, conscious of a great shortage of ammunition. "And I don't want to move, and that's flat! I can't understand why you hate that dear old man. I admit he's fearfully chatty, but that's no reason why you should throw a fit at the mention of his name."

===Chinese===
不久他就走了，她从她偷听的位置走了出来。
"显然你可以走动而不至于死掉，"她讽刺地说。
"显然我可以，如果我想走动的话。"格蕾塔的声音沙哑。她回到了最后的战壕，意识到弹药严重不足。"而我不想走动，就这么简单！我不明白你为什么讨厌那位可爱的老先生。我承认他确实话多得吓人，但那并不构成你一听到他的名字就大发雷霆的理由。"

===Original===
"When I want your opinion about my peculiarities I will ask you for them," bullied Anita, and it was a wrong move, as she realized.
Mrs. Gurden shrugged her shoulders rapidly.
"If that's the tone you're going to adopt," she said, with an heroic assumption of boldness, "the sooner we part the better, Anita. You've stopped the paper, but I think I'm entitled to some money instead of notice; and if it comes to that, I've had no salary for a month. And as to going down to your beastly old Towers, I simply won't, so there!"
The Princess forced a smile.

===Chinese===
"等我想听你对我怪癖的意见时，我会问你的，"安妮塔霸道地说，但这是个错误的举动，她自己也意识到了。
格登太太（Mrs. Gurden）迅速地耸了耸肩。
"如果你打算用这种态度，"她英勇地装出大胆的样子说，"我们越早分开越好，安妮塔。你停了报纸，但我认为我有权拿到钱而不是通知；而且说到这个，我已经一个月没拿到薪水了。至于去你那可怕的旧塔楼，我就是不去，就这样！"
公主勉强挤出一丝微笑。

===Original===
"My dear Greta, you're getting theatrical. But I realize you're not quite yourself. Now, don't be a little fool, come and rest with me for a week or two. There are one or two big schemes I want to talk over with you, and afterwards we'll pack up and go to Capri or Monte Carlo or somewhere a little more cheerful than Wimbledon."
"I won't!"
It required a tremendous amount of courage to utter those two words of defiance, but it was zero hour to Greta Gurden, and for the moment she had all the ferocity of a mad sheep.

===Chinese===
"亲爱的格蕾塔，你变得太戏剧化了。但我明白你不太对劲。好了，别犯傻了，来跟我住一两个星期吧。有一两个大计划想和你商量，之后我们就收拾行李去卡普里岛（Capri）或蒙特卡洛（Monte Carlo），或者去个比温布尔登（Wimbledon）更令人愉快的地方。"
"我不去！"
说出这两个字的反抗需要巨大的勇气，但对格蕾塔·格登来说，这是她的决定性时刻，此刻她有着疯羊般的凶猛。

===Original===
"I simply won't! If I've got to earn my own living I'll earn it. I can get a job on *Fleet Fashions*—I was offered one last week. I'm tired of your domination and your bullying, and—well, I simply won't go to Wimbledon, and that's a fact!"
Here was a resistance which Anita Bellini had never anticipated. There was not the stuff of sweet reasonableness in her. She had made her way in the world by the force of her character, and her simulations had been confined to hiding her too frequent fits of anger. It was not in her to persuade—she must command or do nothing.

===Chinese===
"我就是不去！如果我得自己谋生，我会去赚的。我能在《舰队时尚》（Fleet Fashions）找到工作——上周有人给我提供过一个职位。我厌倦了你的支配和欺凌，而且——好吧，我就是不去温布尔登，这是事实！"
这是安妮塔·贝里尼从未预料到的反抗。她骨子里没有温和讲理的成分。她在这个世界上靠性格的力量闯出了自己的路，她的伪装仅限于掩盖她过于频繁的暴怒。她不会说服——她只会发号施令或者什么都不做。

===Original===
"You're going to make me look foolish. I've promised—"
"I don't care what I make you look." Greta's head was quivering with determination. "It's not my fault. And whom have you promised?" Without waiting for a reply: "You know how I loathe that house at Wimbledon and those awfully creepy Japanese men of yours."
"Javanese. They're quite nice people. If you refer to your encyclopaedia you will discover they are inoffensive, peace-loving, and domestic."
But sarcasm was wasted on Greta.

===Chinese===
"你会让我显得很蠢。我已经承诺了——"
"我不在乎我让你显得怎样。"格蕾塔的头因决心而颤抖着。"这不是我的错。而且你承诺了谁？"不等回答："你知道我多讨厌温布尔登那栋房子和你那些可怕的日本仆人。"
"爪哇人（Javanese）。他们人很好。如果你查查你的百科全书，会发现他们无害、热爱和平、且顾家。"
但讽刺对格蕾塔没用。

===Original===
"That may be or may not be," she said. "All I know is that I'm not coming with you."
"Stay and be—stay till tomorrow!" snapped the elder woman. "I shan't waste my time or go down on my hands and knees to you. You owe me a lot, Greta—"
"You owe me a month's salary," said the spirited Greta, with admirable courage, "and three months' notice."
Her hands trembling with rage, Anita tore open her bag and flung a packet of one-pound notes on the table. Without another word she strode out of the room and shut the door so violently that the whole house shook.
Greta Gurden sat bolt upright, shivering with triumph, yet with a sinking sense of terror at what the morrow would bring forth. She had charred her boats, but she had not burnt them. Her shaking hand grabbed the telephone.
"Put me on to Scotland Yard," she said.

===Chinese===
"可能是也可能不是，"她说。"我只知道我不跟你走。"
"留下，待着——待到明天！"年长的女人厉声说。"我不会浪费时间，也不会跪下来求你。你欠我很多，格蕾塔——"
"你欠我一个月的薪水，"勇敢的格蕾塔以令人钦佩的勇气说，"还有三个月的通知期。"
安妮塔的手因愤怒而颤抖，她撕开手袋，把一叠一英镑的钞票扔在桌上。一句话没说，她大步走出房间，猛地关上门，整栋房子都震动了。
格蕾塔·格登笔直地坐着，因胜利而颤抖，却又对明天将带来的事感到一种下沉的恐惧。她已烧毁了退路，但尚未烧尽。她颤抖的手抓起电话。
"给我接苏格兰场（Scotland Yard），"她说。

===Original===
She heard the weary sigh of the operator.
"Is Scotland Yard blessed with a number?" she asked.
Greta hung up the phone and looked round in search of the directory. But apparently Scotland Yard had no number, nor did there seem to be such a place on the face of the earth. She was to learn later that the official designation was New Scotland Yard, but she did not dream of looking under the N's. And then she remembered one Leslie Maughan, and the M's yielded a good result. She waited for a while after she had given the number, and then:
"Yes. I want to speak to you."
"Yes, Mrs. Gurden."
Greta started.
"How did you know?"
She heard a laugh.
"I always remember voices, especially nice voices like yours," said the mendacious young lady from Scotland Yard.
"I want to see you very much—very badly, I mean—tremendously."
"In fact, you want to see me," said Leslie. "I'll come along."

===Chinese===
她听到接线员疲惫的叹息声。
"苏格兰场有电话号码吗？"她问。
格蕾塔挂上电话，四处寻找电话簿。但显然苏格兰场没有号码，似乎地球上也不存在这个地方。她后来才知道官方名称是新苏格兰场（New Scotland Yard），但她做梦也没想到去查N开头的条目。然后她想起了一个叫莱斯利·莫恩（Leslie Maughan）的人，查M开头的条目有了好结果。她报了号码后等了一会儿，然后：
"是的。我想和你说话。"
"好的，格登太太。"
格蕾塔吃了一惊。
"你怎么知道？"
她听到一声笑。
"我总能记住声音，尤其是像你这样好听的声音，"来自苏格兰场的那位撒谎的年轻女士说。
"我非常想见你——非常急切，我是说——极其想见。"
"事实上，你想见我，"莱斯利说。"我这就来。"

===Original===
It required some persuasion to induce Lucretia to wait for the arrival of Mr. Coldwell.
"Very well, then," said Leslie patiently, "wait in the street. You'll catch your death of cold, but I don't suppose that will worry you very much. You might even hobnob with a policeman. I trust you."
"I should jolly well say you did!" said the indignant Lucretia.
She compromised by sitting on the baggage in the passage, the door being propped open with a weight. She found it a little more draughty than the street.

===Chinese===
需要费些口舌才能说服卢克丽霞（Lucretia）等待科尔德威尔先生（Mr. Coldwell）的到来。
"那好吧，"莱斯利耐心地说，"在街上等着。你会冻死的，但我想那也不会让你太担心。你甚至可以跟警察套近乎。我相信你。"
"我当然相信你会这么说！"愤慨的卢克丽霞说。
她妥协了，坐在走廊的行李上，门被重物撑开着。她觉得这里比街上还要通风些。

===Original===
Greta's boats seemed a little more burnt than she could have desired when she surveyed the desolation just before Leslie's arrival. She had little stamina for quarrelling, and already her mind was a confusion of fear and penitence when Mrs. Hobbs, who had returned for her evening duties, shown the girl into the dining-room.
"It's awfully good of you to come." Greta was her conventional self, grabbed the girl's hand in both of hers, used that old and artless trick of looking up pleadingly into her visitor's face. "I'm so worried, my dear. The truth is, I've quarrelled with Anita. Definitely and finally," she said, recovering a little of her lost ground. "The paper is dead, as you've probably heard—you know everything at Scotland Yard. That means I'm out of a job, though I can get one tomorrow by asking. Anita has behaved abominably. I should never have dreamt, after all I've been to her, the thought and care and experience I have devoted to her, as it were—do take your hat and coat off. Shall I ask the maid to make you a cup of tea?"

===Chinese===
当格蕾塔在莱斯利到来之前审视这片荒凉时，她的退路似乎比她希望的更烧毁了一些。她没什么耐力吵架，当霍布斯太太（Mrs. Hobbs）回来值晚班、把女孩领进餐厅时，她的心里已充满恐惧与悔恨的混乱。
"你来真是太好了。"格蕾塔恢复了她惯常的样子，用双手抓住女孩的手，使用那种老套而天真的技巧，恳求地抬头看着客人的脸。"我好担心，亲爱的。事实是，我跟安妮塔吵架了。彻底而最终地，"她说，稍微挽回了一点失去的阵地。"报纸停了，你可能已经听说了——你们苏格兰场什么都知道。这意味着我失业了，不过我明天一开口就能找到工作。安妮塔的行为太恶劣了。我做梦也想不到，我对她付出了那么多，投入了那么多心思、关怀和经验，可以说——请把你的帽子和外套脱了吧。要我让女仆给你泡杯茶吗？"

===Original===
Leslie, secretly amused, shook her head. She guessed that the woman had changed her mind since she first sent for her. It was hardly likely that she would trouble to telephone about one of those quarrels which, if her information was accurate, were not an infrequent occurrence between Greta Gurden and the Princess.
"Of course, I've nothing to tell you that would harm Anita." Mrs. Gurden planted one foot firmly on shore, and prepared, figuratively, to splash the waves of her venom with the other. "But she's so peculiar—and such a temper! I shouldn't be surprised if she goes off in a fit of apoplecy one of these days."

===Chinese===
莱斯利暗自好笑，摇了摇头。她猜这女人自从第一次叫她以来已经改变了主意。如果她的信息准确的话，格蕾塔·格登与公主之间的争吵并非罕见，她不太可能为其中一次争吵打电话来。
"当然，我没有什么会伤害安妮塔的事要告诉你。"格登太太一只脚稳稳地踩在岸上，另一只脚则准备比喻性地溅起她毒液的浪花。"但她太古怪了——脾气那么大！要是哪天她突然中风，我也不会惊讶。"

===Original===
"What is her trouble now?"
Greta could tell her this much, she decided, without disloyalty to her late employer. The very thought that she was "late" filled her with dismay.
"She wanted me to go to Wimbledon to stay there for a month, and I hate the place—I simply loath it! I'm rather temperamental; I suppose all artists are—I mean, artists and literary people. And May Towers gives me the horrors. And, of course, she was terribly rude to me, in spite of the fact that I am far from well and my leg aches excruciatingly. Anita is the most unreasonable person. You've no idea, Miss Maughan. Of course, we quarrelled, and I simply told her that I'd have no more to do with her. And then she made a fearful scene because I asked old Dr. Wesley to come up and see me and tell me whether I was fit to be moved. She practically cursed me for calling him. Really, I thought she was going mad. And he's such a dear old soul—awfully talkative, of course, but a perfect gentleman, and a kind man. Why, do you know, he was with Mr. Dawlish for the last twenty-four hours of his life—never left his side, my dear, hoping he'd regain consciousness—so kind!"

===Chinese===
"她现在有什么麻烦？"
格蕾塔决定，这件事可以告诉她，不算对前雇主不忠。想到她现在是"前"雇主，她就充满沮丧。
"她想让我去温布尔登住一个月，我恨那个地方——我简直讨厌它！我相当情绪化；我想所有艺术家都是如此——我是说，艺术家和文学界的人。梅塔楼（May Towers）让我感到恐惧。而且，当然，她对我非常粗鲁，尽管我远未康复，我的腿疼得厉害。安妮塔是最不讲理的人。你无法想象，莫恩小姐（Miss Maughan）。当然，我们吵架了，我干脆告诉她我再也不跟她有任何关系了。然后她大发雷霆，因为我请老韦斯利医生来看看我，告诉他我是否适合被移动。她简直因为我请他而诅咒我。真的，我以为她疯了。而他真是个可爱的老灵魂——当然话多得吓人，但完全是个绅士，是个善良的人。你知道吗，道利什先生生命的最后二十四小时他都守在他身边——从未离开过，亲爱的，希望他能恢复意识——多善良啊！"

===Original===
Leslie was sitting at the other side of the table, her hands folded patiently, waiting for the real story to come. Now she leaned forward, her eyes upon the woman's face.
"Dr. Wesley? Was he the Dawlishes' doctor?"
"A very charming old man, but awfully fond of Mr. Dawlish. Except for six hours just before his death, he was with old Mr. Dawlish for a whole day and a night—never left his side."

===Chinese===
莱斯利坐在桌子对面，双手耐心地交叠着，等待真实的故事出现。现在她倾身向前，眼睛盯着那女人的脸。
"韦斯利医生？他是道利什家的医生吗？"
"一个非常迷人的老先生，但非常喜爱道利什先生。除了他临终前的六小时，他陪伴着老道利什先生整整一天一夜——从未离开过他身边。"

===Original===
Leslie hardly heard the next five minutes' complaint, but when she came to bring her understanding to bear upon her hostess, Greta was not much nearer to the reason for her telephone message.
"…if anything comes out I can always say, and Anita must bear me out, that I never knew this wretched man was a woman. The first thing I saw was Anita and this man struggling, and I wanted to send for the police. And then those wretched men came in and tried to drag the pistol out of Druze's hand—her hand, I mean, Druze's hand. And there was I, lying on a sofa—fainted, my dear, and with simply not a notion in the world that I was wounded—it may sound strange to you, but it is true. When I woke up, Anita was going on like somebody who had lost her head. It was simply ghastly."

===Chinese===
接下来五分钟的抱怨，莱斯利几乎没听进去，但当她开始理解她的女主人时，格蕾塔离她打电话的原因仍然很远。
"……如果有什么事被揭露出来，我总可以说，安妮塔也会支持我的，我从不知道这个可恶的男人是个女人。我看到的第一件事是安妮塔和这个男人在挣扎，我想去叫警察。然后那些可恶的人进来，试图把枪从德鲁兹（Druze）手里夺出来——我是说她手里，德鲁兹的手里。而我呢，躺在沙发上——晕过去了，亲爱的，而且完全不知道自己受伤了——这在你听来可能很奇怪，但这是真的。我醒来时，安妮塔像个疯子一样在叫喊。简直太可怕了。"

===Original===
"Did you see Druze again?"
Greta shook her head.
"No—the language she used before the shooting started!" Greta shuddered. "I simply couldn't repeat half the words she employed. Of course, Anita sent me out of the room; said she didn't know I was there; but just as I started to go out, my dear—bang!" Mrs. Gurden grew dramatic and illustrative. "Bang! And then everything went dark. You know how it does, my dear."
"I can't understand quite," said Leslie. "A few hours after the shooting I found you at Lady Raytham's."
"She sent me—Anita," Mrs. Gurden broke in. "'Go to Jane, but tell her nothing,' said Anita. 'Find out all that you can about Druze—how they parted, if she threatened her.' Those were her words. You know Anita, she's—what is the word?—imperious! I didn't know whether I was on my head or my heels—like that Mr. What's-his-name who's written a story about women. I simply had to. And not an idea in my head that a beastly bullet had gone into my leg. The doctor said that if I hadn't run about the wound would have healed right away. It was only when I got home—my dear, I nearly died." She paused to take breath. "I suppose she'll come tomorrow and ask me to go back. I'm such a forgiving nature—"

===Chinese===
"你又见到德鲁兹了吗？"
格蕾塔摇摇头。
"没有——她在开枪前用的那些语言！"格蕾塔颤抖着。"我连她用过的一半词语都复述不出来。当然，安妮塔把我赶出了房间；说她不知道我在那里；但就在我开始走出去的时候，亲爱的——砰！"格登太太变得戏剧化并加以比划。"砰！然后一切都暗了下来。你知道那种感觉，亲爱的。"
"我不太明白，"莱斯利说。"开枪后几小时，我在雷瑟姆夫人（Lady Raytham）那里找到了你。"
"她派我去的——安妮塔，"格登太太插嘴道。"'去找简（Jane），但什么都别告诉她，'安妮塔说。'尽可能查明关于德鲁兹的一切——她们怎么分手的，她是否威胁过她。'那是她的原话。你知道安妮塔，她是——那个词是什么来着？——专横！我不知道自己是头朝上还是头朝下——就像那个写过女人故事的某某先生。我别无选择。而且脑子里根本没意识到一颗讨厌的子弹打进了我的腿。医生说如果我没到处跑，伤口早就愈合了。直到回到家——亲爱的，我差点死了。"她停下来喘了口气。"我想她明天会来让我回去。我本性就是个宽宏大量的人——"

===Original===
"If there is anything in life that you value, you will stay here, Mrs. Gurden," said Leslie quietly. "I don't want to frighten you, but I think it is my duty to warn you that the Princess Bellini's course is nearly run. As to Druze—"
She had never thought that Druze was murdered; always she had at the back of her mind the possibility of a struggle in which the shots were accidentally fired. There was a good and sufficient reason why Anita Bellini should not shoot the mock butler.

===Chinese===
"如果你生活中有任何你珍视的东西，你就待在这里，格登太太，"莱斯利平静地说。"我不想吓唬你，但我认为有责任警告你，贝里尼公主的路快走到头了。至于德鲁兹——"
她从未想过德鲁兹是被谋杀的；她心里始终认为那可能是一场争吵，枪声是意外走火。安妮塔·贝里尼不杀那个假管家，有一个充分的理由。

===Original===
When she reached her flat, the front door was closed. She opened it and turned on the passage light. Lucretia and the grips were gone, she saw with satisfaction. In the letter-box was a blue-lettered cablegram, and she snatched it out and opened it. This was a reply to one she had sent on her way back from lunch, and she read the message and could have sung in her joy.
She ran up the stairs, her mind divided between this blessed message and her interview with Greta Gurden. Greta was in revolt; that much was clear. But how far would her rage and venom carry her towards a complete betrayal of her employer? As she passed the hall window she noticed that the new safety-catch was in place. Really it was ridiculous to leave the flat at all, she thought. After that one abortive attempt it was not likely that a second would be made.
She almost regretted now that she had agreed to Mr. Coldwell's plan. Throwing open the door of her sitting-room, she put out her hand and turned the light switch. But the room remained in darkness. Had they replaced the fuses? she wondered, and walked into the room.

===Chinese===
当她回到公寓时，前门是关着的。她打开门，打开了走廊的灯。卢克丽霞和行李都不见了，她满意地看到。信箱里有一封蓝色字样的电报，她一把抓出来打开。这是她午餐后回来的路上发出的电报的回复，她读了电报，高兴得几乎要唱起来。
她跑上楼梯，心里一边想着这封祝福的电报，一边想着她与格蕾塔·格登的会面。格蕾塔在反抗；这一点很清楚。但她的愤怒和毒液会把她推向对雇主的彻底背叛到什么程度？经过大厅窗户时，她注意到新的保险装置已经装好。真的，离开公寓简直太荒谬了，她想。在那次失败的尝试之后，不太可能有第二次了。
她现在几乎后悔同意了科尔德威尔先生的计划。她猛地推开起居室的门，伸手去开电灯开关。但房间仍然一片漆黑。他们换好了保险丝吗？她疑惑着，走进了房间。

===Original===
There was no sound, no warning. A great hand suddenly gripped her throat, another covered her mouth. She felt the pressure of a knee in her back, and struggled desperately but unavailingly.
"You scream—you killed!" hissed a voice in her ear, and, summoning all her strength, she tried to nod in agreement with the unspoken demand of her captor.
The door closed softly behind her. There were two men. She felt her ankles gripped and lifted, and she was carried into the bedroom and laid on the bed.
"You scream—you killed!" said the voice again.
The grip about her throat relaxed, but the evil-smelling hand was still on her face.
"I won't scream," she managed to mumble, and the stifling palm was removed.
"You scream, I cut your t'roat. You not scream, I not cut you t'roat—not hurt."
"I shan't scream," she said in a low voice. "May I get up, please?"
There was a whispered consultation in a language which held some gutturals, and then the man who had first spoken said:
"You sit on a chair, keep very quiet, long time, long time."

===Chinese===
没有声音，没有警告。一只大手突然扼住她的喉咙，另一只捂住她的嘴。她感觉到一个膝盖顶在她的背上，她拼命挣扎但无济于事。
"你尖叫——你就死定了！"一个声音在她耳边嘶嘶说道，她用尽全力，试图点头同意绑架者无声的要求。
门在她身后轻轻关上。有两个男人。她感觉到脚踝被抓住并抬了起来，然后被抬进卧室放在床上。
"你尖叫——你就死定了！"那个声音又说。
扼住她喉咙的手松开了，但那只臭烘烘的手仍然捂在她的脸上。
"我不会尖叫，"她勉强含糊地说，令人窒息的手掌移开了。
"你尖叫，我就割你喉咙。你不尖叫，我就不割你喉咙——不伤害。"
"我不会尖叫，"她低声说。"我可以起来吗？"
有人用一种带喉音的语言低声商议，然后第一个说话的人说：
"你坐在椅子上，保持非常安静，很长时间，很长时间。"

===Original===
He gripped her by the arm and assisted her back to the dining-room, guiding her to a chair, though there was enough light from a street lamp for her to pick her way.
There were two men—little men; their heads were not much above her shoulder. Broad, squat, and, as she had reason to know, immensely strong. She could not see their faces; by accident or arrangement their backs were to the window. He who was evidently chief of the two said something in an unknown language, and his companion withdrew to the landing, and the hall and landing lights went out. Presently he came back, and, to her surprise, he was joined by a third. Again there was a whispered consultation, and the third man disappeared, the other two squatting on the carpet before her, impassive, silent, watching, as she guessed, with eyes that did not leave her for a second. A quarter of an hour they sat thus, and then:
"I speak English liddle bit. I hear English well," said the man. "I tell you trut'. Last night Nigara cut your t'roat. This night he not hurt." He added a phrase she could not understand.
"What are you going to do with me?" she asked.

===Chinese===
他抓住她的胳膊，扶她回到餐厅，引导她坐到椅子上，尽管街灯的光线足以让她看清路。
有两个男人——小个子男人；他们的头不比她的肩膀高多少。身材宽阔、矮壮，而且，她有理由知道，力大无穷。她看不清他们的脸；不知是偶然还是有意，他们的背对着窗户。显然是那两人头目的那个用一种未知的语言说了些什么，他的同伴退到了楼梯平台上，大厅和楼梯平台的灯熄灭了。不久他回来了，令她惊讶的是，第三个男人也加入了。又是一阵低声商议，第三个男人消失了，另外两个蹲在她面前的地毯上，面无表情，沉默，注视着，她猜想，眼睛一秒钟也没离开过她。他们就这样坐了一刻钟，然后：
"我讲一点英语。我听英语很好，"那人说。"我告诉你真话。昨晚尼加拉（Nigara）要割你喉咙。今晚他不伤害你。"他加了一句她听不懂的话。
"你们打算怎么处置我？"她问。

===Original===
"Presently, by-and-by," said the little man, after he had repeated her words slowly and had grasped their meaning, "you and me walk into carriage. While you walk you see peoples. If you speak to peoples I cut you t'roat."
Very definite, but the repetition of the phrase amused her mildly.
"You're rather monotonous, aren't you?" she asked. "And after I get into the carriage, what happens?"
There was a pause while he took this in.
"By-and-by you see," he said.
The third man came back now, and she gathered that he was in reality the leading member of the gang, for on his word the two others vanished through the door and he took their place.
"You won't be hurt unless you give us trouble," he said. To her surprise he spoke in perfect English. "My patron requires you."

===Chinese===
"很快，过一会儿，"小个子男人在慢慢重复她的话并理解了意思后说，"你和我走进马车。你走的时候看见人。如果你对人说话，我就割你喉咙。"
非常明确，但这句话的重复让她略感好笑。
"你相当单调，不是吗？"她问。"我上了马车之后呢？"
他花了一会儿才听懂这句话。
"过一会儿你就知道了，"他说。
第三个男人现在回来了，她明白他实际上是这群人的头目，因为在他发话后，另外两个从门口消失了，他取而代之。
"只要你给我们制造麻烦，你不会受伤的，"他说。令她惊讶的是，他说着完美的英语。"我的主顾要见你。"

===Original===
"Who is your patron?"
It gave her a sense of comfort to know that this queer little shape could understand all she said and could converse intelligently. It made him less of a strange and menacing animal, and removed some of the terror from the situation. And it delayed the moment when she would find her cumbersome garter a vital safeguard.
"I cannot answer your questions, miss," he replied. "But you will not be hurt. Last night you would have been killed—I myself would have killed you—but that is not the order today. If you are sensible and quiet, nothing will happen."
He stood up and looked out of the window; neither the shades nor the curtains had been drawn, and he could see to the opposite side of the road.
"I must tell you what will occur," he said. He had a trick of pedantry which might have amused her at any other time. "This house is being watched by the police. After a while they will grow tired and careless, and then my friend will signal to me that they have walked away. When that happens we will go."
She could not see him; she could only guess that his "friend" was one of the two. She had noticed that all three were dressed in correct European garb, and the incongruity of their overcoaks and derby hats added a touch of the bizarre.

===Chinese===
"你的主顾是谁？"
知道这个古怪的小个子能听懂她说的每一句话并能明智地交谈，让她感到一种安慰。这让他不那么像一个陌生而危险的动物，也减轻了她的一些恐惧。而且这推迟了她发现她笨重的吊袜带是关键保命工具的时刻。
"我不能回答你的问题，小姐，"他回答。"但你不会受伤。昨晚你本会被杀死——我自己会杀了你——但今天的命令不是这样。如果你明智而安静，什么都不会发生。"
他站起来望向窗外；遮光帘和窗帘都没有拉上，他能看到马路对面。
"我必须告诉你将会发生什么，"他说。他有一种学究式的习惯，换作其他时候可能会让她觉得好笑。"这栋房子正被警察监视。过一会儿他们会变得疲倦和大意，然后我的朋友会向我示意他们已经走开了。到时候我们就走。"
她看不见他；只能猜他的"朋友"是那两个人之一。她注意到他们三个都穿着得体的欧式服装，而他们大衣和圆顶礼帽的不协调增添了一丝怪异。

===Original===
"Will you therefore sit nearer to the window, at your writing place? If the telephone rings you will not answer."
So they sat, he on one side of the table and she on the other, his eyes roving to the sidewalk and from the sidewalk to his prisoner. She saw the limousines stream past on their way to the theatres, and wondered if, on any stage in London, there would be enacted a drama quite as improbable as this in which she played a leading part.
After a long interval of silence:
"I suppose you realize that, when I do not arrive at Mr. Coldwell's house, he will either telephone or come back for me?"
He nodded.
"We have already made provision," he said simply. "We have sent him a telegram in your name, saying that you have been called away to—" He hesitated. "I cannot remember the town; it is in the West and is on the sea."
"Plymouth?" she asked quickly.
"Plymouth," he nodded. "The telegram also told him your hotel. Plymouth is very far, and by the time he discovers you have not arrived"—a pause—"by that time you will not be here."
"Where shall I be?" she asked.
"You will be in the harem of Diga Nagara, the great prince who is dead yet is alive."
Leslie Maughan did not swoon. She stared across the table at the little man. He was nodding solemnly.
"Diga Nagara, the great prince—who was dead and is alive!"

===Chinese===
"请你坐到靠近窗户的写字台那里好吗？如果电话响了，不要接。"
于是他们坐下了，他在桌子一边，她在另一边，他的目光从人行道扫向他的囚犯，又从他的囚犯扫向人行道。她看见豪华轿车在去剧院的路上川流不过，她想知道，在伦敦的任何舞台上，是否会上演一出比她正在主演的这出更不可能的戏剧。
经过漫长的沉默：
"我想你明白，当我没有到达科尔德威尔先生家时，他要么会打电话，要么会回来找我？"
他点了点头。
"我们已经做好了安排，"他简单地说。"我们以你的名义给他发了封电报，说你被叫去——"他犹豫了一下。"我不记得那个镇名了；它在西边，靠海。"
"普利茅斯（Plymouth）？"她迅速问道。
"普利茅斯，"他点头。"电报还告诉他你的旅馆。普利茅斯很远，等他发现你没有到达时"——他停顿了一下——"到那时你已经不在这里了。"
"我会在哪里？"她问。
"你会在迪加·纳加拉（Diga Nagara）的后宫里，那位伟大的王子，死了却还活着。"
莱斯利·莫恩没有晕倒。她隔着桌子瞪着那个小个子男人。他正庄严地点着头。
"迪加·纳加拉，伟大的王子——死了却还活着！"

## XVIII / 第十八章

===Original===
Children﻿—little Elizabeth and that unseen boy of his﻿—! Peter Dawlish walked up and down his cramped room, his hands in his pockets, an unlighted cigarette between his lips. The hopelessness of it all! Where and how could he begin his search? That baby of his belonged to the world of unreality, to the mists of dreams. Elizabeth was real. He could see those wide, frightened eyes of hers, the transparent pallor of her face. He shut his eyes and there she was again, frail, delicate, pleading for help he was powerless to give.
He was alone in the house. Through the thin partition walls which separated one jerry-built cottage from the other he heard the sound of a man and a wife quarrelling. In the street a boy was whistling flatly a popular tune. If Mrs. Inglethorne were here he would have the truth though he had to choke it from her. Who else would know but she?
He had been such a short time in the lodging that he was not even acquainted with her friends﻿—the slinking little thieves who came to barter and haggle over the property they had stolen knew no more of her than that she was a mean and grinding bargainer. She had no cronies to come and spend the evening with her; by very reason of her peculiar business, she could not risk the giving or taking of confidences.

===Chinese===
孩子——小伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）和他那个未曾谋面的儿子——！彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish）在他那间狭小的房间里踱来踱去，双手插在口袋里，嘴角叼着一根没点燃的香烟。这一切是多么无望！他该从哪里、又该如何开始寻找？他的那个婴儿属于虚幻的世界，属于梦的迷雾。伊丽莎白是真实的。他能看到她那双睁大的、惊恐的眼睛，看到她脸上那种透明的苍白。他闭上眼睛，她又出现了，脆弱、纤弱，恳求着他无力给予的帮助。
房子里只有他一个人。透过分隔一栋栋劣质小屋的薄薄隔板墙，他听到一对夫妻争吵的声音。街上有个男孩走调地吹着一首流行曲调。如果英格尔索恩太太（Mrs. Inglethorne）在这里，他就能得到真相，即使得从她嘴里硬挤出来。除了她，还有谁知道呢？
他在这住所待的时间太短，甚至不认识她的朋友——那些偷偷摸摸的小偷来这里讨价还价、争执他们偷来的财物，对她只知道她是个刻薄、斤斤计较的生意人。她没有密友来和她共度夜晚；正因她那特殊的行当，她不敢冒险与人互诉衷肠。

===Original===
The police had been to the house and made a perfunctory search, their object being to discover other evidence against her. But they had looked only for articles of value which she might have purchased: lengths of cloth and silk (she specialized in this trade), and they were not particularly concerned about Elizabeth. Nobody cared very much about Elizabeth, except Leslie and he.
This thought occurred to him as he walked to and fro﻿—and thought breeds thought. Might he not, searching with another object, discover what they had overlooked﻿—one fragment of a clue that would bring him to the child? Why should he be concerned? What legal or moral right had he to detach Mrs. Inglethorne’s daughter from her legal guardian? He considered this matter, only to brush it aside. Presently he carried the lamp downstairs with the faintly pleasurable hope which comes to all who engage in secret searches.
The woman’s room was accessible. The lock he had broken had not been repaired. He went in, put the lamp on the mantelpiece and looked round. Search parties usually leave chaos behind them, but the police in their investigation had, if anything, tidied the room. There were a number of dresses, obviously the woman’s, stacked on the bed; two oleographs that once decorated the wall had been lifted down﻿—clean squares on the wallpaper marked their old position. By the side of the clothes was a square wooden box, of the kind that soldiers use for the transportation of their possessions. This had been opened and was unlocked. The lid had jammed upon a wedge of cloth as it had been closed, and there was a gap of an inch.

===Chinese===
警方来过这房子，做了敷衍的搜查，目的是寻找其他对她不利的证据。但他们只寻找她可能购买的贵重物品：成匹的布料和丝绸（她专做这行生意），并不特别关心伊丽莎白。除了莱斯利（Leslie）和他，没人在乎伊丽莎白。
这个念头在他来回踱步时出现——而思绪会衍生思绪。如果他带着另一个目标去搜查，会不会发现他们遗漏的东西——一小片线索，能指引他找到那个孩子？他为什么要关心？他凭什么法律或道德权利，把英格尔索恩太太的女儿从她的法定监护人身边带走？他考虑了这个问题，只是又把它抛到一边。很快，他提着灯下楼，带着一种所有参与秘密搜寻的人都会有的、微弱而愉悦的希望。
那个女人的房间畅通无阻。他弄坏的锁还没修好。他走进去，把灯放在壁炉架上，环顾四周。搜查队通常会留下一片狼藉，但警方的调查如果有什么成效的话，倒是把房间整理了一下。床上堆着一些裙子，显然是那个女人的；墙上曾挂着两幅石版画，已被取下——壁纸上留下的干净方块标示着它们原来的位置。衣服旁边是一个方形木箱，是士兵用来运输个人物品的那种。箱子被打开过，没上锁。盖子合上时夹住了一块布楔，留出一英寸的缝隙。

===Original===
Where would a woman like Mrs. Inglethorne keep papers? Or did she keep papers at all? He tried to remember the habits of her type, acquired at secondhand from his fortuitous acquaintances in Dartmoor Prison. Under her bed? But the police had obviously rolled up the mattress and made that elementary examination. There was nothing here﻿—nothing. He opened the big black box, disparagingly. And then he saw, with a quickening interest, that the inside of the lid was almost covered by newspaper cuttings which had been pasted on the wood. Here was revealed Mrs. Inglethorne’s “scrapbook,” and incidentally her favourite daydreams. A headline caught his eye.

> “Heiress Traced by Her Baby Sock!”

Another headline ran:

> “Child’s Mother Tracked by Initial on Infant’s Gown.”

He carried the lamp to a little table and read the cuttings carefully. They all dealt with one subject: the identification of unknown children that had brought fabulous fortunes to the lucky person who had traced their descent. Some of the cuttings were very old, yellow with age and scarcely decipherable. Evidently Mrs. Ingletorne’s collection covered a long period.

===Chinese===
像英格尔索恩太太这样的女人会把文件放在哪里？或者她根本就没有文件？他试图回忆她这类人的习惯，这些习惯是他从达特穆尔监狱（Dartmoor Prison）偶然结识的那些人那里间接了解到的。在她床下？但警方显然已经掀起床垫做了基本检查。这里什么也没有——什么也没有。他轻蔑地打开了那个大黑箱子。接着，他带着越来越浓的兴趣看到，箱盖内侧几乎贴满了剪报，贴在木板上。这里展现的是英格尔索恩太太的“剪贴簿”，也顺带展示了她最爱做的白日梦。一个标题吸引了他的目光。

> “女继承人凭婴儿袜寻获！”

另一个标题写道：

> “孩子母亲凭婴儿衣服上的首字母追踪。”

他把灯拿到小桌旁，仔细阅读剪报。它们都涉及同一主题：通过识别身份不明的孩子，为追溯其身世的幸运儿带来巨额财富。有些剪报非常古老，因年代久远而发黄，几乎无法辨认。显然，英格尔索恩太太的收藏涵盖了很长一段时间。

===Original===
He supposed the police had searched the box, which was nearly filled with little cylinder-shaped budles tied around with tape. Linen, coarse calico, cotton﻿—diving into the mass, his fingers touched silk. The bundles had once been white, but constant fingering and dust had left them an indescribable hue. He untied a bundle and opened it. It consisted of a child’s cotton nighdress, a little pair of woollen shoes, and a small knitted shawl. Pinned to the shoes was a scrap of paper on which was written in an illiterate hand the words: “Mrs. Larse boy, 10 days old, measles 9 months.” Here then was the beginning and end of Mrs. Larse’s boy. “Measles 9 months” was his epitaph.
She was a baby farmer; he had guessed that. He opened another bundle hopefully. Somewhere here would be a reference to Elizabeth. The second package had nothing but a coarse calico robe and a penned inscription: “Young girl named Leavey, 5 days, whooping cough 6 weeks.” One by one he unrolled these little tragedies, and few indeed were they who had not their death certificates inscribed laconically at the end. Some had two papers, identically inscribed. He supposed that the repetition was due to Mrs. Inglethorne’s careless and haphazard system of “bookkeeping.”

===Chinese===
他猜想警方搜查过这个箱子，箱子里几乎塞满了用带子捆扎的小圆柱形包裹。亚麻布、粗棉布、棉布——把手伸进那堆东西，他的手指碰到了丝绸。这些包裹曾经是白色的，但不断的翻动和灰尘给它们留下了一种难以形容的色泽。他解开一个包裹打开。里面是一件孩子的棉质睡衣、一双小小的羊毛鞋和一条针织小披肩。鞋上别着一张纸片，上面用不识字的手迹写着：“拉尔斯太太（Mrs. Larse）的男孩，10天大，麻疹，9个月。”这就是拉尔斯太太男孩的始与终。“麻疹，9个月”就是他的墓志铭。
她是个“婴儿农场主”（baby farmer）；他猜到了。他满怀希望地打开另一个包裹。这里面某处应该会有对伊丽莎白的提及。第二个包裹里只有一件粗棉布罩衫和一行手写的题字：“名叫利维（Leavey）的女婴，5天大，百日咳，6周。”他逐一展开这些小小的悲剧，很少有人在末尾没有用简洁的文字注明死亡证明。有些包裹里有两张纸，题字完全相同。他猜想这种重复是由于英格尔索恩太太那马虎随意的“记账”系统。

===Original===
He had examined twelve and took out the thirteenth, wondering what potency there was in that lucky or unlucky number. The nightdress he unrolled was of the finest linen, the most expensive of all he had examined. The shawl was of heavy silk, and the microscopic shirt of the most delicate flannel. For some time he could not find the inscription, but eventually it was discovered inside the shawl. Only three words, but they set his heart beating:
“Miss Martha’s girl.”
The bundle dropped from his nerveless hands. “Miss Martha’s﻿—girl!”
He took the letter out of his pocket. “I have found a home for your son.” He read again the pencilled words “Martha’s servant.” And Martha’s servant was﻿—Mrs. Inglethorne!
Miss Martha’s girl. This woman could not have made a mistake. One by one he examined the clothes separately, and then, pinned to the inside of the dress, near the collar, he found a second paper written in the same hand, and, reading it, he uttered a hoarse cry.
“Miss Martha’s girl Elizabeth.”

===Chinese===
他已经检查了十二个，拿出了第十三个，想知道这个或吉或凶的数字有什么魔力。他展开的睡衣是用最好的亚麻布做的，是他检查过的所有衣物中最贵的。披肩是厚重的丝绸，那件微型衬衫是最精致的法兰绒。有一阵子他找不到题字，但最终在披肩内侧发现了。只有三个字，却让他的心狂跳起来：
“玛莎小姐（Miss Martha）的女孩。”
包裹从他无力的手中掉落。“玛莎小姐的——女孩！”
他从口袋里掏出那封信。“我已经为你儿子找到了一个家。”他再次读着铅笔写的字：“玛莎的仆人。”而玛莎的仆人就是——英格尔索恩太太！
玛莎小姐的女孩。这个女人不可能搞错。他一件件单独检查那些衣服，然后，在裙子内侧靠近领口的地方，别着第二张纸，也是同样的字迹。读着它，他发出一声嘶哑的叫喊。
“玛莎小姐的女孩伊丽莎白。”

===Original===
Feverishly he untied the other bundles, but found no further clue. His knees were trembling as he mounted the stairs, the precious garments close to his heart. Putting down the lamp on the table, he examined again these pitiful souvenirs. He must see Leslie at once. Not daring to leave the clothes behind, he folded them and put them into his pocket. He used the silk shawl as a neckcloth under his thin overcoat; the night was bitterly cold, but it was not the warmth of the soft fabric which brought a glow to his heart.
The windows of the flat were in darkness, but he remembered that they had heavy velvet curtains, and possibly they were drawn. Ringing the bell, he waited. There was no answer. He rang again. And then a man appeared from a nearby doorway and strolled up to him.
“Who do you want?” he asked in a tone of authority. Peter guessed that he was a detective.
“I want Miss Maughan. My name is Dawlish.”
“Oh, Dawlish, yes. Miss Maughan isn’t in. She is staying at Inspector Coldwell’s house in Finchley. There is nobody in the flat.”
He did not hide his disappointment; he was so full of his discovery that he had to tell somebody. He had to see her. The detective gave him the inspector’s address, and he walked across the Charing Cross Road, intending to make his way to the Tube station. He reached the other side of the road, and then something made him look back at the windows of her apartments. And in that instant he saw a quick flicker of light, as though somebody had turned on an electric hand-lamp for the fraction of a second and had extinguished it immediately.

===Chinese===
他发狂似的解开其他包裹，但没有找到更多线索。他走上楼梯时膝盖在发抖，那珍贵的衣物紧贴着他的心。他把灯放在桌上，再次审视这些可怜的纪念品。他必须立刻去见莱斯利。不敢把衣服留下，他把它们叠好放进口袋。他把丝绸披肩当围巾塞在薄薄的大衣底下；夜晚寒冷刺骨，但让他的心感到温暖的，并非柔软织物的热度。
公寓的窗户一片漆黑，但他记得他们挂着厚重的天鹅绒窗帘，可能已经拉上了。他按了门铃，等待着。没有回应。他又按了一次。接着，一个男人从附近的门厅出现，朝他踱步而来。
“你找谁？”他用一种威严的语气问道。彼得猜他是个侦探。
“我找莫恩小姐（Miss Maughan）。我是道利什（Dawlish）。”
“哦，道利什，是的。莫恩小姐不在。她在芬奇利（Finchley）科尔德威尔警探（Inspector Coldwell）的家里。公寓里没人。”
他没有掩饰自己的失望；他满脑子都是自己的发现，必须告诉某人。他必须见到她。侦探给了他警探的地址，他穿过查令十字路（Charing Cross Road），打算去地铁站。他走到路对面，接着，某种东西让他回头看了一眼她公寓的窗户。就在那一瞬间，他看到一道快速闪烁的光，仿佛有人打开了一盏手电筒，只亮了一瞬间就立刻熄灭了。

===Original===
Peter stopped. Somebody was in Leslie’s room. He walked slowly across the road. The detective had disappeared; was, in point of fact, walking to the back of the block to visit his fellow-watcher. As Peter stood, hesitating, he saw the street door move slightly, and, acting on an impulse, he pushed it wide open and took one step into the darkness.
“Who’s there?” he said, and that was all that he remembered.
Something soft and heavy fell with a thud on his head, crushing his soft hat as though it were paper. He stumbled on to his knees, and a second blow laid him prostrate, the blood trickling down his face and staining the soft silk that had once enwrapped his child.
There were no loiterers in Charing Cross Road that bitter night, when a chill northeast wind sent people hurrying to the shelter of their homes. There was no lounger to tell the detective of three people who had walked hurriedly across the sidewalk into the car which was drawing to the kerb at the very moment Peter pushed open the door.

===Chinese===
彼得停了下来。有人在莱斯利的房间里。他慢慢穿过马路。那个侦探已经消失了；事实上，他正走到街区后面去拜访他的同伴监视者。彼得站在那里，犹豫着，他看到大门微微移动了一下，于是，出于一种冲动，他把门猛地推开，跨步走进黑暗中。
“谁在那儿？”他说，而这是他最后的记忆。
一个柔软而沉重的东西砰地一声砸在他头上，像纸一样压扁了他那顶软帽。他踉跄着跪倒在地，第二击将他打倒在地，鲜血顺着他的脸流下，染红了那曾包裹过他孩子的柔软丝绸。
那个寒冷的夜晚，查令十字路上没有闲逛的人，一股凛冽的东北风催促着人们匆匆赶回家躲避。没有闲人告诉侦探，就在彼得推开大门的那一刻，有三个人匆忙穿过人行道，钻进了一辆正停靠在路边的汽车。

## XX / 第二十章

===Original===
It seemed to Peter Dawlish that he had been unconscious for an eternity when he turned over on his back with a groan and felt carefully towards his damaged head. His face was wet and sticky, and when he essayed to rise to his feet, it seemed that the whole of the building was oscillating violently. Presently, however, he was up, keeping to the wall for support, and, grasping the handle of the door, he jerked it open and was instantly gripped with hands of steel.

"Hullo, who are you?" asked a stern voice.

"I don't know﻿—Dawlish﻿—something happened. I saw a light and came over﻿—and then the door opened and I don't remember much more."

The detective recognized him.

"The door opened?" he said anxiously. "Was somebody in the flat?"

Peter nodded and winced.

"Give me a drink," he said, and the detective guided him by the arm and led him upstairs to Leslie's room.

===Chinese===
彼得·道利什（Peter Dawlish）觉得自己昏迷了整整一个世纪，才呻吟着翻过身来，小心翼翼地摸了摸受伤的脑袋。他的脸上湿漉漉、黏糊糊的，试着站起来时，整栋房子仿佛都在剧烈摇晃。但他还是站了起来，扶着墙稳住身子，抓住门把手猛地一拉——门开了，一双铁钳般的手立刻抓住了他。

"喂，你是谁？"一个严厉的声音问道。

"我不知道……达利什……出事了。我看见有灯光就过来——然后门打开了，后面的事我就记不太清了。"

那个探员认出了他。

"门开了？"他焦急地问，"公寓里有人？"

彼得点点头，痛得龇牙咧嘴。

"给我喝点水，"他说。探员搀着他的胳膊，带他上楼去了莱斯利（Leslie）的房间。

===Original===
A glass of ice-cold water revived him and he was able to tell a coherent story of his experience.

"It couldn't have been more than ten minutes ago," said the detective. "I went round to see my opposite number and I'll swear I wasn't gone for more than that time."

Suddenly he stooped to the floor and took up something. It was a loose native slipper that had slipped from the foot of Leslie's captor in the hurry of departure. The light he had shown when he searched for this was the light that Peter saw.

"Just wait; I'll call Mr. Coldwell."

Inspector Coldwell was at dinner when the message came.

"Hang on, I'll come down," he said. "I've had a wire from Miss Maughan that she's going to Plymouth, but that doesn't mean anything."

===Chinese===
一杯冰水让他缓过神来，能够把刚才的遭遇讲个大概。

"最多不过十分钟前的事，"探员说，"我去找对面的同事，我敢发誓我没走开超过那个时间。"

他忽然弯腰从地上拾起一样东西——那是一只松垮的本地拖鞋，莱斯利的绑架者仓促逃跑时从脚上滑落的。他搜查时点亮的灯，正是彼得看到的那盏。

"等着，我去叫科尔德威尔（Coldwell）先生。"

科尔德威尔警探（Inspector Coldwell）收到消息时正在吃晚餐。

"别挂，我马上下来，"他说，"我刚收到莫恩（Maughan）小姐的电报，说她要去普利茅斯，但这说明不了什么。"

===Original===
He was in the flat twenty minutes later. By this time Peter's wound had been roughly dressed, and he had washed the blood from his face. Save for the throb of the wound, he was little the worse for his experience.

"They coshed you with a rubber club; it is rather a good method," said Coldwell callously.

He looked round the room with pursed lips and a frown.

"It doesn't follow that because those birds were here, she was here," he said, and glanced at his watch. "Too early for Miss Maughan to have arrived at Plymouth. Just wait; I want to make sure."

He drove to the telegraph office from which the message had been sent, and was fortunate to find the postmaster just leaving his office.

"I want to see the telegram that was sent from here about five o'clock tonight addressed to me."

"You want to see the original telegram, I suppose? That won't be difficult."

===Chinese===
二十分钟后他赶到了公寓。此时彼得的伤口已粗略包扎过，脸上的血也洗干净了。除了伤口阵阵作痛，他几乎没受什么大碍。

"他们用橡胶棒把你打晕了，这招挺管用，"科尔德威尔冷淡地说。

他抿着嘴，皱着眉环视房间。

"那些家伙在这儿，不等于她也在这儿，"他看了看表，"莫恩小姐到普利茅斯还早着呢。等着，我去核实一下。"

他开车去了发出那封电报的电报局，幸运的是邮电局长正要锁门离开。

"我想看看今晚五点左右从这里发出、寄给我的那封电报。"

"您要看电报原件？没问题。"

===Original===
It was more difficult than he supposed, and half an hour's precious time was wasted before the pencilled form was produced. Coldwell had only to glance at the writing to know that it was not in Leslie's hand. Yet a woman had written it; that was obvious from the characteristic writing.

He returned to the flat and sent the detective in a cab to Scotland Yard and Peter employed this interval to tell him of what he had found in Mrs. Inglethorne's box.

"I pretty well guessed that," said Coldwell. "So did Leslie﻿—Miss Maughan. 'The son' meant nothing. This unfortunate lady had intended keeping the child with her if it were a girl, and that was not the wish of the gang who were bleeding her. They told her she had a son. But I'm going to make sure about that before we go any farther. Somehow I'm not so scared about Leslie Maughan as I ought to be perhaps. She's got a sort of gun."

===Chinese===
事情比他想的麻烦，半小时宝贵的时间白白耗掉了，那份铅笔填写的表格才被找出来。科尔德威尔只需扫一眼笔迹，就知道这不是莱斯利写的。但那确实是女人的字迹——从字的特征上一眼就能看出来。

他回到公寓，派那个探员坐出租车去苏格兰场（Scotland Yard），彼得趁这个间隙告诉他，在英格尔索恩（Inglethorne）太太的箱子里发现了什么。

"我早就猜到了，"科尔德威尔说，"莱斯利——莫恩小姐——也猜到了。'儿子'毫无意义。那位不幸的太太本打算如果是女孩就自己养，但那些讹诈她的团伙不想那样。他们告诉她生了个儿子。但在我继续查下去之前，我要先确认这一点。不知怎么，我对莱斯利·莫恩的担心没有本该有的那么重。她有一把……算是一种枪。"

===Original===
A quarter of an hour after, his cab drew up before the gloomy doors of Holloway Prison, and after a strict scrutiny of his credentials he was admitted, and conducted to one of the main halls of the gaol, where the remand prisoners were housed. The chief wardress opened the door and went in. Presently she came out and beckoned him into the cell.

Mrs. Inglethorne was sitting, a scowl on her face, her big raw hands clasped before her. She knew Coldwell, and lifted her lip in a grin of rage.

"Don't you come in here!" she said shrilly. "I'm not going to talk to you. If you want to find that kid, you go and find her! And that'll take you some time, I'll bet!"

"Listen!" Coldwell had a very direct way with criminals. "Whether you'll get a nine or a lagging depends on the answer you give me, Mrs. Inglethorne. There's just a chance that you may get something worse than a lagging."

===Chinese===
一刻钟后，他的出租车停在霍洛威监狱（Holloway Prison）阴沉沉的大门前。仔细查验过证件后他被放了进去，带到监狱关押还押犯的主要牢区。女牢头打开门走进去，不一会儿出来招手让他进牢房。

英格尔索恩太太坐在那里，满脸阴沉，粗大粗糙的双手交叠在身前。她认识科尔德威尔，翻起嘴唇露出愤怒的狞笑。

"你别进来！"她尖声说，"我不跟你说话。你要找那小崽子，自己去找！我敢说够你找一阵子的！"

"听着！"科尔德威尔对付罪犯向来直截了当，"你被判九年还是流放，取决于你怎么回答我，英格尔索恩太太。还有个可能——你会比流放更惨。"

===Original===
She scowled up at him.

"What do you mean?"

Very deliberately he sketched a portion of her life; told her where she had lived, and how long she had stayed in her various places of abode. She made no comment or correction, looking down at her hands, and only when he paused did she meet his eyes.

"Is that all?" she asked insolently.

"Not quite all. You have been engaged in baby farming for the past twenty years. In 1916, in the month of July, you received from one called Arthur Druze a baby boy of a few days old. Where is that child?"

"You'd better find out," she said.

"It is for you to find out," he said, in that hard, metallic voice which he adopted on occasions. "You have to prove to me that that child is alive, or there's another charge against you."

===Chinese===
她恶狠狠地瞪着他。

"你什么意思？"

他不紧不慢地勾勒了她的一段人生轨迹：住过哪里，在各处分别待了多久。她既不辩解也不纠正，只是低头看着自己的双手；直到他停顿下来，她才抬眼看他。

"就这些？"她傲慢地问。

"还不止这些。过去二十年你一直靠替人养婴孩为生。1916年7月，一个叫阿瑟·德鲁兹（Arthur Druze）的人交给你一个出生才几天的男婴。那孩子现在在哪里？"

"你最好自己去查，"她说。

"这得你来交代，"他用那种偶尔才使出的冷硬金属般的声音说道，"你得向我证明那孩子还活着，否则又多一条控告你的罪名。"

===Original===
"Eh?" She was startled. The big mouth trembled. "You can't charge me﻿—"

"I'll charge you with murder, and I'll dig up the garden of every house you've occupied in the past six years to find evidence."

Mrs. Inglethorne's many-chinned jaw dropped; her eyes stared wildly, and in their depths Coldwell read the very terror of death.

"I've done nothing﻿—like that!" she almost screamed.

"You were Martha's servant, weren't you?"

She nodded dumbly, and then, throwing herself on the couch, she writhed like a woman demented. And in her dementia she broke the habit of a lifetime and told the truth.

===Chinese===
"什么？"她吃了一惊，大嘴巴哆嗦起来，"你不能指控我——"

"我会指控你谋杀，并且把你过去六年住过的每所房子的花园都挖一遍来找证据。"

英格尔索恩太太那好几层下巴的嘴巴耷拉了下来，眼睛发狂般圆睁着，科尔德威尔从她眼底读出了对死亡的彻骨恐惧。

"我没干过——那种事！"她几乎尖叫起来。

"你是玛莎（Martha）的仆人，对吧？"

她木然地点点头，然后扑倒在长椅上，像个疯妇一样扭动身体。就在这种癫狂之中，她破了一辈子的习惯——说出了实话。

===Original===
A police tender was standing outside the door of Leslie's flat when Coldwell came back, and a dozen men stood about on the sidewalk. He beckoned Peter to him.

"You had better come along," he said.

"Where are you going?"

"To Wimbledon. Do you feel fit enough? There may not be a scrap, but I rather imagine that her supreme and exalted Highness will die fighting."

"Is Leslie there?"

Coldwell nodded.

===Chinese===
科尔德威尔回来时，一辆警车停在莱斯利的公寓门外，十几个人站在人行道上。他朝彼得招了招手。

"你最好一起来，"他说。

"去哪儿？"

"温布尔登（Wimbledon）。你身体撑得住吗？可能不会打起来，但我猜她那位至高无上的殿下会拼到最后一口气。"

"莱斯利在那儿？"

科尔德威尔点了点头。

===Original===
A hundred yards short of May Towers the tender stopped and the little army of men got down. On the journey Coldwell had made his arrangements. Four of the detectives were to make their way to the back of the house; the remainder were to force the entrance. It was Coldwell who rang the bell. In his right hand he gripped an axe, ready to strike at the chain the moment the door was opened.

Standing behind him, Peter saw him stoop his head.

"Can you hear anything?" he whispered.

"No, sir."

"Thought I heard a scream."

He waited a few seconds longer, and then:

"Give me the crowbar."

Somebody passed him up the long steel bar, and with a swing he drove the clawed ends between door and lintel. Again he struck, and this time had his purchase. Pulling back with all his strength, the door cracked open. Two blows from the axe broke the chain, and they streamed into the dark hall and up the stairs.

===Chinese===
离梅塔楼（May Towers）一百码的地方，车队停了下来，这支小小的队伍下了车。一路上科尔德威尔已经部署好了：四名探员绕到房子后面，其余的人强攻正门。按铃的是科尔德威尔。他右手攥着一把斧头，门一开就准备劈断门链。

彼得站在他身后，看见他低下头。

"你听见什么了吗？"他低声问。

"没有，长官。"

"我以为听到一声尖叫。"

他又等了几秒钟，然后说：

"把撬棍递给我。"

有人把一根长钢撬递过去，他抡起来将带爪的一头楔进门框与门板之间。又是一下，这下楔实了。他拼尽全力向后一拽，门嘎吱一声裂开。两斧头砍断了门链，众人涌进漆黑的大厅，冲上楼梯。

===Original===
The squat Javanese stooped and lifted the girl without an effort, and as he did so the little men who stood around clapped their hands rhythmically and droned the marriage song of their class. Leslie heard and set her teeth, as she felt herself raised in the strong hands of this hideous little man.

She had a glimpse of Anita Bellini﻿—the hate in her eyes made her shudder in spite of herself.

"Goodbye, little Maughan!" she mocked. "There is death at the end of this."

And then she stopped, her eyes glaring towards the door.

"Stand fast, everybody! Tell these fellows not to move, Bellini!"

It was Coldwell's voice. Leslie felt herself slipping from the encircling arms. Then suddenly somebody caught her, and she looked round into the haggard face of Peter Dawlish.

===Chinese===
那个矮胖的爪哇人弯腰毫不费力地把女孩抱了起来。他动手时，周围的小个子们有节奏地拍着手，哼唱着他们那一行的婚歌。莱斯利听见了，咬紧牙关，感到自己被这个丑陋小男人的强壮双手举了起来。

她瞥见安妮塔·贝里尼（Anita Bellini）——对方眼中的恨意让她不由自主地打了个寒颤。

"再见了，小莫恩！"她嘲弄道，"这条路的尽头是死。"

她忽然住了嘴，眼睛死死盯住房门。

"所有人不许动！告诉这帮家伙不许动，贝里尼！"

那是科尔德威尔的声音。莱斯利感到自己从环抱的双臂中滑落下来。忽然有人接住了她，她扭头看见了彼得·道利什憔悴的面孔。

===Original===
"No gunplay," said Coldwell gently, "and there will be no trouble. I want you, Bellini; I suppose you are prepared for that?"

"I am called Princess Bellini," she began.

"Whether you're Princess Bellini or Annie Druze or Alice Druze is a matter of supreme indifference to me," said Coldwell, as he caught her wrist. "But you have the distinction of being the first woman I've ever handcuffed."

He snapped the cold circle about her wrist.

"But then, you see, most of the ladies I've pinched have been gentle little souls who wouldn't hurt a fly."

She made no reply. That old look had come into her face again which Leslie had seen before.

Then Anita Bellini did an unexpectedly generous thing. She nodded to the wondering group of natives shepherded behind three armed detectives.

"These men have done no harm," she said. "They have merely carried out my instructions in ignorance of the law."

===Chinese===
"别动枪，"科尔德威尔轻声说，"就不会有麻烦。贝里尼，我找的就是你——我想你早就料到了吧？"

"我叫贝里尼公主（Princess Bellini），"她开口道。

"你是贝里尼公主还是安妮·德鲁兹（Annie Druze）还是爱丽丝·德鲁兹（Alice Druze），对我来说毫无所谓，"科尔德威尔一边说一边抓住她的手腕，"不过你倒是有个殊荣——你是第一个被我铐上的女人。"

他咔哒一声把冰冷的铁环扣在她手腕上。

"不过你看，我抓过的那些女士大多是连苍蝇都不敢伤害的温柔小人儿。"

她没有回答。莱斯利以前见过的那种神情又浮现在她脸上。

这时安妮塔·贝里尼做了一件出人意料的大方之举。她朝那群被三名持枪探员看守在后面、满脸困惑的当地人点了点头。

"这些人没有做错任何事，"她说，"他们只是在不知法的情况下执行了我的指令。"

===Original===
She said something in Javanese to the man who had held Leslie, and he grinned and answered in the same language.

"My head boy here"﻿—she nodded to him﻿—"will accept responsibility for the other natives."

And then, with a sideways jerk of her head and a hard smile:

"Well, here is the end of the Druzes," she said.

"Not quite." Leslie's quiet voice interrupted her. "Martha has still to be disposed of."

There was anger, but there was fear also in Anita Bellini's grimace.

"Martha? What do you mean﻿—Martha?" she asked sharply. "I have not seen her for years."

Leslie smiled.

"I saw her two days ago, so I have the advantage of you," she said.

===Chinese===
她用爪哇语对那个抱过莱斯利的男人说了几句，那人咧嘴一笑，用同样的语言回答。

"我的这位头领"——她朝他点了点头——"会替其他人担责任。"

随后她歪了歪头，冷冷一笑：

"好了，德鲁兹家族到此为止了，"她说。

"还没完。"莱斯利平静的声音打断了她，"玛莎（Martha）还没解决。"

安妮塔·贝里尼的脸上既有愤怒，也有恐惧。

"玛莎？你说什么——玛莎？"她尖声问道，"我好多年没见过她了。"

莱斯利微微一笑。

"我两天前见过她，所以我比你有优势，"她说。

===Original===
They waited only long enough for Leslie to gather a change of dress and a coat for the prisoner, and thereafter Anita Bellini went out of her life forever, except for the day when Leslie stood in the witness-box and testified against the monocled monocled prisoner, who did not look at her but sat staring straight ahead at the scarlet-robed judge.

Before she collected the clothes she went in search of Elizabeth, and found her weeping in her bed in the little dressing-room, and persuaded her to dress. By the time the Princess was out of the house and on her way to Wimbledon police station, the child was arrayed in her rags. Leslie stood in the doorway looking at her, and she was very near to tears.

"Elizabeth, do you remember how you used to pretend you had all sorts of nice fathers?"

The child nodded and smiled.

"Well, I'm going to introduce you to a real one."

"A real father?" asked the girl breathlessly. "My father?"

"And you'll never guess who he is."

Suddenly the child was clinging to her, her arms locked about her neck. Thus Peter found them, weeping together.

===Chinese===
她们只等了莱斯利收拾好换洗衣物和给囚犯的外套，随后安妮塔·贝里尼就永远地退出了她的生活——除了那天莱斯利站在证人席上，对着那个戴单片眼镜的囚犯作证的日子；囚犯没有看她，只是直直地望着前方身穿红袍的法官。

在收拾衣服之前，莱斯利去找了伊丽莎白（Elizabeth），发现她正躺在小更衣室的床上哭泣，便劝她穿好衣服。等到公主被押出房子、踏上前往温布尔登警察局的路时，那孩子已穿上了她的破衣烂衫。莱斯利站在门口看着她，几乎要落下泪来。

"伊丽莎白，你还记得你以前假装有各种各样好爸爸的事吗？"

孩子点点头，笑了。

"那好，我要给你介绍一个真正的爸爸。"

"真正的爸爸？"女孩屏住呼吸问，"我爸爸？"

"你绝对猜不到他是谁。"

孩子忽然扑过来抱住了她，双臂紧紧箍住她的脖子。彼得找到她们时，她们正相拥而泣。

## XXI

===Original===
It was not often that Mrs. Donald Dawlish made a call at any hour of the day; the appearance in Berkeley Square of her big Rolls was something of an event. But at eleven o'clock at night﻿ ﻿…

"Mrs. Dawlish?" said Jane in wonder, when the footman came to her with the news. She had not seen the woman for two years. Indeed, Mrs. Dawlish's attitude of late had been frankly antagonistic. "Ask her to come up, please."

The woman strode into the room, patting her mop of untidy white hair into place. She wore the black which suited her better than any more vivid shade, and on her bosom blazed a diamond star which was just a little too large to be altogether ornamental.

"I suppose you're surprised at my coming at this hour?" She dropped her shawl on the settee, and, walking to the fire, held out her hands to the blaze.

"I am﻿—a little," said Jane, wondering what was coming next. Nothing short of a catastrophe could have brought Peter's mother in such circumstances.

===Chinese===
唐纳德·达利什太太（Mrs. Donald Dawlish）白天出门做客的时候并不多；她那辆劳斯莱斯出现在伯克利广场上总算是件不大不小的事。但晚上十一点钟……

"达利什太太？"当男仆进来禀报时，简（Jane）惊讶地问道。她已经有两年没见过这个女人了。事实上，达利什太太近来的态度一直是赤裸裸的敌意。"请她上来吧。"

那女人阔步走进房间，拍了拍她那一头蓬乱的白发。她穿着黑色衣服——这比任何鲜艳的颜色都更适合她——胸前别着一枚钻石星章，大得几乎过了头，不太像纯粹的装饰品。

"你大概想不到我这么晚来吧？"她把披肩扔在长榻上，走到壁炉前，双手伸向火焰。

"是有点——意外，"简说，心里盘算着接下来会发生什么。若非出了什么天大的事，彼得的母亲绝不会在这种时候登门。

===Original===
"I've been a good friend of yours, Jane﻿—in the past," she began, and her look asked for confirmation; but Jane was silent. "There's trouble, bad trouble, over that will of the old man's," she went on. "I've had a letter from his lawyer tonight, asking me to give them all sorts of information that I am not prepared to give. The will was proved six years ago. They can do nothing now, but they nag and nag, and I'm getting tired of it all. They may be acting for Peter, but I doubt it. But Peter can stop this persecution."

It was the first news that Jane Raytham had had of any trouble in connection with the will, but the request was one which she could not pass unchallenged.

"I know nothing about the matter," she said. "Peter, of course, must do as he wishes. I have no influence there."

"You have a big influence," said Mrs. Dawlish emphatically. "Peter has found out about the child; I suppose you know that?"

Jane nodded.

"The man is crazy to find it, and﻿—"

She met the grey eyes and stopped.

===Chinese===
"我过去对你一直不错，简——过去，"她开口道，目光似乎在寻求认同；但简沉默不语。"那老头的遗嘱出了麻烦，大麻烦，"她接着说，"今晚我收到他律师的来信，要我提供各种各样的信息，而我一个字都不想给。遗嘱六年前就立过了，他们现在什么也做不了，可就是纠缠不休，我烦透了。他们也许是替彼得办事，但我表示怀疑。不过彼得可以让他们收手。"

这是简·雷瑟姆（Jane Raytham）第一次听说遗嘱出了问题，但她不可能对这个要求置之不理。

"这事我一无所知，"她说，"彼得当然可以按自己的意愿行事。我影响不了他。"

"你影响得了，"达利什太太斩钉截铁地说，"彼得发现了那个孩子的事；我想你知道吧？"

简点了点头。

"他疯了一样要找到那孩子，而且——"

她迎上了那双灰色的眼睛，住了口。

===Original===
"I am crazy to find it too," said Jane Raytham in a low voice.

"Are you?" Mrs. Dawlish was honestly surprised. "I didn't think you were that kind﻿—to worry about﻿—things. Well, that's all the better from my point of view. I can give you the child. You can tell Peter that I'll give you the child and make him a handsome allowance if he will stop his lawyers from worrying me."

"You can give me the child? You know where he is?" Jane's voice shook.

"Well﻿—yes, I do. It wasn't a boy, Jane."

Jane Raytham shrank back as if she had been struck.

"Not a boy? A girl? And you promised me﻿—"

"There's no sense in talking about promises, or what happened eight years ago," said Margaret Dawlish coldly. "I'm talking about the present. Yes, it was a girl. Druze took her to an old servant of mine﻿—Martha's servant."

===Chinese===
"我也疯了一样要找到她，"简·雷瑟姆低声说。

"你也想？"达利什太太真心感到意外，"我没想到你是那种会为——事情——操心的人。那好，这对我来说反而更好。我可以把孩子交给你。你告诉彼得，只要他让他的律师别再烦我，我就把孩子给你，还会给他一笔可观的生活费。"

"你能把孩子交给我？你知道他在哪里？"简的声音发颤。

"嗯——是的，我知道。不是男孩，简。"

简·雷瑟姆像挨了一记耳光似的往后一缩。

"不是男孩？是女孩？你明明答应过我——"

"谈什么承诺，谈什么八年前的事，都没有意义了，"玛格丽特·达利什（Margaret Dawlish）冷冷地说，"我说的是现在。是的，是个女孩。德鲁兹（Druze）把她交给了我的一个老仆人——玛莎（Martha）的仆人。"

===Original===
Jane could only stare at her, speechless with amazement.

"You﻿—you're Martha?"

Mrs. Dawlish nodded.

"Martha Druze?"

"Martha Dawlish. I am entitled to that name; not even Peter can take it from me. I married old Dawlish a fortnight after his wife died in childbirth. Anita bullied him into it, if you want to know the truth. She would have married him herself, but Bellini was alive. I was her favourite sister and she always wanted me to make a good marriage. I don't know what she had been to my husband and I don't very much care, but she was an attractive woman in those days, before she let herself go; at any rate she had enough influence to make him marry me."

Jane passed her hands before her eyes, as though she were trying to sweep away the mist which still obscured a clear view.

"You're Martha?" she said again. "Of course, I knew you were a nurse. Then Peter?"

===Chinese===
简只能瞪着她，惊愕得说不出话来。

"你——你就是玛莎？"

达利什太太点了点头。

"玛莎·德鲁兹？"

"玛莎·达利什。这个名字我受之无愧；就算是彼得也不能从我这里夺走。老达利什的妻子产后两周就死了，我在她死后两周嫁给了他。是安妮塔（Anita）逼他这么做的——你要想知道真相的话。她自己本来也想嫁给他，但贝里尼（Bellini）还活着。我是她最疼爱的妹妹，她一直希望我嫁个好人家。我不知道她跟我丈夫之间有过什么，我也不太在意，但她那时候还是个有吸引力的女人，在她放任自己之前；不管怎么说，她有足够的影响力让他娶了我。"

简用手在眼前晃了晃，仿佛想拂去遮住视线的薄雾。

"你就是玛莎？"她又问了一遍，"当然，我知道你是个护士。那彼得呢？"

===Original===
"No, Peter isn't my son, if that is what you're going to say. I insisted that he shouldn't be told. I felt it would weaken my position and authority. Mr. Dawlish was rather an easygoing man and he agreed. If Peter had had the brains of a gnat he wouldn't have needed telling. He had only to see the registration of his birth and compare it with my marriage certificate to know as much as you know now. Jane, will you help me with him? I don't care how large is the allowance I make him."

Jane shook her head helplessly.

"I don't know what I can do. I can't think very clearly, only﻿—I want the child﻿—my girl."

The hard face of Mrs. Dawlish creased in a rare smile.

"Is there nobody else who wants her?" she asked significantly. "Has Peter no rights? You haven't thought of that, I suppose?"

"I have thought of it," said Jane in a low voice. "But I know Peter. And whether I have her, or he, she will be free to us both. We're going very swiftly down the slope, and the slope is getting steeper," she shook her head, "and God knows where we shall land at the bottom. I've been just as wicked as a woman can be. I'm a bigamist﻿—don't interrupt me, please﻿—I'm a bigamist, and my husband must know. I don't think it will worry him as much as it worries me, and in a way he'll be rather glad to get rid of me. But I can face all that if I have my baby! I'll do what I can," she went on quickly, recovering the lost balance, "if it doesn't hurt Peter﻿—I've hurt him enough. He is too good a man to be wounded any farther. I cannot see him tonight; I will write to him and ask if I may see him tomorrow, and then﻿—"

===Chinese===
"不，彼得不是我儿子——我知道你要说这个。我坚持不让人告诉他。我觉得那样会削弱我的地位和权威。达利什先生是个随和的人，他同意了。但凡彼得有蚊子那么点脑子，他就根本不需要别人告诉他。他只要去看一眼自己的出生登记，再跟我的结婚证一比，就能知道你现在知道的一切。简，你能帮我对付他吗？不管给他多少生活费我都不在乎。"

简无助地摇了摇头。

"我不知道我能做什么。我现在脑子转不过来，只是——我想要那个孩子——我的女儿。"

达利什太太那张冷硬的脸上难得露出一丝微笑。

"就没有别人也想要她吗？"她意味深长地问，"彼得就没有权利？你大概没想过吧？"

"我想过了，"简低声说，"但我了解彼得。不管孩子在我这儿还是在他那儿，她对我们两个人都是自由的。我们正沿着斜坡飞速下滑，而且坡越来越陡，"她摇了摇头，"天知道滑到坡底会是什么样子。我一直坏到了女人能做到的极限。我是个重婚犯——请别打断我——我是个重婚犯，我丈夫必须知道。我想他不会像我这么难受，在某种程度上他可能还巴不得甩掉我。但如果能找回我的孩子，我就能面对这一切！我会尽力的，"她很快地接着说，找回了失去的平衡，"只要不伤害彼得——我已经伤他伤得够深了。他是个太好的人，不能再让他受创了。今晚我不能见他；我会写信给他，问他明天能不能见面，然后——"

===Original===
The door was opening slowly and a man came in whose head was whitely bandaged. At first she did not recognize him, and then:

"Why﻿—why, Peter!" she faltered.

He was leading by the hand a little girl in a worn, stained ulster. The golden head was hatless. Jane Raytham looked down into that beautiful child face, saw the clear eyes looking at her wonderingly, solemnly, and put up her hand to her throat, hardly daring to speak. She opened her lips; no sound came. She made yet another effort.

"Who is this, Peter?"

It was not like her own voice.

"This is Elizabeth," said Peter gently. "Elizabeth"﻿—he stooped and looked into the child's face﻿—"Elizabeth, this is your mother!"

===Chinese===
门缓缓开了，一个头部裹着白色绷带的男人走了进来。她一开始没认出他来，随后——

"怎——怎么，彼得！"她结结巴巴地说。

他牵着一个小女孩的手，那孩子穿着一件破旧沾污的长大衣。一头金发没戴帽子。简·雷瑟姆低头看着那张美丽的孩子的脸庞，看见那双清澈的眼睛正好奇地、庄重地望着她，便抬手捂住喉咙，几乎不敢开口。她张开嘴唇，发不出声音。她又试了一次。

"这是谁，彼得？"

那不像她自己的声音。

"这是伊丽莎白（Elizabeth），"彼得柔声说，"伊丽莎白"——他弯下腰看着孩子的脸——"伊丽莎白，这是你的妈妈！"

## XXII

===Original===
"I'm sorry to have brought you down to this very unpretentious little flat of mine," said Leslie, "but I have discovered in myself some of the qualities of a showman, and really and truly most of the documents and proofs I have are here."

And then she laughed, rocking from side to side in her chair.

"What is the joke?" asked Coldwell suspiciously.

"You look so like Christy Minstrels, all sitting round in a circle with your hands on your knees, and it's two o'clock in the morning, and﻿—oh; there are a dozen reasons why I should laugh. I'll begin at the beginning, shall I?

"I suppose everybody knows how I worked up an interest in this case, through finding a book of poems in a little Cumberland farmhouse, and how I put two and several together, made them four, guessed them six, and finally proved their real quantity beyond doubt.

"There was a family living in Devonshire named Druze."

===Chinese===
"很抱歉把你们请到我这间寒酸的小公寓里来，"莱斯利（Leslie）说，"但我发现自己身上还真有几分表演天赋，而且说实话，我手头大部分文件和证据都在这儿。"

她说着便笑了起来，身子在椅子上前摇后晃。

"有什么好笑的？"科尔德威尔（Coldwell）狐疑地问。

"你们围成一圈坐在那儿，手搁在膝盖上，活脱脱一帮克里斯蒂吟游诗人——而且现在是凌晨两点，还有——噢，我笑的理由多着呢。我从头讲起，好不好？

"我猜大家都知道我是怎么对这案子产生兴趣的——在坎伯兰（Cumberland）一间小农舍里发现了一本诗集。然后我把二和若干加在一起，算出四个，猜出六个，最后证实了它们的真实数量，铁证如山。

"德文郡（Devonshire）有一户姓德鲁兹（Druze）的人家。"

===Original===
Briefly she retailed all that the clergyman had told her, and all she had learnt from subsequent inquiries.

"Annie Druze was in reality Anita. Alice was Arthur Druze, and Martha, the younger of the two, eventually became Mrs. Dawlish. The three girls were very stanch friends. They had made some sort of compact in their childhood to stand together through thick and thin, and that is the only creditable aspect of their subsequent careers. Annie went abroad as a lady's maid, and scraped an acquaintance in some way with an impecunious scion of an Italian family and married him. Martha had a training in a hospital, became a maternity nurse, and was subsequently called in to nurse Peter's mother with her first child.

"Alice, the middle sister, joined her sister in Java, where the prince had taken some minor position. I have had a long talk with Martha, and she tells me that Alice Druze became Arthur Druze as the result of a masquerade. She went one night to a fancy dress ball dressed as a man, and nobody guessed her identity. The possibility may have occurred to Anita, as she had become, that in this guise her sister would be of use to her, for there is little question that even so long ago Anita was engaged in blackmail. There is proof that she blackmailed a Government official of Java, and there is the record of a complaint made to the English police in '89, when she returned to this country, from the wife of one of her victims. And she did not stop at blackmail. Martha﻿—who, to save her own skin, has betrayed everybody﻿—says that she had forged three bills of exchange to her knowledge. It is established that it was Anita who forged Lord Everreed's signature, and, taking advantage of Peter being out of the way, got Druze to cash the cheque, the proceeds of which were divided between the two sisters. Whether she did this out of sheer wickedness and with Martha's knowledge in order to ruin Peter, or whether she was in low water, I cannot discover. Martha suggests the latter reason, and swears that she knew nothing about the forgery until later. I have my own opinion.

===Chinese===
她简要复述了牧师告诉她的所有事，以及她后续调查所得的一切。

"安妮·德鲁兹（Annie Druze）其实就是安妮塔（Anita）。爱丽丝（Alice）就是阿瑟·德鲁兹（Arthur Druze），而玛莎（Martha）——两姐妹中较小的那个——最终成了达利什太太（Mrs. Dawlish）。三个女孩是非常要好的朋友。她们小时候就订下了某种盟约，要同甘共苦、患难与共，这也是她们日后所作所为中唯一值得一提的地方。安妮出国做了女仆，不知怎的认识了一个落魄的意大利贵族子弟，嫁给了他。玛莎在医院受过培训，成了一名产科护士，后来被请去照料彼得（Peter）母亲的头胎。

"二妹爱丽丝去了爪哇投奔姐姐，那里那位亲王谋了个小职位。我跟玛莎长谈过一次，她告诉我爱丽丝·德鲁兹之所以变成阿瑟·德鲁兹，是因为一场化装舞会。有天晚上她男装去参加化装舞会，没人识破她的身份。安妮塔——她已经变成了那副样子——可能想到了这种伪装对妹妹的用处，因为毫无疑问安妮塔早在那时就开始干敲诈勒索的勾当了。有证据表明她敲诈过爪哇的一名政府官员，还有1889年她回到英国时一名受害者的妻子向英国警方报案的记录。而且她不止敲诈。玛莎——为了自保出卖了所有人——说据她所知安妮塔伪造了三张汇票。已查实伪造埃弗里德勋爵（Lord Everreed）签名的是安妮塔，她趁彼得不在，让德鲁兹去兑现了支票，赃款由两姐妹瓜分。她是出于纯粹的恶意、知情的玛莎合谋毁掉彼得，还是因为手头拮据，我无法确定。玛莎说是后者，还发誓说她当时对伪造一事毫不知情。我有自己的看法。

===Original===
"Anita was distinctly acquainted with Jane before Peter knew her, but she did not become interested in her until after her marriage and return to England. The arrest of Peter coincided with Anita learning that Lord Raytham, a very rich man, was anxious to marry Jane, who in some mysterious fashion had disappeared. Anita guessed the cause and went in search of and found her. She learnt of Jane's condition and kept by her, her object being to persuade her to marry Raytham, so that she might profitably exploit the new Lady Raytham. She tried to persuade Jane that her marriage wasn't legal, hoping that the girl in her desperation would commit bigamy and be under her thumb for the rest of her life. But Jane made one desperate attempt to free herself from the marriage. She went to Reno, applied for a divorce, and that divorce was granted."

"Granted?" Jane's voice was shrill, almost a scream. "It was not granted, Leslie; it was refused﻿—!"

"It was granted. Your decree was made absolute. I have a cablegram from the clerk of the court to that effect﻿—it arrived last night. Naturally, Anita did her best to prevent the divorce, because, if it were given, she had practically no hold except the child, which was subsequently taken away by her sister and handed to Mrs. Inglethorne, who for four years was in Martha's employment. When she found she couldn't stop the divorce, she induced Jane to go out of court whilst the judge was giving his decision. Her car was waiting at the door of the court, and Jane was sitting in it, waiting for the verdict. It was not until Anita came out of court and joined her in the car that Jane learnt that the divorce had been refused. She married Raytham, believing that she was a bigamist, and yet finding poor sort of comfort in the belief that there had been some sort of irregularity in her marriage which made it invalid.

"For seven years Jane Raytham has been paying toll to the blackmailer, supposedly the man who had charge of the child, in reality to Anita Bellini and her sister.

===Chinese===
"安妮塔在彼得认识简（Jane）之前就认识她了，但直到简结婚回到英国后安妮塔才对她产生兴趣。彼得被捕的时候，安妮塔恰好得知雷瑟姆勋爵（Lord Raytham）——一个非常富有的人——急于迎娶神秘失踪的简。安妮塔猜到了原因，四处寻找并找到了她。她了解到简的处境后便留在她身边，目的是劝她嫁给雷瑟姆，以便从新任雷瑟姆夫人身上大捞一笔。她试图让简相信自己的婚姻不合法，指望这个绝望的女孩犯下重婚罪，从此一辈子受她摆布。但简做了一次孤注一掷的尝试来摆脱那段婚姻。她去了里诺（Reno），申请离婚，离婚获准了。"

"获准了？"简的声音尖利得几乎是在尖叫，"没有获准，莱斯利；被驳回了——！"

"获准了。你的判决已经生效。我有一封法院书记官发来的电报证实此事——昨晚才到。安妮塔自然拼尽全力阻止离婚，因为一旦离婚获准，她除了那个孩子之外就几乎没有任何把柄了。那孩子后来被她姐姐带走，交给了英格尔索恩太太（Mrs. Inglethorne），后者在玛莎手下干了四年。当她发现无法阻止离婚时，便诱使简在法官宣判时离开了法庭。她的车就等在法院门口，简坐在车里等着判决结果。直到安妮塔从法院出来上了车，简才知道离婚被驳回了。她嫁给了雷瑟姆，以为自己是个重婚犯，却又自我安慰地觉得自己的婚姻存在某种瑕疵使之无效。

"七年来简·雷瑟姆一直在向那个勒索者交纳赎金——那人名义上是看管孩子的人，实际上就是安妮塔·贝里尼（Anita Bellini）和她的姐姐。

===Original===
"Immediately after her return from America, Jane went to Appledore, her time being very near at hand. It was then that Martha was called in, and the poor girl learned that white-aproned nurse was the terrible Mrs. Dawlish whom Peter hated and feared. This was the beginning of Jane's time of torment which endured until a week ago. Then Druze, as I will call her, got scared. I think I was the person responsible. My inquiries about the twenty thousand pounds that had been drawn from Jane's bank, information which came to Scotland Yard in quite a normal way, frightened her and she decided to go abroad, getting as much money as she possibly could before she left.

"Jane gave her her emerald chain, and with this Druze went off to interview her sister. There was some little quarrel as to the division of the spoils. Anita, who was the stronger of the two, snatched the chain from her sister's hands, never expecting that the woman, infuriated with drink and anger, carried a pistol. In the struggle which followed, Druze was shot, but in some miraculous fashion still retained her hold of the square emerald. I can only imagine that Anita was so beside herself with grief that she did not make a search. In a panic she had the body put on the car and taken to the lonely spot and left there. But new clues were coming to light every day. Mrs. Inglethorne reported the presence of Peter Dawlish in her house and his interest in the child. Imagining that he suspected who Elizabeth was, and that his coming to Severall Street was designed, she had the little girl taken to Wimbledon, and concentrated all her mind upon getting rid of my unworthy self. For in me she thought she saw her chief enemy, and I think she was right.

"And that," said Leslie simply, "is that!"

===Chinese===
"简从美国回来后立刻去了阿普尔多尔（Appledore），因为她的预产期快到了。就在这时玛莎被请了来，可怜的姑娘这才知道那个白围裙护士就是彼得又恨又怕的可怕的达利什太太。简的噩梦就此开始，一直持续到一周之前。然后德鲁兹——我就这么叫她吧——慌了。我认为罪魁祸首是我。我对简的银行里被提走两万英镑一事展开调查——苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）以完全正常的方式收到了这一信息——这吓到了她，她决定出国，临走前尽可能多捞一笔。

"简把她的绿宝石项链交给了她，德鲁兹拿着这条项链去找她姐姐。两人在分赃问题上发生了小争执。安妮塔更强硬，一把从妹妹手里夺过项链，没想到那个喝得烂醉、怒火中烧的女人身上带着枪。随后的扭打中德鲁兹中了弹，但不知怎的竟还死死攥着那枚方形绿宝石。我只能想象安妮塔当时悲痛欲绝，根本没去搜查。她惊慌失措地让人把尸体搬上车，运到那个偏僻的地方扔下了。但新的线索每天都浮出水面。英格尔索恩太太报告说彼得·道利什出现在她家里，而且对那个孩子很感兴趣。安妮塔以为他猜到了伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）的身份，他来塞弗拉尔街（Severall Street）是有目的的，于是她让人把小女孩带到温布尔登（Wimbledon），把全部心思都花在除掉我这个不值一提的人身上。因为在她看来，我就是她的头号敌人，而我觉得她没说错。"

"这就是全部经过，"莱斯利平静地说，"到此为止！"

===Original===
Mr. Coldwell got up stiffly and stretched himself.

"I'm going home to bed. It's very unlikely that you will be troubled by the little yellow boys, and I think I can leave you and your Lucretia here without any misgivings. I don't know how this is going to look in court, or who will be brought into the case and who will not, but those things are the little unpleasantnesses which you will have to live through and live down."

Jane knew he was addressing her and smiled.

"I can live everything down," she said, "and live through everything, if somebody will let that little yellow head sleep on my pillow now and again."

She walked across to Peter and held out her hand.

"I don't know whether I'm glad﻿—about the divorce," she said. "I think I am. And I hope you are, Peter."

She cast a swift sidelong glance towards Leslie, who was arranging her papers at the desk, and dropped her voice still lower.

"Do you think somebody else is glad?" she asked.

"I hope so," said Peter, and for the first and last time Jane Raytham felt a little twinge that had a remote resemblance to jealousy.

It was gone in a second.

"Come and see me tomorrow﻿—I want to arrange things for﻿—our family."

And when his lips twitched.

"That smile was almost paternal," she said.

===Chinese===
科尔德威尔先生僵硬地站起来伸了个懒腰。

"我回家睡觉去了。那些小黄脸子再来找你麻烦的可能性不大，我想我可以放心地把你和你的卢克丽霞（Lucretia）留在这里了。我不知道在法庭上这案子会怎么收场，谁会被牵扯进来、谁不会，但这些都是你必须熬过去、挺过来的小麻烦。"

简知道他是在对她说，便笑了笑。

"我什么都能熬过去，"她说，"什么都能挺过来，只要有人偶尔让那颗小金脑袋枕在我的枕头上睡一觉。"

她走到彼得面前，伸出了手。

"我不知道该不该为——离婚的事——高兴，"她说，"我想我是高兴的。我希望你也高兴，彼得。"

她飞快地瞥了一眼正在书桌前整理文件的莱斯利，把声音压得更低。

"你觉得别人高兴吗？"她问。

"我希望如此，"彼得说。简·雷瑟姆此生第一次、也是最后一次感到了一丝隐隐约约像嫉妒的东西。

转瞬即逝。

"明天来看我——我想安排一下——我们一家人的事。"

他的嘴唇微微抽动了一下。

"那个笑容简直像个慈父，"她说。

===Original===
They were all gone at last except Peter and Leslie, and Lucretia, washing up noisily in the scullery, her door half open to ensure the proprieties.

"Well?" asked Leslie.

"Very well﻿—bewilderingly well."

"I told you about Mrs. Dawlish and what she intends doing?"

He nodded.

"You can, of course, charge her with being privy to the forgery, but I think it was Anita's work. It will be so much better if you allow her to pass the property to you by deed of gift. That makes you a very rich man, Peter. What are you going to do with it? Buy a house in Park Lane?"

"Would you like a house in Park Lane," he asked.

"I'd like almost any kind of house, Peter," she said quietly.

Lucretia, looking through the half-opened door, saw the brown head of her mistress pillowed on Peter's shabby jacket, saw him bend his head and kiss her.

Lucretia sneered.

"My Gawd!" she said, addressing nobody in particular. "These women!"

===Chinese===
最后只剩下彼得和莱斯利，还有卢克丽霞在洗碗间里叮叮当当地洗碗，门半掩着以避嫌。

"怎么样？"莱斯利问。

"非常好——好得让人难以置信。"

"我跟你说过达利什太太打算怎么做了吧？"

他点了点头。

"你当然可以告她参与伪造，但我觉得那是安妮塔干的。让她把财产以赠与方式转让给你会好得多。那样你就是个大富翁了，彼得。你打算怎么花？在公园巷（Park Lane）买栋房子？"

"你想要一栋公园巷的房子吗？"他问。

"我差不多什么样的房子都想要，彼得，"她轻声说。

卢克丽霞透过半掩的门看见女主人棕色的脑袋枕在彼得破旧的外套上，看见他低下头吻了她。

卢克丽霞嗤之以鼻。

"我的老天！"她自言自语道，"这些女人哪！"
